Cells are first-fit CLIQUES over members ordered by first sighting, not connected components: a 60 % price cut is compatible on its own, so components fuse two chains and then lose the whole family — the ceskereality 2+kk family loses all 107 of its K-B pairs as a component and only its 26 cross-chain ones as cells. Mode `cell` is the shipped shape of the rule, `family` (refuse every edge of an impure family) is measured and rejected at 4x the cost for the same coverage (M94). `Settings.validate` now lets the honest clock (E62) pay its price with E85 **or** E65, never with neither, and still never without E84. **OFF by default** (`family_guard_mode="off"`), and the incremental lane RAISES on it: a family is a property of the pair set a pass sees one slice of, and it only ever grows, so the rail owed is E64's — re-evaluation triggered by a family gaining a member rather than by a block census growing, demoting to the BAND and never unmerging, with K-B the one certificate E64's blanket certificate exemption must not cover. The batch side is built (`autodedup/family.py`); the incremental side is owed. **W11's verification then withdrew the structural case above** (M99): re-read blind against the adverts, the two ceskereality families that carry 64 of the guard's 77 refusals are each ONE unit — the 2+kk family's "two concurrent price chains" are one co-operative flat whose members all print `podíl 3 399 000` **and** `Anuita: 5 126 000`, and 3 399 000 + 5 126 000 = 8 525 000, while the 1+kk family's "28,6 vs 27,2" is a two-character edit in an otherwise byte-identical body that is corrected once at advert 3 of 21 and never reverts. The re-read finds **no** family that is unambiguously several units posted serially, and the sealed split then measured the guard changing NOTHING (M100). It stays DATA and OFF, its candidate row kept as a record at `settings/refuted/w11_candidate.json` (E87), and the real-time lane keeps g6's rules until it carries a family index.
- **E86** **A family guard's verdict must be an invariant of the FAMILY, not of the order its members arrived in.** E85 partitions a K-B family by first-fit over members ordered by first sighting, and first-fit is not confluent: re-partitioning the same families under 20 shuffled orders moves the partition on **4 of the 37 families of size >= 3**, one of them into three distinct partitions (M101). Which certificate survives is then a property of arrival order — the one thing a replay, a backfill or an incremental pass changes — so E70's replay equivalence cannot hold for it and E64's rail cannot repair it, because the pair another order would have refused is not the pair the rail re-opens. Three consequences, all shipped: `cell` and `family` are **batch-only** and their order is pinned to first sighting so a cohort pass and its replay agree (`tests/autodedup/test_family_guard.py` pins the pinning **and** the defect, by refusing a different edge when the same three adverts arrive in another order); a fourth mode **`pair`** refuses only an edge whose own two adverts are incompatible, which no third advert and no order can move, and it is the only mode an incremental rail may be designed on (M102) — the lane still raises on all three until that rail exists; and the run's own ordering is a rail too, since holding K-B rows back until the family can be read must change the verdict and nothing else: `harness.run_engine` merges the held rows back into key order and sorts the decision list before clustering, so `pairs.jsonl.gz` and the cluster edge order are byte-for-byte what a run without the guard writes (`test_engine_e2e.py::test_the_family_guard_does_not_reorder_the_run_artifacts`).
- **E87** **A gate may only accept a price a SEALED read has measured.** W11's build widened `Settings.validate` so the honest clock (E62) could pay its price with E85 *or* E65 — an argument made before the seal was opened. The seal then measured E85 **inert**: against the same arm without it, 0 sealed labelled duplicates gained, 0 lost, all 27 sealed demotions unlabelled, and the one sealed reliable false merge kept by design inside a cell (M100). A price that buys nothing is not a price, so the gate reverts: under the honest clock the E65 image floor is again the only payment `validate` accepts, and the honest clock's true price is an open question D30 (vii) owes, not a row this gate may assume. The refuted arm keeps its record at **`settings/refuted/w11_candidate.json`** — outside `settings/`, where every file must be constructible — and is built the way it was measured, by assignment, exactly as W10's no-floor arm was.
- **E88** **The honest clock ships everywhere EXCEPT where the hazard lives — and the two definitions that tried to say where were both measured too wide to ship.** W11 left one open path to the honest clock (D30 vii): a price `validate` could accept that is not the blanket E65 floor. E88 is that attempt, pre-registered and then refused by its own rule. **The mechanism.** A K-B certificate — the only merge path resting on the honest clock's disjoint windows — inside a new-development serial-poster FAMILY is **HELD**: the pair is re-decided with K-B withdrawn and falls to K-C, the model or E63, exactly as E85's refusal does — the hold removes evidence and never manufactures a contradiction, so it is never itself a veto or a reject, though the model that then reads the pair may of course score it under `t_lo` (20 of NARROW's 1,227 held pairs and 20 of WIDE's 1,523 land there, by the model's own verdict and no rule of this one). What is new is the PREDICATE, which reads the family's PROVENANCE (is this a development being sold serially?) rather than whether its members could be one unit. **The family** is the connected component of K-B-certified pairs restricted to edges inside one source, one broker and one address block — a connected component, so unlike E85's first-fit cells the partition is an INVARIANT of the member set and E86's order defect cannot reach it (`tests/autodedup/test_development.py` pins it under 20 shuffles). **The markers**, all readable at decision time: project vocabulary (`rezidenc`, `projekt`, `developer`, `novostavb`, `etap`, `cenik`, `kolaudac`, `dokonceni`) in at least half the members; co-operative financing vocabulary (`podil`, `anuit`, `druzstevn`) in at least half of a family of three or more; family size; block density (other same-category listings at the family's address); and members printing different unit-level facts, which is REPORTED and in neither definition, because M99 read that signal as a typo corrected once. **Two definitions, both written down and committed before the first arm ran**: NARROW = vocabulary AND size ≥ 3, WIDE = vocabulary OR size ≥ 4 OR block density ≥ 20 (E63's own C3 block bar, not a new number). **Measured (M104–M107): both do exactly what they were built to do and both cost more than the honest clock buys.** Each removes both contested gold negatives from the merge zone; each also withholds the honest chains beside them, because a broker's own re-post chain prints the same project vocabulary, reaches the same size and sits at the same dense address as a developer's serial catalogue. On dev + validation the honest clock gains **165** reliable labelled duplicates over g6; NARROW gives back **212** and WIDE **297**, so the pre-stated rule (the widest hold whose cost is under one third of the gain) names **no candidate**. The rule ships as DATA and OFF (`development_hold_mode="off"`), `Settings.validate` accepts it as an alternative price to the E65 floor (and refuses it without E84), and the real-time lane RAISES on it — with the one thing that is genuinely better than E85's position stated: the hold is **monotone** (a family only gains members, vocabulary only arrives, a census only fills up), so a family can only ever TURN development-like and the rail owed is nameable, E64's with a family trigger. **And one convention this wave had to fix to run at all**: its cohort, arms and construction reproduce W11's split map to the listing, so a fresh holdout could only be a fresh SEED — and under "a seal is named by its map" that seal could be committed only by overwriting a SPENT one. `seals.seal_id` now names a seed-bearing seal by map AND seed; the four seals cut before this rule keep their map-only names.
- **E89** **A price the seal measures NEGATIVE is not a price — the gate takes back E88's widening exactly as E87 took back E85's.** W12's build widened `Settings.validate` so the honest clock (E62) could pay with the E65 image floor *or* the E88 new-development hold, and said in the same breath that E87 still binds: a gate may only accept a price a SEALED read has measured. The W12 seal has now been opened once and measured it. On the test side the NARROW hold merges **145 of 238** reliable labelled duplicates against g6's **177** — McNemar gained 7, lost 39, exact two-sided p < 1e-5 — and removes **0** false merges, because there are none to remove: g6, the candidate and the honest arm all carry 0 reliable false merges at pair, block, family and cluster grain (M110). E85 was inert; the hold is NEGATIVE — it pays 32 true merges for no measured safety. So the gate reverts, the E65 image floor is again the only payment `validate` accepts, and the three rows that exercised the hold keep their records at **`settings/refuted/{w12_candidate,w12_narrow,w12_wide}.json`** — outside `settings/`, where every file must be constructible — built the way they were measured, by assignment, exactly as W10's no-floor arm and W11's guard arm were. **And the boundary the same read exposed (M111): a hold on K-B cannot be the honest clock's price at all**, because K-B is not the only channel the clock opens — 21 of the honest arm's 469 new merges over g6 arrive through the model (13 of them at exactly the `model|cross` threshold 0.9788), E63 (7) and K-R (1), since the clock moves the FEATURES and therefore the SCORE, and the hold keeps every one of them, four inside a real new development. A successor to E88 must act on the score, not only on the certificate; must carry a minimum family size on every limb (M109); and must reach the development that posts a unit only TWICE (M114), which no size bar ever will. The rule itself stays as DATA and OFF, and lifting it is a settings change — `development_hold_mode`, with `development.holds` live, tested and unreachable from the real-time lane.

<!-- W9g's three rules were written as E83/E83a/E84 and moved to E90/E90a/E91 (and M84-M88 to
     M150-M154, D28 to D33) when W10 landed on main first and took those numbers. Numbers are
     never re-used and never renumbered once merged; these had not merged yet. -->
- **E90** **A cohort statistic the lane READS but nothing WRITES is not frozen — it is absent, and absent is a value.** W9f's first live pass issued **1,024 K-B, 75 K-R and ZERO K-C** where the batch generation `g6` over the same cohort issued **1,771 K-C**, and 5,888 of the 7,878 pairs both generations hold moved in score. The replay-equivalence proof (44,724 of 44,724) could not see it, because replay feeds the incremental path from the export ARTIFACT while the live lane reads through `SqlFacts` — the same blind spot E77 names, at a different input. The input was the corpus-wide pHash population: E70 freezes it into `autodedup.phash_pop` and `SqlFacts` joins every image against that table, reading an absent hash as a population of **0** — but **no code in this repository ever writes that table**, and it holds **0 rows**. The export, by contrast, computes the population with a `count(DISTINCT listing_id)` over `public.images`, which cannot return less than 1 for a hash that exists. So in production every image carried `pop = 0`, `Image.pop_is_measured()` was false for every gallery, `build_fingerprint` published `catalog_ratio = None` for every listing, `catalog_ratio_max` was ABSENT on every pair — and K-C, which requires that ratio to be PRESENT, became structurally unreachable while K-B, which does not read it, went on firing. Three consequences follow from one missing table: a certificate that cannot be earned, E9's catalogue subtraction disarmed (nothing can reach `catalog_df = 8`, so stock photographs stay in the pHash, CLIP and tag galleries and crowd the `phash_sample` cap), and `ContextIndex.shared_image_population` reading 0 for every pair, which is the hazard limb E64's rail is built on. The rule is general: **a frozen statistic needs a writer, a row count and a refusal.** The seed that cuts a generation's calibration is the only place that can populate one, a lane whose frozen input is empty must refuse rather than read zeros as measurements, and `pop` must carry the export's distinction — `None` is UNKNOWN, `0` is a lie no real photograph can tell. `--mode rt_parity` is the instrument that finds this class: it builds a sample of listings twice, once from an export artifact and once through `SqlFacts`, diffs them field by field, and re-scores the same pairs from both fact sets under one settings/model/calibration — a defect that lives between the two readings has nowhere to hide from it.
- **E90a** **Which settings and which model a pass ran under is part of its result, and a dispatch default is not a choice.** The same live pass ran with empty `args`, so `load_settings(None)` gave `Settings()` and `load_model(None)` gave the uncalibrated `hand_initialised()` prior — `t_lo 0.30` against w8's `0.1823`, `t_hi 0.97` against `1.0`, no `t_hi_by_stratum`, no bridge, no context rule — while `g6` was scored under `w8` and `w6_gold`. The seed recorded it faithfully (`rt_calibration.model_version = 'hand_v1'`) and nothing read it back: `autodedup.pairs.model_version` is **NULL on all 15,923 rt rows**, so the store could not say which scorer had taken its decisions. A generation's settings and model belong to the generation, beside its calibration, and a pass must load them from there and stamp them on every row it writes — the frozen calibration pins the cohort statistics and pins neither.
- **E91** **A live lane's facts are built by the EXPORT's builders from the export's statements, and a PARITY SAMPLE is a gate on every pass — not an instrument you run when something looks wrong.** E77 made the lane read `COHORT_LISTINGS_SQL` and its five siblings through `build_listing_record` / `build_image_record`; W9f then shipped anyway, because the one input those builders do not read from `public` — the corpus-wide pHash population, frozen by E70 into `autodedup.phash_pop` — had no writer, held 0 rows, and was read as a population of **0** rather than as an unknown. One live pass, 36,940 pairs, ZERO K-C (E90). Three parts, and all three are the rule: **(a) the frozen statistic is carried by the SEED.** The number the live lane needs is the one the export already measured with `count(DISTINCT listing_id)` over `public.images`, so `rt_seed` copies it out of the artifact into `autodedup.phash_pop` — no scan of `public.images` at seed time and none at pass time, and the lane then joins against exactly the statistic the batch engine scored with. A seed whose artifact carries no measured population (`phash_pop_ok=false`) is REFUSED. **(b) A hash the frozen table does not carry is UNKNOWN (`pop = None`), never 0** — the export writes the same null when its own probe does not run, and `pop = 0` is a claim no real photograph can support (it appears on at least its own listing). There is no cheaper way to measure a new one: `public.images` carries no index on `phash` (checked live — `images_phash_idx` is on `sreality_id`), D8 forbids adding one, and no in-schema mirror of (hash → listing) exists, so the only measurement is the export's own sequential scan. **The staleness this implies is stated, not hidden**: the population is as of the export, it can only have grown, and a photograph that arrived after it is unmeasured — which costs that listing its `catalog_ratio` (conservative: K-C cannot fire for it) rather than asserting that its photo is unique. `population.images_unmeasured` on every pass summary is what says when the generation is due a fresh export and re-seed. **(c) Parity is a GATE.** The seed cuts a baseline of FOUR digests per sampled listing — `stored` (columns, location, attrs, price history, gallery identity and order), `sighting` (`last_seen_at`, `is_active`, `inactive_at`), `producer` (pHash, CLIP vector, CLIP tags) and `population` (the frozen count per hash) — into `autodedup.settings` beside the calibration, and every pass re-checks a fresh slice of it BEFORE it opens its transaction. Tolerance **zero**: a `stored` or `population` digest that differs on a listing whose content has not changed since the export stops the pass — loud, non-zero, nothing written, no cursor moved. Producer movement is REPORTED and not refused, because the pHash and CLIP jobs re-run over the corpus on their own cadence and a frozen calibration cannot ask them not to — and so is the SIGHTING clock (`last_seen_at`, `is_active`, `inactive_at`), which rules #4 and #3 move with no snapshot behind either. `--mode rt_parity` stays what it is: the field-by-field report you run when the gate refuses.
- **E92** **A decision records the EVIDENCE it rested on, and a feed brings the listing back when that evidence arrives.** W9g proved the live lane reads the export's facts about a listing the export CARRIED; it said nothing about an arrival, and an arrival is decided before its photographs are evidence. Measured: images land **~1 minute** after `first_seen_at`, `images.phash` is written by an hourly job at **:20** and the CLIP vector and tags by another at **:40** (**p50 2.47 h, p90 4.51 h** to a first tag), while the lane claims at `first_seen_at + 5..15 min`. So **~80% of arrivals were decided with every `phash` NULL**, **78% never carry a second snapshot**, and not one of W9's six feeds is keyed on a hash or a vector ARRIVING — a phash appends no snapshot, flips no flag and moves no location. The listing was decided once, blind, and never again: its stored index keys never gained a pHash posting either, so **no other listing's photo probe could retrieve it** for the life of the generation (pass 2 claimed 0, and the keys still carried none). Stripping photo evidence from the artifact's own pairs moves 15 of 400 scores past 0.01, 8 zones (4 merge→band, 4 reject→band) and **every K-C**. The rule has two halves and this is the first: **`rt_fp` records what the decision rested on** — `ev_images`, `ev_phash`, `ev_clip`, `ev_tags` and `first_decided_at` (migration 540) — and a **seventh feed** round-robins this generation's rows whose evidence is incomplete or whose first decision is inside `rt_evidence_horizon_hours` (data, **48 h**), probes `public.images` by listing id and re-claims every listing whose photographs no longer match. The probe is the only read of `public.images` outside a fact fetch and it is bounded like every other cost on this lane: `images_listing_id_idx`, **12.4 buffers a listing** (2,484 buffers / 3,085 images / 200 listings, 17 ms warm), and it reads NO other table because `images.clip_tagged_at` is the CLIP job's own stamp — measured identical to `image_clip_embeddings` and `image_clip_tags` on **73,208 of 73,208** trial-scope images, at a fourteenth of the buffers the two joins cost (M115, M116). A re-claim rewrites the postings (so the pHash band exists) and re-scores the pairs; `first_decided_at` is coalesced, never restamped, so a re-decision cannot restart the horizon.
- **E93** **Do not act on evidence you do not have yet: a MERGE that rests on photographs WAITS for them; a reject does not.** The half E92 owes, and the reason the sweep is not enough on its own: a listing claimed blind can be merged blind — 19 of the trial cohort's merges are (M118) — and E92 would then re-open a group rather than never form it. So while either side's gallery **exists and carries no hash at all** and that side is inside the horizon, a merge that does not rest on a certificate needing no photograph — **K-B** (one portal, one broker, one body, live windows that never touched) or **K-R** (a shared agency order code), both text-and-clock facts — is **HELD in the band** with reason `evidence_pending`, carrying what it was holding (`held_zone`, `held_reason`, `held_certificate`) and certifying nothing. Rejects are never held: a pair refused on text, price or geometry is refused on evidence the lane HAS, and holding it would be the anti-conservative direction. The hold is released when the evidence lands **or** when the horizon passes — and the second case moves no fact at all, so the sweep's second arm asks for the held pairs BY REASON (`decision = 'evidence_pending'`) rather than waiting for a digest that will never move. **What it costs, honestly**: a text-or-certificate decision still completes within the pass that claims the listing; a photo-dependent merge waits for the hourly producers, so its arrival-to-merge time is that distribution — p50 2.47 h, p90 4.51 h, bounded at 48 h — rather than the engine's sub-second. The production change that would remove the wait is **hashing on download in the image worker** (`scraper/`), which is outside this program's territory and the operator's decision (D26); nothing in this lane may reach for it. And the hold is an ARGUMENT, not a setting: the batch engine has no clock, so a pass given no hold is W9's pass exactly and E70's replay equivalence is untouched — proved by re-running it (44,724 of 44,724, 849 clusters) and by a replay variant that withholds every photograph at first claim, delivers them, expires the horizon, and reaches **the batch engine's state at both grains, 0 differing** (M118).
- **E94** **A gate that checked nothing must REFUSE, and one image's moved producer excuses ONE image.** W9g's gate counted breaches and nothing else, which is two vacuities and both were demonstrated: give every baseline listing a newer snapshot and every stored fact can then be broken with `checked` **0**, `breaches` **0**, `ok` true; delete every baseline listing and `absent` 120 passes just as quietly. Drift is monotone in export age (4 of 200 at three days), so the gate **self-weakened as the export aged** — precisely backwards. Three floors, all data and all refusing rather than passing: a minimum COMPARED count (`rt_parity_min_checked`, **15**) and a minimum SHARE of the slice (`rt_parity_min_checked_share`, **0.6**), because an absolute floor alone is defeated from both ends — a baseline smaller than 15 could never meet it and a 120-listing seed sample could meet it with 105 listings drifted or absent — with an EMPTY baseline requiring at least one comparison; and a ceiling on the share of phash-bearing images whose hash the frozen population cannot measure (`rt_parity_max_unknown_pop_share`, **0.15**, against a measured 4.0% at three days). And the population is compared **per IMAGE**: W9g skipped a listing's whole population check as soon as any image of it had been re-hashed, so one re-hashed frame excused its fourteen neighbours and a population decaying image by image ran green — the W9f defect in slow motion. The baseline therefore carries `[image_id, phash, pop]` per image (~54 KB for 120 listings, still one settings row), a frame whose own hash moved is excused and counted, and every other frame of that listing is still checked.
- **E95** **A frozen statistic has an expiry date, and the pass says how old it is every time.** E91 froze the pHash population and stated its staleness; nothing anywhere refused on it. The direction of the error is what makes that a defect rather than a shortcoming: a frozen population can only **UNDERCOUNT**, and an undercount is the ANTI-conservative direction — a frame that has spread since the export is still read as rare, E9 does not subtract it, and the pair gets image evidence the batch engine would deny it. So every pass reports the **EXPORT's** age (not the seed's: what is frozen is the cohort's statistics, and they go on ageing however recently the generation was cut from them), the calibration row's own age, and the population's **coverage** — the share of the photographs this pass actually scored that the frozen table could measure — and the lane REFUSES beyond `rt_calibration_max_age_days` (data, **14**). A baseline that cannot say when its cohort was exported is refused too, because a rail that cannot be evaluated is not a rail. **The refresh is one action, and what it preserves is stated:** a fresh export plus `rt_seed ... reseed=true`, which re-cuts the calibration, re-materialises `autodedup.phash_pop`, re-cuts the parity baseline and re-backfills the fingerprints. Operator verdicts and must-not-links are **generation-free** and survive untouched; `rt` pairs and clusters are rebuilt by the passes that follow, and the old rows are left where they are rather than rewritten, because they were decided under a scorer and on facts the new generation does not share.
- **E96** **An unmeasured population BANDS; it never merges.** The corollary E91(b) implies and W9h states as a rule, because it is what makes the staleness above tolerable rather than dangerous. A hash that appeared after the export cannot be counted without the export's own sequential scan — `images_phash_idx` is on `(sreality_id) where phash is not null`, there is no index from a hash to its carriers, D8 forbids adding one and schema `autodedup` mirrors nothing — so it reads **UNKNOWN**, `pop = None`. `pop_is_measured()` is then false, `catalog_ratio` is None, `catalog_ratio_max` is **ABSENT** on the pair, and `certificate_c` requires it PRESENT: the pair falls to the model score and **bands rather than merging on photo evidence nobody measured**. Measured at three days of export age: **115 of 2,847 images (4.0%)** read an unknown population and **54 of 400 pairs (13.5%)** lose `catalog_ratio_max` — the number E94's ceiling and E95's age rail are both proxies for, and the number a fresh export resets.
- **E110** **The honest clock's price is E84, and it is a price a SEALED read has measured — because the one number that refused the arm was a label the OPERATOR has now overturned.** E87 and E89 each took back a widening of `Settings.validate` on the same principle: a gate may only accept a price a sealed read has measured. W13 does not widen the gate, it RETIRES the demand. The arm in question is exactly W11's: the honest clock (E62), the E84 one-minute pair gap, **no E65 image floor, no E85 family guard, no E88 hold**, `w6_gold` unrefitted. W11's seal read it once and measured **347 of 406 sealed reliable labelled duplicates merged against g6's 300 — McNemar gained 47, lost 0, p ~ 7.1e-15 — at band 4,524 against 4,907** (M98). It met every clause of the promotion bar but one, and that one was a count of **1**: the sealed gold negative `522698 x 13221982`, whose dev-side twin is `555448 x 18626270`, both inside the two ceskereality Rezidence K Botiči serial-poster families F518656 and F521778 (D30 i). **On 2026-09-20 19:30 UTC the operator ruled BOTH pairs `same` in the validation UI** (`autodedup.verdicts`, pair grain, explicit, source `op:0871b32a9d227f60`; the W13 labels export carries them, M125). Operator labels outrank gold everywhere in this program, so the arm's reliable false-merge count is 0 at pair, block, family and cluster grain on every read taken since — and the sealed read that already exists becomes its measured price. E65's floor stays as live, tested, enforced settings rows (`certificate_b_min_images`, `certificate_b_min_matched_images`), it is simply no longer **required**: it withheld 89 % of the one-unit re-post chains' merges (M96) to guard against a hazard the operator has now said is not one. **What this acceptance rests on, and what revokes it, stated in the rule so nobody has to re-derive it: it rests on ONE operator ruling about ONE development** — two ceskereality families of one broker at K Botiči — **and it is revoked by an operator NEGATIVE inside a K-B family**, a pair the operator rules is two units that K-B certifies under the honest clock. Revoking it is a settings change, not a code change: a number back in `certificate_b_min_images` restores the W9 arm exactly. Two consequences of retiring the gate are recorded rather than hidden: the refuted rows under `settings/refuted/` **build again** (E87's convention keeps only its first half — `settings/` is what a scoring run may be pointed at, `refuted/` records a verdict), and the three refusals E83/E85/E88 now rest on their `off` defaults and their measurements alone, which is where E28's other refused rules have always rested.
- **E111** **A rule that names the event which revokes it must COUNT that event, not remember it.** E110 accepts the honest live-window clock on one operator ruling about one development and names what takes the acceptance back — an operator NEGATIVE inside a K-B family, a pair the operator rules is two units whose two adverts sit in one component of the pairs K-B certified under that clock. Left as prose, that condition expires silently: labels arrive every time the operator works the validation UI, and nobody re-derives a sentence in a rule index. So it is a check — `autodedup/revocation.py`, printed by `harness evaluate` on every run — and its three definitions are the ones already in the program rather than a second spelling of them. The **family** is `family.kb_families`' family, read off the CERTIFICATE and never off a zone or a merge edge (E85: a component read off its own verdict moves under it). The **pair** must have BOTH adverts inside ONE family: an operator negative between two chains, or between a family member and an outsider, is a separation the engine already makes and says nothing about K-B. The **tier** is the operator's alone — a gold `different` inside these families is exactly the reading E110 overturned (D31 vii: gold contradicts the operator on 5 of 10 same-family positives), so counting it would revoke the rule with the evidence the rule rejects; an operator `must_not_link` counts however it is spelled. The check REPORTS and never decides, because revoking E110 is a settings change and not a code change: a number back in `certificate_b_min_images` restores the W9 arm exactly. At promotion it reads **0 negatives over 196 operator-labelled pairs inside 173 K-B families** (M134).
- **E97** **A generation is BUILT, not backfilled — and a re-seed that keeps the old rows is not a re-seed.** Two design defects, both invisible to E70's replay-equivalence proof because replay starts from an EMPTY store and feeds every listing as an arrival. **(a)** A seed with `backfill=true` writes `rt_fp` and `fp_key` for every listing of the cohort and **NOT ONE PAIR**, so the generation holds the whole scope and knows of no duplicate in it: a later arrival can only ever merge with what arrives after the seed, or with one old listing at a time, and its group misses that old listing's own duplicates. The fix is not a "backfill the pairs" step — a pair set written outside `run_pass` is a second engine, and the one thing this program will not have is two — it is to make every in-scope listing an **ARRIVAL**, which is the exact path the proof covers: `backfill=false` plus the bootstrap phase (E98). A backfilled seed remains right for one case and one only, and it is named: a REFRESH of a generation whose pairs were scored under the scorer still in force (E95's recipe), where the fingerprints are being re-cut and the decisions are not. **(b)** `reseed=true` re-cuts the calibration, the frozen population and the parity baseline and **deletes nothing**, so the 15,923 pairs and 703 clusters written by the defective 2026-09-20 pass — wrong scorer, `model_version` NULL on every row, no photo evidence — survived the re-seed of 2026-09-20 and **nothing in the lane will ever re-decide them**: a pass re-scores a pair when a fingerprint digest moves, and those digests still agree. So `fresh=true`, valid only beside `reseed=true`, is a DELETE of this generation's rows inside the seed's own transaction: `pairs`, `cluster_members`, `cluster_conflicts` (keyed on `detail ->> 'generation'`, which is where that table carries it), `clusters`, `rt_fp`, `fp_key`, `rt_block_cell`, `rt_scope_ids`, `rt_scope_scan`, `rt_retire_event`, the seven `scan_cursor` rows and the lease. It never touches `verdicts`, `must_not_link`, the judge's `judgements`, another generation or any `public` table, the summary reports the rows removed **per table**, and a seed that refuses after it — an empty scope, a parity baseline that cannot clear E94's floors — puts every row back.
- **E98** **A build is a PHASE, and a claim is bounded by the clock as well as by the count.** The steady-state lane is built to touch `public` as little as possible: the entrant feed claims a seventh of a slice out of a membership SNAPSHOT and one scope block is re-walked per pass on a 1 h / 6 h cadence (W9e/R3), because a quarter's walk is 231 MB of cold heap reads. Applied to a scope nothing has listed yet, that is a day to even ENUMERATE Vysočany and eight hours of claiming after it. A cadence is what keeps a steady-state lane off `public`; a scope with no steady state has nothing for it to keep. So the seed persists `rt_bootstrap:<generation>` (data, never a dispatch flag — the passes that do the building are the `*/10` schedule's and none of them carries an argument) and while it is on: the entrant claim is the whole `max_listings` rather than a seventh of it, and **every block this generation has never walked is walked in the SAME pass**, while a block already walked once falls straight back to the cadence. The rolling-day scan cap (`rt_enter_max_scans_per_day`) still holds — the cadence is what the lane INTENDS to spend, the cap is what it is ALLOWED to spend, and nothing suspends the second. The phase ends **by itself**, in the transaction of the pass that finds every block walked and the backlog inside what it just claimed. And the second half: `max_listings` bounds work, not TIME, and the runner's bound is 25 minutes. The one live pass that ever did work claimed **77 listings in 876.7 s** — 14.6 minutes of that timeout on a claim a sixth of the shipped one, 78% of it in `cluster_s`. So every pass measures its own rate into `rt_pass_rate_per_s:<generation>` and the next claims at most `rt_pass_budget_s x rate` (900 s x a default of 0.1 listings/s, which is that one live measurement). The default is deliberately the pathological case: a build's first pass claims ~90, measures the real rate, and the second claims the full slice — one extra pass is the price of never guessing the rate upward, and a pass that dies on the timeout has written nothing, advanced no cursor and left the lease held until it expires.
- **E99** **Replay proves the mechanism; only the LIVE STORE against the batch engine proves the generation.** `replay.py` feeds the incremental path from an export ARTIFACT and compares it with the batch engine over that same artifact, in one process, from an empty store — and it has been green four times (44,724 of 44,724 pairs, 849 of 849 clusters, twice more under a shuffled arrival order and with every photograph withheld). Every defect this lane has shipped lived where that proof cannot look: in the rows a real generation HOLDS. E90 (zero K-C against g6's 1,771) needed `rt_parity` to see, and E97(b) needed nothing more than a `count(*)` nobody ran. So `--mode rt_equivalence` is the standing instrument: given the live generation and a batch generation scored on the SAME export under the same settings and model, **inside the live generation's own scope**, it compares the pairs both sides hold (zone, certificate, score within 1e-6), the pairs one side holds **by CAUSE** — `scope` (the batch cohort is wider: two towns and the assembled negative control), `not_in_live_store` (a listing the build has not reached), `arrival_after_export`, `store_floor` (a reject is stored only at or above it) and `retention` (a row below the floor that nothing has re-scored yet) — and the clusters by member set and by key, trimmed to the scope on both sides. A one-sided pair with no cause is **`unexplained`**, and `unexplained` fails the verdict: that is the difference between a check and a description. It writes nothing — not a `public` row, not an `autodedup` row, not an `iterations` row — and `not_in_live_store` fails the verdict too, because a comparison taken mid-build reads an unfinished build as a disagreement.
- **E130** **D43's GATE: a merge across a stated fact is a false merge, so it is demoted.** The operator's ruling of 2026-09-21 redefines the error. Two adverts are INDISTINGUISHABLE when nothing either of them states separates the units, merging those is the WANTED outcome even where a human or a judge called them "different unit, same project", and a false merge is a merge ACROSS a stated fact. The gate is a named layer in `decide.apply_d43_rule`, settings-switchable (`d43_gate`), and it writes the fact into the reason — `certificate:K-C:d43_gate:total_floors` — so the store says WHICH fact demoted each g7 merge without re-running the pass. It demotes 273 of g7's 5,158 merges before the W14 fact work and 195 after it.
- **E131** **D43's PROMOTION, and the rail that stands where the predicate is weakest.** A band pair carrying no stated fact is promoted to merge. The predicate is an ABSENCE test, so it is strongest where both adverts are rich and weakest where they state almost nothing — the one live hand-read error in the whole corpus (Regus physical office against virtual office: same address, same portal, one side has no area at all) is exactly that shape. So promotion carries a rail: the two adverts must state and agree on ≥ `d43_promote_min_agreeing` of nine comparable attributes (`indistinguishable.agreeing_attributes`), OR share ≥ 1 tight NON-CATALOGUE photo frame. The photo alternative is not a softening: a shared frame is the evidence counting stated fields merely proxies, and it removes the rail's bias against the portals whose adverts are prose (157 of the 172 thinnest pairs involve bazos). Reason codes `d43_promote:predicate` / `:agree:2` / `:photo`.
- **E132** **D43 is also a CLUSTER invariant, because a group is transitive.** A pairwise gate alone lets A–B and B–C each pass while A and C differ on the floor: without this limb the relaxed arms carry 6 real negatives and 4 bad groups, with it 0. `d43.ClusterRelation` memoises the predicate per unordered pair and `guards.cluster_invariants_ok` reads it LAST, after the cheap limbs. Its two old limbs are retired into settings (`cluster_floor_spread`, `cluster_disposition`): D43 reads both pairwise with a measured tolerance, so keeping them charges one difference twice — `floor_spread` alone refused **842 of g7's 858** rejected unions, because two portals disagree about `přízemí`.
- **E133** **A portal convention is FITTED, never hard-coded, and it moves `floor` and `total_floors` together or not at all.** Some portals count the ground floor and some do not, so one vocabulary difference is otherwise charged twice against the same pair. `floor_convention.measure_camps` fits `source → camp` from ANCHOR pairs the caller supplies — adverts so close on what survives the convention (area ≤ 1 %, price ≤ 0.1 %) that a floor gap can only be vocabulary — and REFUSES any source it cannot place. On 3,306 anchors it places seven portals ({sreality, mmreality, realitymix, remax, bezrealitky} = 1, {ceskereality, idnes} = 0) and drops bazos, which posts both ways; the table ships in the settings row, the fit and its per-pair evidence in the wave artifacts. `total_floors` may differ by the camp offset ONLY when `floor` differs by exactly the same offset — measured over 954 anchors, 478 shift the floor with `total_floors` EQUAL and 106 shift both, so a `total_floors` gap on its own is still a fact.
- **E134** **The asking price is a PATH, not a number.** A dead advert holds the price of the day it died while the live one moved on, so two current numbers are two moments. Two adverts whose price histories ever name the same amount (within `d43_price_path_tol`) are not told apart by the momentary gap: paths agree on **90.4 %** of known duplicates against **51.4 %** of the one-project-two-units hazard class. The other half of the idea — two adverts live at the same time whose paths never meet CONTRADICT each other, even on one portal — was built (`d43_price_colive_contradiction`) and measured at **−25 dev duplicates and +25 losses**, so it ships OFF and is recorded rather than re-proposed.
- **E135** **The obec separates two adverts only when BOTH sides are resolved at street grain or finer.** M199 measured a region of 140 obce, NOT selected by one town: **533 of 22,421** structurally certain duplicates are recorded under two obce (a village against the district town it is advertised under, median 4.8 km; same-name collisions exist), and every one of them has at least one side known only to the obec or the quarter. Conditioned on both sides at street grain the cost is **0 of 2,905**. The trial cohort cannot see this, because it is SELECTED by `obec_kod` — its 0-of-1,317 is a selection effect, not a measurement, and reading it as one is how the town nearly became a rule-floor veto (E128/E129, REFUTED).
- **E136** **The area tolerance is ASYMMETRIC, and the asymmetry is the principle.** Promotion reads `area_band_pct` (3 %): merging on the ABSENCE of a fact is the one place the engine has no positive evidence to fall back on. The gate and the cluster invariant read `d43_gate_area_tol` (8 %, the engine's own merge-grade guard): they are about to overrule evidence the engine already CERTIFIED, and a parse defect in the 3–8 % band must not split a certified merge. Worth +4 dev and +5 sealed duplicates, and it takes the area-caused losses from 8 to 1.
- **E137** **A group is an invariant of the member set, not of arrival order (E72/E86 at cluster grain).** When a component of the merge graph cannot be one group, E33 refuses a union and moves on, so which members fall out depends on which edge was seen first. `repartition.partition` instead cuts the component into maximal consistent sub-groups: the edges are read in `edge_rank`'s total order — a property of the SET — and a bounded local search then moves single members only on a STRICT improvement in the merge evidence realised inside groups, so one input gives one partition whatever order the caller held the edges in (tested under shuffles). It buys **+34 dev duplicates** at 0 cost on every reference. It is NOT what recovers the losses the W14 anatomy attributed to greedy order — see D45.
- **E138** **The GATE has its own reading, and the CLUSTER invariant does not share all of it.** E136 generalises: demoting means overruling a certified merge, so the gate does not demote on a difference that is INFERRED rather than stated (`interior` is a CLIP similarity, `floorplan` a model flag — neither is anything an advert says), on two street names whose pins are ≤ `d43_street_min_distance_m` apart (a corner building; the town probe's own `street_kills_a_merge` example is 0.1 m), or on one storey across a portal boundary the camps cannot place (E133's dropped source). The cluster invariant takes the street and storey limbs and REFUSES the image one: it is the only thing standing between a development and one big group, and dropping its best hazard-cell discriminator (`tag_room_clip_min2`, AUC 0.820) recovers 9 labelled duplicates while building **4 bad groups**. The dial exists (`d43_cluster_image_facts`) so the refusal stays measurable rather than remembered.
- **E139** **A pass that loaded no operator negatives must not look like one that loaded them.** g7 reported `n_must_not_link = 45` and every one of those was the E61 designator veto set — the operator's 246 permanent negatives never reached the batch build, and one total could not say so. The score lane now reads `autodedup.must_not_link` BY SOURCE, the settings row decides whether the operator's rows bind (`operator_must_not_link`, default true), `harness.load_must_not_link` reads the labels lane's own `must_not_link.jsonl` artifact, and every run summary carries `must_not_link: {operator, unit_designator_veto, total, loaded}`. Honouring the rows costs 3 listings and 1 flip and 0 labelled duplicates, so this is a correctness question and never a volume one.
- **E140** **The land-register parcel is the object's identity, and DISJOINT printed sets are two objects.** A Czech pozemek or dům advert prints the parcel the object stands on — `parcelní číslo 934/11`, `parc. č. 1633`, `p.č. 2361 a p.č. 2362`, `st. p. č. 44` — and that number is what the register knows the object by, so two adverts printing parcels with nothing in common are two objects however alike they look. The conflict is DISJOINTNESS and never inequality: an advert also names the access road, the neighbour's plot and the parcel the garage stands on, and on the region cohort a strict-equality reading costs 12 certain duplicates (8 of them one Krkonoše roubenka whose parcel list two portals parse differently) against 1 for disjointness — and that 1 is two of three Paceřice building plots that share one photo set, so the reference is wrong there, not the rule. The reader unescapes HTML entities (one sreality broker publishes an escaped body), requires a list separator to carry a comma, a `+` or the word `a`/`i`, and refuses a number followed by `m²`, without which `parc. č. 123, 2 393 m²` reads the AREA as a second parcel and makes two different plots share one. Buys 8 of the 18 groups g8 fused, and 235 of 7,354 structural negatives.
- **E141** **Where the developer prints no unit number, the building's own numbering of its ACCESSORIES does.** E61 reads `byt č. 3` and `jednotka č. 12`; a broker who prints neither still prints the parking space, the cellar and the garage that come with THIS flat — `parkovací stání pro auto č. 47` against `stání č. 32 a sklepní kóje č. 25`. Read per kind and compared as sets, disjoint sets distinguish and a subset does not (one side names only the space included in the rent, the other also the one available for extra). `stání` is a whole word so `stanice metra` can never become a parking space, and `pokoj č. 2` is deliberately not read — that is a room inside one flat. Costs 6 of 22,421 region certain duplicates, and all six are the ONE Turnov `V Ráji` pair the blind hand-read called different.
- **E142** **The offered EXTENT: how much of the same kind of thing is on offer.** Three spellings, one fact. A serviced-office operator sells one room as several products (`kancelář pro 1 osobu` at 10,890 Kč against `pro 2 pracovní místa` at 15,590, with the BUILDING's generic 50 m² and the building's address on both); a room let offers one room or two (`Pronajmu pokoj` at 8,500 against `Pronajmu 2 spojené pokoje` at 12,000); a land advert offers a share of one parcel or of four. The first two need a conjunction, because a bare capacity regex fires on `zálohy na služby pro 1 osobu` and on `byt se hodí pro 1 osobu` and 5 of its 7 hits in g8 were exactly those: the office noun must carry the phrase, the prices must differ beyond `d43_offered_extent_price_tol`, and the two adverts must have been on sale at the same time. The parcel limb needs neither — a printed inventory one advert offers strictly more of is a different offer outright, and the proper-subset test excludes the parse-noise shape (two portals reading one body's parcel list differently is an INTERSECTION, not a containment). Costs 0 labelled duplicates on either cohort.
- **E143** **The two-unit signature: one advert has ONE area and ONE price at any moment.** Two adverts whose area AND price BOTH differ beyond rounding are two units, and this is the hazard the operator ranked above all others: the engine's tolerances (area 3 %, cross-portal price 5 %, same-portal 60 %) are far wider than the real separation between neighbouring units of one project, which is 0.5–2 %. A re-post carries a new price at the SAME area; a second portal carries the same price at a ROUNDED area; the developer's next flat carries both. Two rails keep it honest — an agreeing price PATH excuses a moment (E134), and a floor area BOTH bodies print excuses a stored-column basis difference (`užitná 51 m² (podlahová 55 m²)` against `podlahová 55 m² (užitná 51 m²)`, Harfa Living). Tolerances 0.5 % / 0.5 % / 0.5 %, and `validate` refuses an area tolerance at or above `area_band_pct` because above it the signature is the `area` fact spelled twice. Costs 58 of 22,421 region certain duplicates, **0** of the 1,415 carrying a shared agency ORDER code, **0** operator-tier labelled duplicates and **0** sealed ones; buys 16 of the 18 fused groups and 12 more that phase 2 never listed, of which a hand-read calls 10 genuinely two units (`repair/handread_two_unit_sound.json`).
- **E144** **A co-live test reads a DURATION, and a same-portal price gap is still not a fact.** E134's co-live limb compared two windows as a boolean, so two adverts whose windows merely touch across a re-post boundary counted as simultaneous — 38 of the 63 raw contradictions. `overlap_days` and `d43_price_colive_min_overlap_days` fix that, and `d43_price_colive_same_source_only` gives the limb the side the evidence has (every contradiction at ≥ 7 days overlap is same-portal already). The limb still ships OFF, and W15 refuses it on a MECHANISM rather than on its cost: one Czech advert legitimately carries two prices for one unit at the same time. `13894353 × 13981528` is one Harrachov flat (`rezidence ZORA, Dům B, byt č. 301`, word-identical body, one broker) at its freehold price of 8,350,000 and at its co-operative SHARE price of 1,670,000 — 80 % apart, co-live throughout; `503105 × 18778135` is one 357 m² plot at an exekutorská-dražba figure of 5,400 and at an asking price of 17,000. The "55 % is the widest gap any known duplicate shows on one portal" that the group attack measured is a TRIAL-cohort statistic: on the region cohort same-portal certain duplicates reach 80 %, 15 of them above 40 %. So no same-portal price bar separates a re-post from a second unit, and `PRICE_SAME_SOURCE_TOL` is not tightened either.
- **E145** **The ground-floor convention belongs to the FEED, not to the portal.** E133 fits a camp per SOURCE and reads a one-storey gap inside one source as a fact, on the premise that one portal is one convention. The data refuses the premise: among same-portal KNOWN duplicates a one-storey gap runs at 7.5 % on sreality, 6.8 % on ceskereality, 15 % on realitymix, 23.5 % on bazos and 0.8–1.7 % on idnes. The number is written by the broker whose feed the portal republishes, so `floor_same_source_feed: broker` makes the same-portal clause ask for a shared `broker_key`, and an advert with no broker key (bazos, bezrealitky) is never known to share a feed with anyone. This is the single repair that pays: on the trial cohort it takes the operator-tier losses from **32 to 22** — under the bar of 30 — and recovers 33 dev and 3 sealed labelled duplicates on its own, because it stops the invariant cutting the V Aleji 131 m² 4+1 in two over one storey that two ceskereality feeds spell differently.

**The apply path, BUILT — W30 (E300–E306). Dark by default; the operator turns it on.**
- **E300** **The engine's groups may become production merges — only through THE chokepoint, only when switched on.** `autodedup/apply.py` (lane modes `apply` / `unapply`, `.github/workflows/autodedup.yml`) reads ONE generation's stored groups and calls `toolkit.property_identity.merge_properties` with `source='autodedup'` — a third value of `property_merge_events.source` (migration **558**), so an engine merge can never read as the removed legacy engine's `'auto'` (CLAUDE.md rule 15). Nothing is decided here that the engine did not decide: the groups are read as stored, and every check below only REMOVES a group from the plan. `autodedup.merges` (528's reserved ledger) stays reserved and empty; the ledger is `autodedup.applied_merges` (558), one row per (group, retired property), dry runs and refusals included.
- **E301** **One merge group per engine group, one transaction per group.** Every retired property of a group merges under ONE `merge_group_id`, so `unmerge_group` undoes the whole group, and the group's merges run inside one outer transaction — a chokepoint refusal on the third property rolls back the first two. **The survivor is the property with the most listings**; a tie goes to the oldest `properties.first_seen_at`, then the lowest id (deterministic; supersedes §11's oldest-listing sketch). `reason = 'autodedup <generation> <cluster_key>'`, `confidence` = the group's `min_edge_score`, `markers` = generation, cluster_key, model and feature version, members, run id and the scope the run ran under.
- **E302** **The generation stamp is written in the merge's own transaction.** `property_merge_events.generation = 'autodedup:<generation>'` is set by a WRITE-ONLY update scoped to the group's own `merge_group_id`, inside the same transaction as the merges. D7 holds: the apply path reads nothing of `property_merge_events`; its only history is its own ledger.
- **E303** **A group is refused, and the reason recorded, when:** — and every check reads **the whole set the merge would put on the survivor**, the members plus every other child of every involved property, because a merge moves them all — a member has no property yet (`unattached_member`, rule 19's deferred grouping); a property is missing or not `active` (`property_not_active`); that set exceeds the scope's `max_cluster_size` (`oversize` — the cap bounds the property the merge builds, not the group); an operator negative would be contradicted — a negative pair verdict (`operator_pair_verdict`) or `must_not_link` row (`must_not_link`) with BOTH its sides in the set, or a negative group verdict whose WHOLE `member_ids` set is in it (`operator_group_verdict`: E58's "these are not one property" is contradicted by any merge that unites the set — the ruled membership or any superset of it, under whichever key — so rulings are fetched by the listings they name, never by the key they were taken under; a row with no `member_ids` names no set and refuses its key in every generation, fail-closed); deal types mix or `category_main_compatible` refuses a pair anywhere in the set (`category_type_mix` / `category_main_incompatible`, the chokepoint's own helper, re-checked there); a listing in the set is outside the run's scope (`carries_out_of_scope_listings`); a property carries a listing the engine put in ANOTHER group of the generation (`property_spans_groups` — E37 at property grain: applying it would fuse two groups through a link the engine never made); a property carries a listing NO group of the generation holds (`carries_ungrouped_listings`) **unless this engine's own live merge already put that listing on that property together with a grouped member** (its ledger's `member_ids`, closed transitively) — the engine vouched for its members only, an older merge (the removed legacy engine's among them) is not its word, and D7 forbids reading who else made one, so a property an OPERATOR merged that carries listings the engine did not group is refused too, fail-closed; or the ledger says no (E305). **The plan is re-checked at apply time:** inside each group's own transaction, just before it merges, the involved properties' listings and the three negatives are read again; a group whose set changed since the plan (`changed_since_plan`) or that a negative recorded during the run now covers is recorded `skipped` and not merged. **A real-time lane generation (`rt…`) is refused outright**: another concurrency group rewrites it every few minutes, so no plan read across several statements can hold still.
- **E304** **Dark three ways.** A dry run is the DEFAULT and writes only the ledger. A live run needs `app_settings.autodedup_apply_enabled = true` — **absent is OFF**, and it is re-read before EVERY group (E39), so flipping it off stops a run between two groups — and an `app_settings.autodedup_apply_scope` object that names `category_types` AND an area (`blocks`, `listing_ids` or `all_blocks: true`; a key set to null is absent) ON ITS OWN — a dispatch cannot supply either, so /settings is the one authority on where live merges happen. A run's own arguments NARROW that row (sets intersect, caps take the minimum, `all_blocks` only from the row), never widen it; an empty `dry_run=` is a dry run. At most `max_clusters_per_run` groups per run (default 200, in `cluster_key` order) — E42's per-run cap; E42's per-hour latch and E43's plausibility monitor are NOT built. The switches live in `public.app_settings`, not `autodedup.settings`, and migration 558 seeds both — `false`, and a scope of `prodej` with NO area — because /settings edits only app_settings and `PUT /admin/app_settings/{key}` 404s on an unseeded key: a kill switch the operator cannot reach without SQL is not one. The `autodedup_apply_` prefix keeps them apart from every other program's keys, which was 528's reason for a separate table.
- **E305** **Idempotent, undo is final for its generation, and a ruling after a merge is reported.** A group already on one property is a counted no-op, so a re-run merges nothing twice — unless an operator negative now covers what that property holds: then the run reports it as `ruled_different_after_merge` (run and step summary), with the `unapply` arguments of the live engine merge behind it when there is one, and changes nothing; undoing is the operator's call. The ledger's partial UNIQUE index forbids two live merges of one (survivor, retired) pair. A property this engine retired that is `active` again was restored by someone — the operator's merge ledger or `unapply` — and is never re-merged on the engine's own authority (`restored_outside_engine`). Only the engine's OWN undo (`undone_by = 'autodedup-unapply:<run>'`) releases a property to a later generation: when `unapply` reaches a group someone already undid, it records `undone_by = 'external'`, and that property stays `restored_outside_engine` for every later generation. `unapply` (NOT gated by the switch) undoes a generation's live groups newest-first through `unmerge_group`; that generation never re-applies (`generation_unapplied`), a later one may. A group whose survivor a LATER merge has since retired is skipped with the reason and the merge to undo first (from the ledger, when the engine made it) — `unmerge_group` would only reactivate empty retired properties — and an undo that would move no listing back is rolled back and reported, never recorded as undone. E41 as built: a CATEGORY refusal at the chokepoint is final for that group in that generation (`refused_at_chokepoint_before`); a property-state refusal (a concurrent operator merge) is re-planned from fresh state on the next run; any other error records the group `failed` and stops the run.
- **E306** **Undo restores the listings and the deal pipeline, not the rest of the operator state.** `unmerge_group` replays the merge's listing moves and restores pipeline cards losslessly (rule 22); collections, tags and notes carried onto the survivor stay there (rule 18: unmerge is best-effort). The ROLLOUT's first week is read on the review pages for exactly that reason.

**Identity is DEMONSTRATED, not presumed — W16 / g8c (D50)**

- **E150** **The reader that knows no FORM.** Every reader before this one knows a spelling — `parcelní číslo`, `stání č.`, `označením B36` — and every new cohort has produced a spelling none of them knew: `B1.2.2` against `B1.2.3`, `F2.103` against `F3.103`, a plot `s označením 7` against `8`, a garage block `G3` against `G4`, `58,90 m²` against `58,70 m²`. Knowing the form is not a strategy, because the developer chooses the form. What does not change is the SHAPE of the hazard: two adverts for two units of one project are written from ONE template, word-identical except at the few positions that name the unit. So `autodedup/body_align.py` knows no form at all — it aligns the two token streams (`difflib`, no model, $0) and reads the positions where a short replace sits between agreeing context on both sides. A token is reduced to what it STATES, never to how it was spelt: a unit CODE compared whole (`B1.2.2` and `B2.2.2` state the same three numbers in a different order, so the string is the fact), or the numbers it carries with the unit symbols removed, compared by E153's rounding rule. Four ways it could fire on ONE unit and what stops each: truncation is an insert, never a replace, and a one-sided token has contradicted nothing (E12); a rewritten paragraph is longer than `MAX_SEGMENT` and the whole reading needs `d43_body_align_min_ratio` of the two bodies to align first; the price, the date, the phone, the order code, the percentage, the photo count and the availability month are MASKED to sentinels before alignment; and a number the other advert prints somewhere else of its own is a difference of BASIS, not of unit (`48,4 podlahová` / `52,4 užitná`). The STREET is not read here — this module works on a folded stream with no capitals, so "the two tokens after `ulice`" was an adjective as often as a name (`mimořádně` against `velmi` split one 120 m² flat two portals both carried). Settings: `d43_body_align`, `d43_body_align_min_ratio`.
- **E151** **The street the BODY names, read prose against prose.** A portal's resolved street key is missing on exactly the adverts that need it — two Olomouc office blocks, one on Litovelská and one on třída 28. října, same obec, no street key on either side, the prose the only place either street is written. `text_facts.prose_streets` reads it: the keyword is mandatory, the name must be CAPITALISED in the original text (that is what tells a street from the genitive of a common noun), a name introduced as a LANDMARK (`nedaleko`, `v blízkosti`, `zastávka`) is not this advert's address, and the capture is cut at a word that cannot be a street — a portal truncates the title after `ul.` and the body resumes with `Nabízíme`, which otherwise became the only street on its side. Each capture also yields its PREFIXES, and a numeral alone (`20.` of `tř. 20. dubna`) is never a name: an over-read name must never be the only thing on its side, because a street fact is a REFUSAL. It is compared with the other BODY's streets only, never with a resolved key — the key's gaps were refusing duplicates the operator had confirmed. Setting: `d43_prose_street`.
- **E152** **The town the BODY prints, where E135 suppresses the geocode.** E135 drops the obec fact below street grain, which is right for a village routinely advertised under its district town and wrong for two adverts that each PRINT their own different town. The ambiguity is in the GEOCODE, not in the prose: an advert that writes `Oplocany u Tovačova` and never writes `Droždín` has stated which town it is in. Fires only when each body names its own resolved town and neither names the other's. Setting: `d43_prose_obec`.
- **E153** **The headline area read WITHOUT the stored column, by the ROUNDING rule.** `stated_areas` clamps every printed m² to `stored/3 .. stored×3`, and 16 % of the region corpus prints a headline outside that window because the portal stored a terrace, a cellar or the plot — bazos stores `area_m2 = 10.0` for a 76 m² apartment whose body says `terasou o velikosti 10 m²`, and every reader that goes through the clamp is then blind to the 76. So `printed_areas` never looks at the column: it SCOPES each mention by the noun that carries it (the terrace's size is the terrace's, a bedroom's is the bedroom's) and what is left is the size the advert is sold BY. Equality is arithmetic, not a tolerance: two numbers agree when they agree within half of the coarser one's last printed digit, so `50` == `50,5` and `58,90` != `58,70`. Two rails keep it honest — both BODIES must print an area (a stored column alone is the `area` fact at the tolerance a stored column deserves), and the column is then read too and can reconcile them (47,6 against 46,6, both stored 47). The column is dropped only on the exact degenerate signature: the stored figure is one the body prints for an ACCESSORY *and* the body prints a headline several times larger. Settings: `d43_printed_area`.
- **E154** **E145 fails CLOSED, and a storey typed twice is not a flat.** As shipped, `_same_feed` returned False whenever either broker key was null, which SILENCES the same-portal one-storey fact — and bazos, bezrealitky and maxima are 100 % null, so floors 3 and 4 of one bezrealitky new-build fused (Vrbenského 1640/43 at 60,948 and 65,418; THE FIZZ Na Zátorách floors 5 and 6 at 11,290 and 12,025). A null key is UNKNOWN, not a licence: the fact now stands unless both keys are known AND different. Its other half is the guard that makes it affordable — with the feed unknown, the one-storey gap is a fact only when the PRICE disagrees too. One 131 m² 4+1 in a Jablonec vila is re-posted on ceskereality at 6,988,000 and 6,980,000 with its storey written both `1. NP` and `2. NP`, and every Hartigova bazos re-post carries its own `[ID 808xx]` and the same price at floors N and N−1. Neither signal is a fact alone; together they are. Setting: `floor_feed_unknown_closed`.
- **E155** **The parcel forms the narrow keyword missed.** `parcelní číslo: st. 661` (the stavební-parcela prefix, 21 of 25 bodies), `pozemek č. N` (23 of 30), `parcelu N/M` with no `č.` (15 of 19), `pod číslem parcely N` (11 of 11), `na parcele N` (5 of 5), `č. parc. N` (2 of 2). Widening is FAIL-SAFE in one direction only — an empty set is never a conflict, so the narrow reader's silence cost protection, not precision — but a WRONG number is a conflict, so the wide form still requires a keyword and a digit immediately after it, and never reads `parcely o celkové výměře 1000 m2` as a parcel. Setting: `d43_parcel_forms_wide`.
- **E156** **No fact, no separation: the re-partitioner may not drop a member that carries nothing against its group.** The local search moves ONE member at a time and only on a strict gain, so two adverts joined by a merge edge with no fact at all can end in two cells because a conflict elsewhere in the component broke their cell up first — Penzion Horálka (10060166 × 10064638), same 374 m², same price, same body, sreality against mmreality, cut to two singletons by a conflict neither was party to. The repair asks, of each merge edge still cut, whether the invariants can hold its two ends together, and puts one end back where they can. It is a REPAIR, not a second optimisation: re-joining whole cells re-opens the search's own choices and measured WORSE than leaving them alone. Each member moves at most once per round and the edges are read in `edge.rank` order, so the result stays a function of the edge SET. Setting: `repartition_keep_factless`.
- **E157** **(A) The key facts must be POSITIVELY EQUAL.** `demonstrate.demonstration_gap` names the first key fact two adverts do not demonstrably agree on, and a band pair carrying one is not promoted. The area must be KNOWN on both sides from a trustworthy reading and equal within rounding — an area neither the body nor a trustworthy column states is UNKNOWN, and unknown demonstrates nothing. The disposition must be equal (land has none to state). The price must be equal, or on one agreed PATH, or a cut between two SEQUENTIAL postings — and sequential is read relative to the two lives, because two Okružní garages first sighted seven minutes apart, the cheaper one dying two days later, also overlap by "only 1.59 days" when that is its entire life. The obec must be the same. The price limb is read at CLUSTER grain too, for the same reason E132 reads the facts there: a group is built transitively, and three houses of one Hlubočky parcelling at 9,650,000 / 9,750,000 / 9,850,000 sit on six portals, so the pairwise filter refuses every ceskereality pair among them and the group still closes through a sixth portal whose 5 % cross-portal slack covers the 2 % between them. It reads only gaps UNDER 20 %, which is what keeps it clear of D49: every counter-example D49 refused the co-live price contradiction on is a RATIO — one Harrachov flat at a freehold 8,350,000 and a co-operative SHARE of 1,670,000, one plot at a dražba 5,400 and an asking 17,000. The small gap is the neighbouring unit; the large one is two prices for one unit. Settings: `demonstrate_identity`, `demonstrate_price_colive_days`, `demonstrate_price_colive_fraction`, `demonstrate_require_disposition`, `demonstrate_require_obec`, `demonstrate_cluster_price`.
- **E158** **(B) Unit-grade corroboration: the evidence that is about THIS UNIT and not about the building it stands in.** One of a tight non-catalogue photo FILE in common, a body one advert essentially contains, or a shared rare order code. Agreement on public attributes is what every unit of one project has; these three are what only one unit has. Three modes: `unit` asks for one of them everywhere, `development_only` asks for one of them inside a development (`development.PROJECT_TERMS`, one spelling, shared with the hold) and for nothing outside one, `two_of` accepts two of the wider list (the three above plus a shared address point and an identical price path). Setting: `corroboration`, `corroboration_body_containment`, `corroboration_min`.
- **E159** **The ladder is nested by CONSTRUCTION, not by measurement.** Both filters act only on PROMOTION — the merge zone is the engine's own certified evidence and is untouched — and every corroboration mode answers `unit` first, so S ⊆ M ⊆ L holds on merged pairs whatever the data does. `Settings.__post_init__` refuses `corroboration` without `demonstrate_identity`, which is where the identity is enforced rather than hoped for, and every run asserts it on the merged-pair sets.

**Exactness where identity is CLAIMED — W17 / S2 (D53, D54)**

- **E160** **A tolerance is not a demonstration.** E157 reads "price equal" at 5 % across portals and "area equal" off the UNION of the body and the stored column, and both bars are wide enough to hold the next unit. Kozolupy 11,250,000 against 11,500,000 is 2.2 % and sat in one arm-S cluster of 25; Chotěšov 75,52 m² against 75,64 m² met because both portals store 76 and a 0-decimal 76 meets every number that rounds to it. So where identity is being CLAIMED rather than merely not contradicted: the price must be EXACT, or on the other's recorded PATH, or a cut between two sequential postings (`demonstrate_price_exact`, `demonstrate_price_exact_tol` = 0.002, which is what writing 11,25 mil. costs and nothing more); and where BOTH bodies print the size to a DECIMAL, the printed figures decide and the column may not overrule them (`demonstrate_area_printed_decides`, `d43_printed_area_decimals_decide`). Two printed INTEGERS stay on the union — one idnes body prints `62 m²` for a flat whose column, and the other advert's body, both say 54, and the operator has confirmed that pair; an integer carries no claim about its own last digit. The price limb travels to cluster grain through `demonstrate_cluster_price` unchanged, which is what stops a group of 25 holding both Kozolupy twins. Measured cost on certain duplicates: the price limb +0.44 / +0.31 / +0.16 pp on cohort 3 / cohort 4 / the region, the area limb +0.07 / +0.04 / — pp.
- **E161** **The printed unit code, read WHOLE and in the BARE form.** `unit_designators` reads a keyword and stops at the word boundary, so `s označením B2.2.1` reads `B2` and `s označením B1.2.2` reads `B1` — B1 against B1 is no conflict — and `byt B1.2.1 o dispozici`, with no keyword at all, reads nothing. `text_facts.printed_unit_codes` anchors on the unit NOUN instead and keeps the code whole. Two segments minimum, because a bare `B2` is a BUILDING and every flat in it shares it; at most four codes per body, because more is a price list; an empty set is never a conflict. `wide` adds the Roman numeral (`vila II`), the number word (`dům číslo tři`) and the single letter (`jednotka označením A`), each behind an explicit `označením` / `č.` / `číslo` marker — without it `dům i zahrada` and `byt a garáž` are Czech grammar, not unit names. Settings: `d43_unit_codes`, `d43_unit_codes_wide`. Cost on certain duplicates: 0.055 % / 0.029 % / 0.085 %, and the skeptic's hand read of the 10 cohort-3 and 22 region pairs it splits found every one of them two units.
- **E162** **Inside a development a SILENT side fails closed; outside one it does not.** E12 says a missing datum is not a difference, and that is right everywhere except where the whole hazard is that the developer prints the unit's name on one advert and not on the next. So inside a development context a unit code, a printed storey or a printed area that ONE side states and the other never mentions is a demonstration gap (`demonstrate_onesided`). The context must be narrow or the rule is a blanket: `PROJECT_TERMS` on either side — which is what E158's `development_only` reads — fires on 41.8 % / 27.7 % / 16.8 % of the three cohorts' certain duplicates. `development_context_mode="narrow"` asks for the new-build vocabulary on BOTH sides AND the project naming a unit (a whole printed code) or quoting a price list, which is 1.2 % / 1.0 % / 1.2 %, and the one-sided limb it gates then costs 0.10 % / 0.05 % / 0.22 % where the wide reading costs 23× more. `template` is the third reading, measured and NOT shipped: adding "two co-live adverts written from one template" takes 3,574 of cohort 4's 5,062 both-vocabulary certain pairs, because a re-post carries a template too. The stored floor COLUMN is deliberately not one of the one-sided readings — one portal fills it and another does not, which is one-sided on 3,304 of cohort 4's and 4,525 of the region's certain duplicates and says nothing about the advert.
- **E163** **The generic reader healed: what moves between two postings of ONE unit.** E150's mask set let through six classes, each taken from a certain duplicate it split, and they concentrate on SAME-PORTAL re-posts (remax 25 %, realitymix 10.9 %, sreality 10.6 %, idnes 7.7 %): a charge in a spelling `[CENA]` misses (`Provize RK: 12.000.- Kč`, `náklady na bydlení 5500`, `19tis.kč`, `CZK 41,500`), the TERM of a contract (`nájmu činí 3 měsíce`, `anuita na 35 let`), a date with no year (`od 1.7.`, anchored on the date word because a bare `5.13` is a unit code), an order code shorter than `MIN_CODE_LEN` (`ev.č. 0831`), an inventory multiplier (`6x pokoj`), a length in metres (`dlouhý 79 m`, a garage door `2,2 m` — `m2` is untouched because `m\b` cannot match where a `2` follows) and a RANGE one portal printed without its hyphen (masking ONE side is enough: a one-sided token is never read). A code's later segments must also STATE something — `D5. Více` glues into `d5.vice`, which the wide code form read as a code `d5` does not contain. Setting: `d43_body_align_heal`. It closes 77 / 72 / 14 certain-duplicate splits on the three cohorts and newly opens 1 / 3 / 2.
- **E164** **A reading NOBODY states is not a disagreement.** 87 % of arm M's A-limb losses are a MISSING price or an unreadable area rather than a contradiction, and 814 of 818 of them already carry unit-grade corroboration. `demonstration_shortfall` therefore returns `(fact, kind)` and a `missing` kind may be waived where the pair carries evidence only one unit has: three or more tight non-catalogue photo FILES with the weakest-but-one shared room above the interior floor wherever anything is known about it (a developer reuses the exterior render, not three interiors), a shared rare order code, or a body one advert essentially IS — the last only OUTSIDE a development, where a shared body is the developer's template and not this unit's. A `contradiction` is never waived: no photograph outvotes a statement. Settings: `demonstrate_recover_missing`, `demonstrate_recover_min_photos`, `demonstrate_recover_body_containment`.
- **E165** **E143 reads two numbers as SIMULTANEOUS, and two sequential postings never were.** The two-unit signature's whole argument is that an advert has one area and one price AT ANY MOMENT; it says nothing about two adverts that were never on sale at one moment. A re-post at a cut price whose area one portal re-parsed carries both halves of it, and four of the region skeptic's twelve named false splits are exactly that. Setting: `d43_two_unit_requires_colive`. A co-live pair is untouched — there the two numbers ARE simultaneous.
- **E166** **A storey is a fact of its own, never a position in the body aligner.** `body_align` normalising `2. NP` and `1. patro` onto one scale and leaving the token readable newly split 53 cohort-3 and 73 cohort-4 certain duplicates on nothing but a storey — more than the whole heal closed. E133/E145 already measured why: 45 % of cross-portal known duplicates carry a one-storey gap because two portals write `přízemí` two ways, and the camps and the feed are what handle it. So the storey sentinel carries the level and no digit, and the storey the BODY prints (`text_facts.printed_floors`, `1. NP` = the ground floor) is read by the `prose_floor` fact under the SAME rule as the column: a gap of two is a fact anywhere, a gap of one only inside one feed. It is worth reading because the stored column is null on one side of a third of the corpus. Setting: `d43_prose_floor`.

**What the cohort-5 confirmation named — W18 / S3 (D57)**

The cohort-5 confirmation read S2's groups on unseen ground and found **nine** fusions against g7's seven, and the two sets are DISJOINT: S2 correctly splits every one of g7's seven and introduces its own. Each of the seven rules below is one of those shapes, read back into the engine, and each is a settings field measured alone on the five cohorts' certain duplicates before it was shipped (M350–M366).

- **E180** **The ground-floor convention is a difference BETWEEN camps, and inside ONE PORTAL a storey is a storey.** N1/E145 excuses a one-storey gap because `přízemí` is `1. podlaží` on one portal and the floor above it on another; that excuse exists only where the two portals count differently. Two sreality adverts of Rezidence Pražská 84 state floors 2 and 3 for 136 days together at one price and one area, under two different broker keys so E154's feed rule never fired — inside one portal there is no vocabulary left to blame. What survives untouched is E145's own case: idnes against sreality, where the offset is real. **The SCOPE is the portal, not the camp table, and the measurement is why.** `same_camp` — a known offset of ZERO — reads `floor_camps` as evidence that two portals AGREE, and the table was never fitted for that question: it places idnes and ceskereality both at level 0, and a hand read of the 182 certain duplicates the camp scope splits on cohort 5 is almost all idnes against ceskereality one storey apart at identical price and area over long overlaps (112399 x 401485 is 120 m² and 9,490,000 on both sides for 85 days). A table fitted to EXCUSE a gap may not be read as proof that there is nothing to excuse. `d43_floor_within_camp_scope` therefore ships as `source`; the `camp` reading stays in the tree for a wave that re-fits the table. **Three guards ride with it, each bought by a named pair.** (i) Two SEQUENTIAL postings keep the excuse — 29 bazos re-posts of one Dašice advert drift 0/1 between re-posts, and a parse that drifts is not a storey.
