**The sample-size arithmetic, stated because it is where evaluation plans usually break.** A Wilson 95% lower bound of 0.99 on a perfect sample needs **n ≥ 380**; with one error, ~650. A 150-pair stratum can never clear a 0.99 gate — its best possible lower bound is 0.975. The precision sample is therefore **400 gold-judged auto-merge pairs minimum** (D3).

**Why 99.5% precision would justify autonomy — and what it does not.** A false merge fuses two properties and corrupts the price history the operator is buying. It is recoverable: `unmerge_group` replays the ledger deterministically, and every autonomous merge would be a **single-child** `merge_group_id` (E38), so reverting a whole generation is linear. At ~30,000 expected corpus-wide merges, 99.5% would leave ~150 bad merges — all surfaced in the residual/groups UI and all revertible. **Reversibility is what buys the threshold, not the threshold itself.** In this program nothing is applied at all (D4), so nothing can be reverted either: reversibility stays an **argued** claim here — argued from `unmerge_group`'s deterministic replay and from the single-child group shape (E38) — and is carried into W6's decision package **with that caveat stated**. Measuring it means applying and undoing real merges through the production chokepoint, which this program may not do; that rehearsal is Appendix A5 and needs its own operator decision.

---

## 7. Q6 — LLM judge

Full specification is delivered separately as the **JUDGE SPEC**. In outline: pre-flight obligations (the `called_for` migration and `PRICES` rows before any calling code; the `--max-usd` "done not drew" assertion; smoke runs for the never-exercised vision-in-batch and Qwen paths); T1 `gpt-5.6-luna` text on 100% of band pairs at **$0.00086/pair batched**, T2 `gpt-5-mini` vision on ~30% at **$0.00284/pair batched**, blended **$0.0017 per banded pair**; a three-vote gold judge at ~$0.018/pair; the four-way forced-tool verdict with a required `unit_discriminator` (E27); the PII rule (E28); the three separately-reported agreement numbers with **gold-vs-operator below 0.95 stopping the program** (D6).

Two things worth repeating here because they change the cost model. First, `gpt-5-mini` and `gpt-5.6-luna` are **reasoning models**: this design budgets 1,200–1,500 output tokens per verdict, roughly double what a naive count gives, and W3 replaces the estimate with the measured `llm_calls` average before any threshold is fixed (E32). Second, `COMPARISON_MAX_EDGE = 768` at q80 is the single biggest cost lever in the whole program — roughly one third the vision tokens of the 1568 tier.

**Spend gate (D2).** A lane costing **under $10 runs autonomously**; **$25 is the hard per-run cap** wired into `--max-usd`; **$200 is the program total**. Every paid lane asserts the batch read *done*, not merely *drew*, and writes its measured spend into the `autodedup.iterations` row for that iteration so the progress page shows real dollars, never forecasts.

---

## 8. Q7 — Clustering

Constrained union-find with must-not-link (E33), invariants (E34), immutable identity and medoid attachment (E36), and no autonomous bridging (E37) — narrowed by **E57**, which re-offers a refused bridge once and applies it only when the merged member set satisfies every invariant and the edge is a certificate or near-certain.

Every refused union writes an `autodedup.cluster_conflicts` row with the offending invariant — these are the **highest-value items in the validation UI**, because a conflict means the engine found strong evidence in both directions.

**What the cluster row stores** (`autodedup.clusters`): `cluster_key`, `generation`, `size`, `block_key`, `cat_group`, `category_main`, `category_type`, `area_min/max`, `sources[]`, `medoid_listing_id`, `min_edge_score` (the weakest accepted edge — the UI's default sort, because that is where errors live), `mean_edge_score`, `n_judged_edges`, `n_certificate_edges`, `evidence_families` (bitmask), `max_gap_days`, `shared_photo_warning`, `status`, `property_id`, `model_version`, `feature_version`, timestamps. Members live in `cluster_members` with `joined_via_lo/hi`.

**Fragmentation is a gate, not just a metric.** Under E6 a property may carry five or more listings across years. Splitting one true property into three clusters is invisible both to pairwise precision *and* to a residual scan that only surfaces pairs above a score floor — so the operator's UI would show a clean result while the price history the program exists to build stays fragmented. §9 therefore gates on cluster **recall** (mean clusters per true property ≤ 1.15 on gold-judged properties), not only purity.

---

## 9. Q8 — Evaluation protocol

**Labels** (`autodedup.labels`), all fresh and all built inside `autodedup.*` (D7 — nothing is imported from any prior dedup effort, and `property_merge_events` is not a label source): operator verdicts (weight 1.0, the oracle), gold LLM three-vote (0.6), cheap LLM (0.3), rule-certain certificates (bootstrap). Split **60/20/20 by cluster, never by pair** — pairs inside one cluster are not independent and a pair-level split leaks.

**AN IMPLIED LABEL COMES OUT OF THE RULING, NOT OUT OF THE CURRENT CLUSTERING (E58).** Confirming a group asserts that every pair inside it is one property — inside **that** group, the set of adverts the operator was looking at, which `verdicts.member_ids` now records. Before it did, the labels lane and the agreement read expanded a confirmation over `cluster_members` at read time, so a later pass that grew or absorbed the key silently changed what an old label asserted: promoting g5 moved 21 of the operator's 224 confirmations that way and then erased g4's clusters entirely, leaving `mode=labels generation=g4` unable to reproduce itself at all. Both reads now expand `member_ids`; a legacy row with none falls back to the members of its OWN generation, and only where the key belongs to one pass. **"Does this ruling apply here?" is answered on the SET by all three readers** — the Groups page, the agreement read and the labels lane: after 538 every backfilled ruling reads `g4`, so a generation-string test alone would have exported zero implied labels for g5 while the UI showed 203 of those rulings applying to g5 groups that never moved. The string arm survives as a union, not as the test — it is what keeps a pass's own rulings exportable while that pass's clusters are missing from the store. The engine view beside a label (`ENGINE_PAIRS_SQL`) is likewise scoped by the pair's own `generation` column rather than by the `(model_version, feature_version)` its clusters happened to carry — a derivation that broke the moment those clusters were re-stamped away. The seeded `sample_rank` an implied label carries is still a property of the generation's live cluster set, so reproducing a g4 sample needs g4's clustering back (a re-run of the score lane at g4's settings), not just the verdicts.

**Metrics and pass criteria**

| # | metric | how | pass |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Pairwise precision** at auto-merge | 400 gold-judged auto-merge pairs, Clopper–Pearson/Wilson 95% | **LB ≥ 0.99 on n ≥ 380, point ≥ 0.995, no stratum < 0.97** (D3), no systematic error class ≥3 occurrences |
| 2 | **Pairwise recall** | Horvitz–Thompson-weighted over score-decile strata with known sampling rates; bootstrap CI over 2,000 resamples | ≥ 0.85 at auto-merge, ≥ 0.95 including the band |
| 3 | **The recall probe** — the honest attack on "we cannot see the positives we threw away" | 300 sampled listings: uncapped retrieval (every probe, no `max_block_size`) **plus** brute-force in-block CLIP top-50; cheap-judge everything the engine rejected (~9,000 pairs), gold-judge whatever the cheap judge calls a duplicate (~250) | missed-duplicate rate ≤ 2% of listings |
| 4 | **Blocking-recall bound** | one exhaustive O(n²) pass over the ~600-listing sparse block (~180k pairs, pure CPU, $0), labels budgeted | reported, not gated — it bounds what the probes miss |
| 5 | **Cluster purity** | 100 sampled clusters, every internal pair judged | ≥ 0.98 |
| 6 | **Cluster recall / fragmentation** | mean clusters per gold-judged true property | ≤ 1.15 |
| 7 | **Residual-duplicate rate** | the operator scrolls the residual view for 30 minutes | < 5 true duplicates per 100 properties reviewed |
| 8 | **Judge fidelity** | cheap-vs-gold ≥ 0.93; **gold-vs-operator ≥ 0.95 on a 200-pair operator session run through the validation UI, or the program stops** (D6); gold unanimity ≥ 0.92 | as stated |
| 9 | **Cost per decided listing** | read from `llm_calls` by `called_for` and model (E32) | projected monthly ≤ $150 |

**Metric 10 (revert wall-clock) has moved to Appendix A5 and is not a gate of this program.** Timing a revert means applying 500 real merges through `merge_properties` and undoing them — production writes to `properties`, `listings.property_id`, `property_merge_events` and operator state, which D4 forbids here. Reversibility therefore stays the argued claim of §6, and W6's decision package says so in those words.

**Sample sizes.** 4,000 cheap bootstrap labels; 1,200 gold; 400 precision; ~9,250 recall probe; 200 operator-anchored (the D6 session); 100 clusters. Strata: auto-merge / band / near-reject / far-reject / same-portal / cross-portal / bazos / remax / land+commercial / exploded-photo-block / **negative control**.

**How the D6 agreement number is computed (E55), and by what.** It is read live off the store by `GET /autodedup/agreement` and shown on the progress page as **"Shoda operátor × soudce"**, rather than computed once by a lane at the end of a wave. The comparison is **binary — one property, or not** — which since D39 is also what the operator is asked (the store may still hold the two finer values, and they count as "not one" like any negative); the judge keeps its own four. `same` ↔ `same_property` is "one"; `different` / `same_building_different_unit` / `same_project_different_unit` ↔ `different_property` / `same_building_different_unit` is "not one". The **operator's label set is two things unioned**: explicit pair verdicts, and the pairs **IMPLIED** by a cluster verdict of `same` (confirming a group is a statement about every pair inside it — 103 confirmed groups carry far more pair-grade evidence than the handful of pairs ruled on one at a time). An explicit verdict **wins** over an implied one, `unsure` is not a label, and only `same` clusters imply anything: a rejected group says the members are not *all* one property, never which pair inside it was wrong. The implied expansion is **bounded** (clusters above 12 members are not expanded — n members imply n(n−1)/2 pairs — and the number of confirmed groups skipped is reported, not dropped silently). The **judge's** label is the best tier per pair, **gold > vision > text**, with `oss` excluded entirely and `insufficient_evidence` excluded from the agreement and **counted separately**. Each stratum reports n, agreement and a **Wilson 95%** interval (computed in `autodedup/agreement.py`, in Python, where it can be checked by hand), plus the two directions of disagreement kept apart — judge said `different` where the operator said `same`, and the reverse — and the explicit/implied split of the labels, so the number can never quietly become a number about groups. The page prints the D6 bar beside it and how far the session is from the ~200 gold-labelled pairs the gate names; below that bar it says the sample is not yet enough to decide, rather than painting a verdict on an unfinished number.

**Feedback loop.** Operator verdicts land in `autodedup.verdicts` and, for negatives, in `must_not_link`. They override gold labels, become weight-1.0 training rows, and would drive **the latch** (E42) on any future write path. A refit is triggered manually, never automatically, and a new model must beat the incumbent **on the sealed test split** before `activate` flips the active flag.

**The verdict vocabulary is three values on the page and five in the store** (migration 528, widened by **532**, narrowed on the surface by **D39**). The page asks `same` · `different` · `unsure` — **Stejné / Různé / Nevím** — because the engine consumes every negative identically: one permanent `must_not_link`, one negative calibration label. The two finer values `532` wrote (`same_building_different_unit`, `same_project_different_unit`, E49) remain **valid stored values** — the CHECK is untouched, no row is rewritten, a ruling taken under them reads back as **Různé** and a filter asking for `different` returns it — and they are no longer offered anywhere in the UI. **Every negative is negative in the same way**; `same` retracts the operator's own row; `unsure` writes neither.

**The split verdict** — `POST /autodedup/verdict/split` `{cluster_key, generation, units:[{listing_id, unit}], confirm_retract?, note?}` — is how a whole group is ruled on unit by unit, because a group is frequently not one answer. The operator labels every member with a unit (a letter), and the route writes, **in one transaction**:

- for each member pair **inside one unit**: a pair verdict `same`, plus a `must_not_link` **retraction** (the correction half of the loop — a veto the operator has taken back must stop vetoing);
- for each member pair **across two units**: a pair verdict `different` (D39), plus a permanent `must_not_link` with reason `operator split: <relation>`. The body carries **no relation at all** — the letters are the whole statement — and the server reads a missing `relation` as `different`; `relation` and `relations` (E51) survive as the **compatibility path** for a client that still names one, so a bookmarked extension or an older build cannot start writing a verdict it did not mean;
- the **cluster** verdict: `same` when one unit was used, otherwise the **weakest** relation the split used (which, from the page, is always `different`), carrying the assignment (`A: 94020,140903 | B: 94492 | C: 268009`) as its note.

**The candidate split** — `POST /autodedup/verdict/candidate-split` `{candidate_key, generation, units:[{listing_id, unit}], confirm_retract?, note?}` — is the same ruling over a **candidate group** (E56), the packing of residual pairs the `/autodedup/residual` groups view serves. It writes, in ONE transaction and through the same statements: a `same` pair verdict plus a must-not-link **retraction** for each member pair inside one unit, and `different` plus a permanent `must_not_link` for each pair across two units (the same missing-relation default, D39). Three differences from the cluster split, all of them consequences of there being **no cluster row**:

- **no cluster verdict is written** — a candidate group is a packing of this generation's residual pairs, not a proposal the engine made, so there is nothing to confirm or reject; the ruling IS its pair rows, which is also how the card reads itself back (E50);
- **a lock is never written through** — a member pair whose two adverts belong to the SAME already-merged group is skipped entirely (reported as `n_pairs_locked`), because that pair is the Groups page's ruling; and an assignment that gives two adverts of one lock different letters is a **400** that names the group and says where to split it;
- **no reason chips** — the split stamps its chips on the cluster row, and there is none here; stamping the fan-out instead would post one reason row per pair from a single click and the §9 histogram would measure card size rather than what the operator saw. A non-empty `reasons` is a 400, not a silent drop; the free-text note rides on every pair row in front of the assignment summary.

Everything else is unchanged and deliberately so: the assignment must name every advert of the card exactly once (400), the card must exist in the named generation (404, and a generation nothing clustered is a 404 before anything is packed), the pair need not exist in `autodedup.pairs`, and the **409 confirmation** of E52 applies as written. The verdicts it writes are ordinary `kind='pair'` rows, so they reach `AGREEMENT_PAIRS_SQL`'s explicit operator set exactly as a pair ruled one at a time does — which is what makes a card ruled here count towards the D6 gate.

**A split that takes a veto back asks first** (E52): when a same-unit pair already carries a *negative* verdict from the same operator, the route answers **409** naming those pairs and writes nothing until the call repeats with `confirm_retract`. The assignment of a group the operator has already split is read back off the stored pair verdicts (E50), so the page they confirm from is the ruling that exists rather than a blank one.

**The reason vocabulary** (migration 533, `autodedup.verdicts.reasons text[]`). Every verdict control — pair, cluster and split — carries structured **reason chips** plus the free-text note that has been on the table since 528. **Both are optional and both stay** (the operator's own words at D39: keep them, they will be used only sometimes): collapsed by default on cards and rows, never required, never blocking a save, and no count, strip or panel assumes one is there. This is the feedback loop's missing half: the verdict says WHAT the operator decided, the reasons say **what they saw**. A chip like `floor_plan_differs` on a pair the engine merged names a discriminator the feature set does not have, and the per-code histogram (`/autodedup/stats` → `engine.verdict_reasons`, pair and cluster grain kept apart) is directly comparable with the judge's `unit_discriminator` — the same question, asked of the model and of the human. It is read on the progress page as **"Důvody operátora"**.

The codes, with the operator's own labels: `floor_plan_differs` (Jiný půdorys) · `unit_number` (Číslo jednotky) · `kitchen_or_bathroom_differs` (Jiná kuchyň nebo koupelna) · `floor_differs` (Jiné podlaží) · `area_differs` (Jiná výměra) · `price_history` (Cenová historie sedí) · `description` (Popis) · `broker` (Stejný makléř) · `identical_photos` (Stejné fotky) · `catalogue_photos_only` (Jen katalogové fotky) · `relisted_after_gap` (Znovu inzerováno po pauze) · `same_project` (Stejný projekt) · `other` (Jiné).

Mechanics, in one place: the registry lives in `autodedup/verdict_reasons.py` and is **served** at `GET /autodedup/verdict-reasons` so the SPA hard-codes none of it (a copied list is a second vocabulary, and it drifts the first review session that names a new shape). The column carries **no CHECK constraint** — unlike 532's `verdict` domain — because the admin API is the only writer and validates every code (unknown ⇒ **400**), so growing the list is one line of Python and no migration. Codes are **de-duplicated, order preserved**; the note stays optional. A **split** stamps its reasons on the **cluster row only**, never on the pairwise fan-out: a split is one ruling, per-pair reasons would claim the operator said something about each edge that they never said, and — the reason it matters to the readout — one click on a 6-member group would otherwise post 15 pair rows, so the pair histogram would measure cluster size instead of evidence and stop being comparable with the judge's per-pair `unit_discriminator`. The fan-out rows keep their machine note (`operator split: …`). The cluster note still leads with the operator's words and then the assignment string. Re-deciding a verdict **upserts** reasons and note with it; editing only the annotation re-posts the stored verdict through the same endpoint. A store that predates 533 answers **503 naming 533** on a write (`UndefinedColumn`, distinct from 532's rejected `CheckViolation` value); a READ degrades instead — the stats histogram comes back empty, and the four review statements that select `v.reasons` (groups, group detail, residual, pair) answer `store_ready: false` like a missing relation, since `store_ready` probes the RELATION and cannot see a missing column. In the SPA, a card that shows both the split's picker and the cluster verdict's names them apart (**„důvod rozdělení"** vs **„důvod verdiktu"**): two identical toggles over two different drafts silently drop whichever set was not the one saved.

**Cluster MEMBERSHIP is the validation, never `autodedup.pairs`.** A cluster is the union of the edges the engine accepted, so two members can sit in one cluster with no scored edge between them at all; requiring a pair row would refuse exactly the rulings a five-member group needs. The assignment must therefore name every member of the cluster exactly once and nobody else (400), the cluster must exist in the named generation (404), and a store that predates migration 532 answers **503 naming the migration** rather than 500ing on the CHECK violation.

---

## 10. Q9 — Cost model at full scale

**Governing equation (E25):** `monthly = L × c × b × $0.0017`.

**L, measured not guessed.** The doc-sourced figure is 39,794 listings first seen in 7 days ≈ 5,685/day ≈ **170,000/month**. The census re-measures it (probe B5) before any threshold is fixed. `c = 20` candidates/listing.

| band `b` | monthly | per listing |
|---|---|---|
| 0.005 | **$29** | $0.00017 |
| 0.010 | **$58** | $0.00034 |
| 0.015 | **$87** | $0.00051 |
| 0.020 | **$116** | $0.00068 |
| 0.026 | $150 | $0.00088 — the ceiling |

`ad_band_budget` ships at **0.015 ≈ $87/month**, with `T_lo` auto-raising to fit and the sacrificed recall printed. Everything outside the band costs **$0**. This is the steady-state figure a *future* always-on lane would carry; in this program the only spend is the trial's own iterations (§14).

**Per-listing marginal cost** for blocking, features, scoring and clustering: pure CPU over already-paid substrate. **$0.**

**Corpus backfill is explicitly out of scope for this program (D9).** The arithmetic is retained in Appendix A so the rollout conversation has a number to start from, but nothing in W0–W7 backfills anything.

**Storage** (Supabase, outside the LLM budget — see the schema migration's sizing block): trial cohort well under 60 MB. The full-corpus projection is `listing_fp` 250 MB, `image_band` 660 MB (the largest line; `ad_anchor_images` 3→2 would take it to ~440 MB at a recall cost), `phash_pop` 30 MB, `pairs` 200 MB (only `score ≥ 0.02` persists), everything else ~100 MB — **~1.2 GB** — and that projection only becomes real if a backfill is later approved. No multi-GB pair table: rejected pairs are re-derivable from the block index and are counted, not stored.

**R2 Class-B reads.** The 10M/month free tier is per account and already consumed by production `/images/` redirects. The trial's vision judging adds a five-figure number of GetObjects at $0.36/M overage — pennies, but it belongs in the table because it lands on a line the $200 does not cover.

**GPU: $0.** CLIP (11.3M vectors) and dHash exist corpus-wide; cosines over ≤60 candidates need no ANN index. DINOv3 is a non-asset: migration 480 applied with **0 rows**, and `data/dinov3_config.json` still has null `resolution`/`preprocessing`/`dtype` so the loader deliberately refuses. **$20 is reserved** for a single RTX 3090 bake-off ($0.22/h) *only if* W4's error analysis proves image confusion is the dominant residual error class.

---

## 11. Q10 — Realtime integration (designed; not built in this program unless approved)

W7 ships this section as a **design-only document** by default; building the lane requires explicit operator approval (§14).

**The lane.** One tuple appended to the `lanes` list in `scraper/realtime_worker.py`, following the three-piece contract exactly as `location_resolve` does:

```python
("autodedup", lambda: _lane_loop(
    "autodedup", stop_event, _read_autodedup_interval,
    lambda: _autodedup_pass(stop_event, state), state,
    default_interval=0)),
```

`default_interval=0` is the **fail-safe, not a default**: `_lane_loop` keeps this value when the `app_settings` read *raises*, and the flag lives inside the read that just failed — a positive fallback would turn a dark lane on for one pass on any pooler blip, which is exactly when it must not run. The reader returns 0 unless `autodedup_enabled` is true. `_record_pass(state, "autodedup", last)` puts it in `worker_heartbeats` and on the Health page for free; `check_worker_lane_stall` reads `in_flight_s` only, so an idle lane never false-alarms. A pass is bounded by `LANE_PASS_TIMEOUT_SECONDS = 1800` and `_supervised` restarts it after 30 s if it crashes.

**The queue.** `autodedup.resolve_queue`, drained `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` (the `listing_detail_queue` idiom, CLAUDE.md rule 19). Two producers: a one-line enqueue beside the existing `dirty_properties` enqueues in `scraper/db.py` (fired on `new`, and on `updated` only when a **matching-relevant** column moved — price, area, disposition, floor, description, image set); and a backstop sweep over `listing_fp` rows whose `fp_version` is stale. `listing_snapshots` is an append-on-content-change feed (CLAUDE.md rules 2 and 8), so this needs no change to any scraper write path beyond the enqueue line.

**Per-listing pass** (target p95 < 60 s from enqueue): build/refresh `listing_fp` + `image_band` (delete-then-insert by `listing_id`, idempotent) → probe K1–K6 → bulk-fetch the block → features → rule floor → model → zone. Band pairs go to `autodedup.judge_queue`; the worker would drain them **synchronously** with a pre-flight budget check (the 24 h batch window plus GitHub cron throttle — a `*/30` lane has measured 80–256 minute gaps — would make "realtime" a fiction), while a dispatched Actions lane uses Batch at half price.

**Mutual exclusion:** lease-row CAS (`location_data/resolver/lease.py` pattern), **never** `pg_advisory_lock` — session locks strand over the transaction pooler. The same lease lets the worker and a dispatched Actions run share the code without overlap.

**Idempotency.** `listing_fp` keyed on `listing_id`; `pairs` on `(listing_lo, listing_hi)` with `lo < hi` CHECK; `image_band` on `(band_no, band_val, listing_id)`. All writes `ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE`. Re-running `resolve()` on the same listing, or re-running a whole generation, is safe. Both sides are additionally resolved through `resolve_active_property_ids` (`toolkit/property_identity.py`, which follows `merged_into`) before a pair is proposed: if they already share an active property the decision is a recorded **no-op** and never reaches `/autodedup/groups` — in shadow mode that is what keeps already-merged pairs out of the operator's review queue.

**The merge write — Appendix A material, never executed in this program.** Survivor = the property of the listing with the oldest `first_seen_at`, matching `api/property_merge.py`'s convention.

```python
group = str(uuid.uuid4())
store.record_intent(conn, lo, hi, group)          # E40: recorded BEFORE the call
res = merge_properties(conn, survivor_id=surv, retired_id=ret,
        reason=f"autodedup:{model_version}:{lo}-{hi}", source="auto",
        confidence=score, merge_group_id=group,
        markers={"engine": "autodedup", "generation": GENERATION,
                 "model_version": MV, "feature_version": FV,
                 "cluster_key": ck, "families": fams,
                 "gap_days": gap, "judge_llm_call_id": cid})
store.stamp_generation(conn, group)               # write-only touch of property_merge_events
store.enqueue_dirty(conn, surv)
```

`merge_properties` takes `merge_group_id` as a real keyword (verified), re-enforces E2/E3 at the chokepoint and raises `MergeError` — the last rail, never bypassed, with the policy at E41. Note that `recompute_one`, `recompute_mf_one` and `sync_browse_list` run **inline per merge**, which is why a bulk revert is a bulk read-model rebuild of unmeasured duration — the reason the rehearsal is gated as Appendix A5 rather than run here.

**Kill switches, escalating:** `app_settings.autodedup_apply_enabled=false` (score and store, merge nothing — **the whole program's mode**, E39/D4, and since W30 the one key the apply path reads before every group, E304; the `autodedup_write_enabled` this ladder once named was never built) → `autodedup_enabled` interval 0 (lane dark) → the merges-per-hour latch (E42, not built) → the operator-error-rate latch that a future write path would add (§9's feedback loop: operator verdicts marking applied merges wrong flip `autodedup_apply_enabled` false above a rolling error rate the rollout decision sets). The first of these is the whole program's mode; the rest are Appendix A material.

**Unmerge safety.** `scripts/autodedup_revert.py --generation <g> | --since <ts> | --merge-group <id>` walks `autodedup.merges` newest-first and calls `unmerge_group`. Because every autonomous group has exactly one child, the revert is linear and conflict-free. **It is never exercised in this program** — no merge is applied, so none can be reverted (D4). The rehearsal that would time it is Appendix A5.

---

## 11a. Q10b — The real-time SHADOW lane, built (W9), hardened (W9b), scoped (W9c), railed (W9d), costed (W9e)

§11 designed a worker lane and left it unbuilt. W9 built the path itself — **the same decisions, made one arrival at a time** — as a dark, $0, shadow-only lane, and proved by replay that it reaches the cohort pass's state. W9b is what the verification of that build owed: the replay exercised an in-memory twin, and **every one of the four blocking defects lived in the SQL adapter it never touched** (the fingerprint row was never written, so the dirty set was always empty and every pass reported green over zero work; the certificate and the evidence families were dropped on read-back, so a component clustered in a different edge order than the cohort pass; the families bitmask was written as a count; E64's rail queried with an empty id array) — and a fifth, which CI's schema gate found while W9b was closing them: **the lane's fact SQL was hand-written and thinner than the export's**, so production would have decided on listings with no attributes, no price history and no broker identity at all. Nothing here merges, nothing writes outside schema `autodedup`, and nothing calls a model (D19). What it changes about §11 is the mechanism, not the posture: §11's `realtime_worker` tuple stays the eventual home, and the workflow below is the form that can run today without touching the production image.

### What a pass does

One pass is the cohort pass restricted to a working set, in the cohort pass's own order.

1. **Claim (E73, E79).** SIX bounded feeds, all index-served, all read-only and all restricted to `rt_scope`: **new** rows off `listings_pkey`, **content changes** off `listing_snapshots_pkey` (rule #2 makes that table an append-on-content-change feed, so it needs no column and no trigger of its own), **activity flips** off the partial `listings_inactive_at_idx`, and a round-robin sweep for **revivals** — `touch_listings` un-delists in place (`is_active = true, inactive_at = null`, same id, no snapshot), so no forward cursor can ever see one. Each feed ignores rows younger than a settle lag, the flip feed pages on `(inactive_at, id)` rather than on the stamp alone, and the new-row feed carries an anti-join straggler sweep. All three of those are defects W9 shipped and W9b closed, and each is measured rather than argued (M75, M76). W9c adds the **fifth** feed and the rule that governs all of them: a round-robin sweep of this generation's own fingerprint rows against the scope, which is the only way a listing the geocoder MOVED out of the scope is ever noticed — it touches no cursor. Each forward feed reads a bounded WINDOW off its own index, resolves the scope for that window through `listing_location_pkey`, and **advances to the window's end even when nothing in it was in scope**; the scope holds 0.6% of the corpus, so a feed that advanced only over survivors would never arrive. W9d adds the **sixth**, which is the fifth one's mirror image: `public.listing_location` is written asynchronously from the scrape, so an in-scope listing can carry no `obec_kod` at all when its arrival window passes — measured live, **6.2% of the listings first seen in the last 6 h**, against a 4.2–7.1% steady state — and nothing in W9c's five feeds would ever have claimed it once its location resolved. The entrant sweep round-robins the SCOPE's own listing ids, **one block a pass**, and hands over the ones this generation has no fingerprint row for.
2. **Refresh.** Rebuild the fingerprint from the facts, compare it with the stored digest, and where it moved rewrite the probe postings and the `rt_fp` row. An unchanged listing costs a digest comparison and stops there — that is the lane's idempotence, and a test asserts a second drain over an unchanged corpus scores 0 pairs and deletes 0, **through the SQL store as well as through the twin**.
3. **Widen (E71).** Add every listing that probes a changed key.
4. **Retrieve.** The same six probes, in `PROBE_PRIORITY` order, filling the same cap, refusing the same guards before the cap spends a slot (E17). Not a restatement: a test asserts the SQL-shaped retrieval equals `BlockIndex.candidates` for every listing of a cohort, and the key derivation IS `BlockIndex.index_keys` with the frozen calibration injected.
5. **Decide.** `pair_features` and `decide_pair`, unmodified: certificates (K-R, K-B, K-C), E45's unit-evidence gate, E46/E47's developer guards, E61's veto, E48's per-stratum cut, the model score, E63's promotion. Only pairs that are new, or whose either side's digest moved, are re-scored. **Before the first write**, the pass checks that the whole wanted pair set fits its budget — **150,000**, 3.2× the densest pass measured over g6 rather than a round number — and REFUSES the claim if it does not (E75) — a truncated pair set re-clusters from a partial edge set and the watermark then steps over the difference for ever (W9's verification measured the truncating build at a cap of 500 on g6: 13,127 of 48,908 pairs written and 38 member sets that the cohort pass never produces).
6. **Cluster (E72).** Re-cluster the touched components with the constrained union-find, the must-not-link set (operator rows plus this generation's E61 vetoes) and E57's bridge pass. Edges are read back WITH their certificate and their families, because E33 orders them certificate-first.
7. **Rail (E64).** Re-open to the band any stamped E63 promotion the live census has overtaken, capped per block, certificates exempt — over the blocks this pass touched, which under a frozen calibration are the only blocks whose census can have moved.

**Why the probes are a side table.** Each of the six keys on a DERIVED value — a 16-bit band of the description SimHash, a 16-bit band of an image pHash, `floor(ln(area)/w)`, the cohort price decile, a grain-prefixed obec/část code, a provenance-tagged house number. None is a column of `public.listings`, so **no index over there could serve one however it were written**, and ruling D8 forbids adding one to a shared hot table anyway. `autodedup.fp_key` (migration 539) is therefore the design and not a workaround: written only by this lane, keyed by generation — because a probe key is a function of the frozen calibration, so two calibrations must never read each other's postings — and every lookup is its own primary key. `autodedup.rt_fp` is its companion, and deliberately NOT migration 528's `listing_fp`: that table is keyed on `listing_id` alone, so it cannot hold two generations, and no lane has ever written it.

### The scope, and what the store is allowed to cost (W9c, E79)

The lane as W9b built it was correct and unbounded: its feeds were the whole corpus's, so a shadow run would have added **~3.4 GB a month** to a schema that is **148 MB** today. W9c makes the working set a required setting.

- **`rt_scope`** is a list of `{grain, code}` over `obec` (`listing_location.obec_kod`) and `cast_obce` (`cast_obce_kod`). It defaults to the three trial blocks — Jablonec **563510**, Turnov **577626**, Praha-Vysočany **490245** — derived from `cohort.BLOCKS` rather than retyped. The assembled Praha negative control is NOT in it: that block is a rule evaluated over address groups at export time, it has no arrival feed, and nothing in the corpus could tell the lane a new listing belongs to it. An empty or missing scope is a **hard error**; `all` is a scope only when `rt_max_schema_mb` could hold what `all` costs.
- **The predicate is index-served without an index of its own.** `listing_location` carries a btree on `(obec_kod, granularity)` and none at all on `cast_obce_kod`, and D8 forbids DDL on a shared table — so the scope is never the driving side of a feed. `EXPLAIN` on the live statements: `Index Scan using listings_pkey` (and `listing_snapshots_pkey`, and `listings_inactive_at_idx` with an incremental sort) for the window, then a `Nested Loop Left Join` of one `Index Scan using listing_location_pkey` per window row. At the shipped window of 1,000 a feed a pass costs a few thousand index probes, against a corpus arrival rate of 161 a pass.
- **The storage guard** reads `pg_total_relation_size` over schema `autodedup` BEFORE the pass writes anything and refuses over `rt_max_schema_mb` (default **400 MB**) — non-zero, cursor unmoved, nothing written. Every summary carries schema MB, rt rows by table and the growth since the previous pass.
- **Retention is the batch lane's own rule**, mirrored rather than invented: `score_lane.storable` keeps the whole merge and band zones plus the reject tail at or above `store_floor` (0.02 at w8). On the scoped cohort that is **13,043 of 44,724** decisions — the store grows with duplicates, not with comparisons — and a pair that falls below the floor on a re-score is deleted rather than left behind.

Measured, not projected (live SELECTs, 30 days to 2026-09-20; the full arithmetic is in `/home/hejtm/autodedup-artifacts/w9c/projection.json`):

| | the three trial blocks | whole corpus |
|---|---|---|
| listings | 4,969 | 872,604 |
| new listings / 30 d | **1,097** (36.6 a day) | 178,190 (5,940 a day) |
| content changes / 30 d | 1,600 | 334,237 |
| activity flips / 30 d | 1,099 | 184,590 |
| arrivals per 10-minute pass | **0.88** | 161 |
| `rt_fp` + `fp_key` + pair rows a day | 37 + 644 + 195 | 5,940 + 104,517 + 31,720 |
| store growth | **0.70 MB a day, 21 MB / 30 d** | 114 MB a day, **3,420 MB / 30 d** |
| growth WITHOUT the retention rule | 62 MB / 30 d | 10.1 GB / 30 d |
| one-off seed backfill | **56 MB** | ~9.6 GB |

### What the scope OWED, and the four rails W9d added

W9c's verification took the scope apart adversarially and found four defects. None of them is a
decision defect — the engine's numbers are unchanged, and the scoped replay below is byte-for-byte
the one W9c ran — but two of them would have emptied the store and two would have let it drift.

- **W9d-1 — a separator-only scope was a NULL scope, and the drift sweep then retired the whole
  store.** `rt_scope` hand-written as `","` (or `" , , "`) parsed to `Scope(blocks=(),
  whole_corpus=False)` with no error: not a whole-corpus run — W9c's rail held in the direction it
  was pointed — but a predicate false for every row, so the four forward feeds claimed nothing and
  the fifth reported **every** `rt_fp` row of the generation as departed. Reproduced: a 100-row
  store, 10 retired on the first claim, the drift cursor wrapping, a 4,887-row store gone in ~49
  passes (~8 h), recoverable only by a re-seed. Two rails now, deliberately independent of each
  other: an empty scope is **unconstructible** (`Scope.__post_init__`, and `parse_scope` refuses a
  token string or a list that names no block), and the drift sweep **refuses on its own** whatever
  the parse did — nothing at all under an empty scope, and nothing when it would retire more than
  `rt_max_retire_fraction` (data, default **0.05**) of the generation's rows in one pass window.
  Over the rail the lane STOPS: non-zero, transaction rolled back, cursor unmoved, the message
  naming `autodedup.settings.rt_scope`. A geocode correction moves a listing or two; only a scope
  that has gone wrong moves a twentieth of the store at once.
- **W9d-2 — a dispatch-arg scope was destructive and not persisted.** `resolve_scope` preferred
  the argument, so one `args=rt_scope=obec:563510` retired every listing of the other two blocks;
  the argument was never written back, so the next scheduled pass read the wider row again — and
  nothing re-adds a retired listing. The PERSISTED row is now the generation's scope: a differing
  argument is **refused** unless `rt_rescope=true`, and a rescope is written back (inside the
  pass's transaction) with the entrant cursor reset to 0, so everything the new scope holds is
  re-claimed rather than merely un-retired. A seeded generation with **no** `rt_scope` row is a
  hard error — falling back to the default was the same destruction with no argument at all.
- **W9d-3 — nothing watched the scope's IN direction, and the straggler look-back was an id
  RANGE.** The entrant feed above is the first half; the second is that `RT_NEW_STRAGGLERS_SQL`
  looked back `after_id - 5000` **id units**, which is 1,482 rows near the head of the corpus and
  ~230 in sparser id space, against a constant whose comment always meant 5,000 **rows**. It is
  now `order by id desc limit N` off `listings_pkey` — a backwards index scan, honestly N rows.
  There is no index-served change stamp to drive the entrant feed off instead: `listing_location`
  carries `resolved_at` with **no index at all** and D8 forbids adding one, so the sweep is served
  by the indexes this schema HAS — `listing_location_obec_granularity` for a town block and, for a
  QUARTER, the narrowest path that exists at all: its **parent obec's** index entries filtered on
  `cast_obce_kod`. `EXPLAIN ANALYZE` on the SHIPPED statement at its shipped slice of 20,000, live:
  Jablonec is a Bitmap Index Scan of **3,115 index rows → 2,780 rows in 57 ms**, and
  Praha-Vysočany **159,353 index rows → 1,439 rows in 5.7 s** (29,265 heap blocks, cold) — which
  is exactly why the sweep walks ONE block a pass rather than the scope, and why its cost is a
  third of a pass's rather than every pass's, well inside the 120 s statement guard. The parent is a registry
  fact, read once a pass from `ruian_admin_units` through `(level, code)` + pkey (`EXPLAIN`: two
  Index Scans, **4.7 ms**; 490245 → 554782), and a quarter the register cannot place is a hard
  error at seed and at pass time rather than a block the sweep silently never walks.
- **W9d-4 — the session guards were set outside the pass's transaction.** `db.connect` speaks to
  Supabase's transaction-mode pooler, which rebinds a connection between queries (the reason
  `prepare_threshold` is disabled), and `statement_timeout` / `idle_in_transaction_session_timeout`
  were issued with `is_local = false` on the autocommit connection BEFORE the transaction opened —
  so the runaway they exist to bound could run on a backend that never saw them, and an unrelated
  consumer could inherit a 120 s `statement_timeout`. All three (`lock_timeout` added) are now the
  transaction's first statements with `is_local = true`, and the Postgres fake asserts it saw them
  **inside** the transaction rather than that they were issued at all.

**And the projection's METHOD line is withdrawn** (the numbers are not). W9c stated its per-row
costs as "3.53× the `pg_column_size` of a representative tuple" and could not reproduce two of its
own numbers from it — because that multiplier is not a constant: it is `autodedup.cluster_members`'
measured **236.8 B a row** (1,695,744 B / 7,162 rows) divided by whichever tuple is called
representative (51 B → 4.64×, 67 B → 3.53×). Neither `fp_key` nor `rt_fp` exists yet — 539 is
unapplied — so nothing about them is measurable, and the honest basis is the measured cost of the
nearest table that does exist, carried as a band: **237 B** a `fp_key` row, **237–669 B** an `rt_fp`
row, **2,869 B** a pair row (measured directly). Re-done on that basis the table below moves by
about a percent — **0.69–0.70 MB a day** under the scope, **112–114 MB a day** corpus-wide
(3.3 GB / 30 d), **9.8–10.2 GB** to seed the corpus — so `CORPUS_PROJECTION_MB = 17,000` is still
that seed plus about two months of its arrivals
(`/home/hejtm/autodedup-artifacts/w9d/projection_correction.json`).

### What the rails OWED, and what W9e measured (E80, E81, E82)

The re-verification of W9d (`/home/hejtm/autodedup-artifacts/w9d/reverify.json`) could not refute
the four rails and found six things beside them — one that made the documented enable path a
no-op, one ops cost the artifact understated by an order of magnitude, and four smaller ones.
All six are closed here, each reproduced as a defect first
(`tests/autodedup/test_incremental_rails.py`).

- **R1 — the documented seed was a silent no-op (E80).** `rt_seed` was gated on
  `AUTODEDUP_REALTIME_ENABLED` and `.github/workflows/autodedup.yml` never passed that variable
  to the lane, so `gh workflow run autodedup.yml -f mode=rt_seed …` — the recipe printed in this
  document and in the workflow header — exited 0 having seeded nothing; flip the variable after
  it and the `*/10` schedule hard-errors on the missing calibration every ten minutes. The seed
  does not consult the switch at all now (seeding is the step BEFORE it), it takes
  `export_run=<id>` and downloads the cohort artifact the way the score and judge lanes do
  (a runner holds no cohort file otherwise), a second seed of a seeded generation is refused
  unless `reseed=true`, and a pass over an unseeded generation is a green `skipped: unseeded`
  with nothing written.
- **R3 — the entrant sweep was 12.1 GB a day of cold reads (E81), and this is the important
  one.** Measured live, per walk, with `EXPLAIN (ANALYZE, BUFFERS)` on the shipped statement:

  | block | plan | rows | buffers | MB | time |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | `obec:563510` Jablonec | Bitmap Index Scan `(obec_kod, granularity)` → Bitmap Heap Scan | 2,780 | 2,745 (2,734 heap blocks) | 21.4 | 61 ms |
  | `obec:577626` Turnov | Index Scan `(obec_kod, granularity)` | 751 | 845 | 6.6 | 402 ms |
  | `cast_obce:490245` Praha-Vysočany, via parent obec 554782 | Bitmap Heap Scan, `Filter: cast_obce_kod`, **140,107 rows removed** | 1,439 | 29,568 (29,265 heap blocks) | **231** | **6.4 s** |

  W9d walked one block a pass, so at the `*/10` cadence each block was walked ~48 times a day:
  **1,591,584 blocks = 12,434 MB (12.1 GB) a day**, of which the quarter alone is 10.8 GB, whether or not
  anything had entered — on the instance that serves Browse. There is no narrower path (checked
  live: no index on `cast_obce_kod`, none on any obec/quarter column of `public.listings`, and
  D8 forbids adding one), and `enter_slice` bounds none of it because the `limit` is applied
  after the bitmap. So the walk became a cadence-driven REFRESH of `autodedup.rt_scope_ids` and
  an ordinary pass claims out of that snapshot inside schema `autodedup`:
  **zero blocks of `public` for this feed**, and the scan bill is what the cadence asks for —
  4 quarter walks + 24 + 24 town walks a day = **52 walks, 204,432 blocks, 1,597 MB a day:
  a 7.8× cut**, capped at `rt_enter_max_scans_per_day` (60) which the lane refuses to exceed.
- **R2 — the retire rail could not fire above ~400,000 rows (E82).** It compared ONE drift
  slice's departed count with a fraction of the whole store. Retirement is now counted over a
  rolling day (`autodedup.rt_retire_event`, written inside the pass's transaction) against the
  store as it stood when that window opened, independent of `drift_slice`.
- **R4 — `rt_scope` was one global row (E82).** It is `rt_scope:<generation>`; the legacy global
  row is read once, for generation `rt` alone.
- **R5 — the entrant feed had no settle lag (E81).** It honours the same lag as the others, on
  the snapshot's `resolved_at` — the resolution IS this feed's arrival event.
- **R6 — a rail was a dispatch argument (E82).** `rt_max_retire_fraction`, the scan cap, the
  cadence and the storage budget are settings rows; on a dispatch they are accepted only beside
  `rt_rescope=true`, and a non-numeric value stops the lane instead of reverting to a default.

### Replay equivalence — the proof, re-run

The cohort of export run `35200225251` (5,687 listings) fed through the incremental path in `first_seen_at` order over 29 passes, at w8.json settings and the `w6_gold` model, against the cohort pass in the same process (`python3 -m autodedup.replay`), now at the **shipped** pair budget rather than an unreachable one:

| | cohort pass | incremental |
|---|---|---|
| pairs decided | 48,908 | **48,908** — 0 on either side alone |
| pair decisions differing (zone, score, reason, certificate, veto, families, probes) | — | **0** |
| clusters | 939 | **939**, cluster keys AND member sets identical |
| arrival order shuffled within each calendar day (seed 7) | — | **identical to the ordered replay** on both grains |
| LLM spend | $0 | **$0** |
| pair-budget refusals | — | **0** at the shipped 150,000 |

Four adversarial schedules over the same cohort — full reverse order, the largest cluster's bridge listing (`15422290`, in an 8-member group) moved first, moved last, and a delist-then-relist of it — reach the same state on both grains, every one of them 48,908 pairs and 939 clusters with 0 differing (M77). The delisting arm is the one worth reading: while the bridge is inactive the corpus clusters into **940** groups, and when it is revived the lane returns to the cohort pass's **939**, member sets and keys identical. And the store itself is now proved, not assumed: the same cohort replayed through `incremental_lane.SqlStore` against a Postgres fake reaches the same pairs and the same clusters as the twin, certificate and evidence families included (M75).

**And the same proof re-run on the SCOPED lane (W9c).** The comparison set is the cohort minus the negative-control block — **4,887 of the cohort's 5,687 listings**, the three trial blocks, and the SAME set on both sides, the batch pass included, because a scoped lane that was compared with an unscoped cohort pass would be measuring the scope rather than the mechanism:

| | cohort pass (scoped) | incremental (scoped) |
|---|---|---|
| listings | 4,887 | 4,887 |
| pairs decided | 44,724 | **44,724** — 0 on either side alone |
| pair decisions differing | — | **0** |
| clusters | 849 | **849**, cluster keys AND member sets identical |
| pairs at the STORED grain (`storable`, E79) | 13,043 | **13,043**, 0 differing |
| pair-budget refusals · LLM spend | — | **0** · **$0** |

(`/home/hejtm/autodedup-artifacts/w9c/replay_scoped/replay.json`, `python3 -m autodedup.replay --scope "obec:563510 obec:577626 cast_obce:490245"`.) The stored grain is proved beside the decided one on purpose: retention is a rule about what the STORE keeps, so comparing only decisions would have left it unproved.

**Re-run unchanged after W9d's rails** (`/home/hejtm/autodedup-artifacts/w9d/replay_scoped/replay.json`, same command, same artifact, same `w8.json` / `w6_gold` / calibration digest `0df04dcdefc787fb`): **4,887 listings, 44,724 pairs on both sides with 0 on either side alone and 0 differing, 849 clusters with keys and member sets identical, 13,043 stored-grain pairs on both sides with 0 differing, 0 pair-budget refusals, $0**, over 25 passes in 96.7 s (50.5 listings/s, 60,846 fact reads, 96 pairs created and later dropped — E71's cap churn). That is the point of re-running it: W9d changed WHICH listings a pass claims and WHEN it refuses, and the decision grain must not move an inch under that.

**And re-run again, unchanged, after W9e's rails** (`/home/hejtm/autodedup-artifacts/w9e/replay_scoped/replay.json`, same command, same artifact, same `w8.json` / `w6_gold` / calibration digest `0df04dcdefc787fb`): **4,887 listings, 44,724 pairs on both sides with 0 on either side alone and 0 differing, 849 clusters with keys and member sets identical, 13,043 stored-grain pairs with 0 differing, 0 pair-budget refusals, $0**, over 25 passes in 97.3 s. W9e moved a feed's SOURCE (a snapshot inside `autodedup` instead of a scan of `public`), its cadence and three rails; the decision grain is where it was.

The pair grain is identical *by construction* once E70 freezes the calibration — a decision is then a pure function of the pair — so what the replay actually tests is retrieval, bookkeeping and the store; what W9 found there was the fan-out cap and a live pHash population, and what W9b found was four blockers in the adapter.

### What it costs

Measured on the live corpus (872,506 listings) over the last 7 days rather than projected: **5,701 new + 11,570 content changes + 6,547 activity flips a day = 23,818 arrivals, ≈165 per 10-minute pass**, against a claim of 500 (125 a feed) — the lane keeps up with ~3.4× headroom. Engine throughput is **47.1 listings/s** single-core at a 200-listing claim and **73.8 at the shipped 500**, and the fact reads fall with the claim size the same way — **6.65 a listing at 500 against 14.3 at 200 and 43.5 at 50**, because a bigger claim amortises the neighbourhood over more arrivals. A 165-arrival pass is therefore a couple of seconds of CPU and ~1,100 listing fetches, and the shipped claim of 500 is the cheap end of that curve rather than the expensive one. The database bill is what W9 did not price and W9b bounded: **a pass is batched to the pass's grain (E74), so the ~17,700 statements a 166-listing claim cost before W9b are now a few dozen — one `unnest`-joined lookup for a whole working set's probe keys, one posting delete, one posting insert, one fingerprint read, `executemany` for the pair rows and one flush of the census cells**. LLM spend is **$0** and structurally so: D19 gives no judge merge authority on the band and this lane calls no provider.

Storage, measured then projected — and W9c is what turned this paragraph from an observation into a budget (E79): **17.6 index keys per listing**, so `autodedup.fp_key` would be **~15.4 M rows ≈ 3.1 GB** at corpus scale — it subsumes §10's `image_band` line and adds the text bands — plus one `rt_fp` row per listing. The frozen calibration is **1.31 MB of JSON for 5,687 listings**, dominated by the token frequency tables; at corpus scale those grow sub-linearly (Heaps) to an estimated tens of MB, which is why `rt_calibration` carries `artifact_url` beside `payload` and the lane writes whichever fits.

### The switches, and how the shadow schedule is turned on

Dark at **three** levels, deliberately not one.

1. `.github/workflows/autodedup_realtime.yml` runs `*/10` and its first step exits **before checkout** unless the repository variable `AUTODEDUP_REALTIME_ENABLED` is exactly `true`.
2. `autodedup/incremental_lane.py` re-reads the same variable, so a manual dispatch cannot bypass it.
3. `autodedup.settings.realtime_enabled = false` stops a lane that is already on, **without a repository change or a deploy** — the operator's own stop button.

Beneath all three, shadow mode's posture is untouched: no `public.listings` row, no `property_id`, no merge. Two passes cannot overlap — the lane takes a lease row in `autodedup.rt_lease` by CAS (**never `pg_advisory_lock`**, which strands over the transaction pooler), its TTL outlives the job timeout, and a run that cannot take it exits green as `skipped: leased`. The workflow has **its own concurrency group**, so a long cohort pass can never evict a real-time pass and a real-time pass can never evict a paid judge run.

**Turning it on is five steps, in this order (E80).** Nothing in a merge starts this lane: the schedule exists in `main` and stays dark until the repository variable is flipped, which is a deliberate act the parent session performs AFTER the merge, in **shadow only** — no merge, no `public.listings` write, no `property_id`. Reversing it is the same flip the other way. W9e is what made this sequence real rather than documented: step 3 used to exit 0 having done nothing, because the seed was gated on the switch step 5 flips.

1. **Apply** migration `539_autodedup_realtime_lane.sql` (additive; `fp_key`, `rt_fp`, `rt_calibration`, `rt_block_cell`, `rt_lease`, `rt_scope_ids`, `rt_scope_scan`, `rt_retire_event` plus the pair-grain columns). Until it is applied the lane exits non-zero rather than running on a guess.
2. **Merge** the branch. The schedule lands dark; a pass over an unseeded generation exits 0 as `skipped: unseeded` (E80), so nothing is red between this step and the next.
3. **Seed the generation — while the lane is still dark:**

   ```
   gh workflow run autodedup.yml --ref main -f mode=rt_seed \
     -f "args=export_run=35200225251,backfill=true,rt_scope=obec:563510 obec:577626 cast_obce:490245"
   ```

   `35200225251` is the export run whose 5,687-listing cohort the batch pass scored; the seed downloads that artifact, keeps the **4,887** listings of it the scope holds, cuts the frozen calibration (E70) over exactly those, writes it to `rt_calibration`, starts the six cursors **at today** (without which the lane would walk the corpus from id 0 — E76), backfills those listings' fingerprints and postings (~56 MB) and writes the scope to `autodedup.settings` under `rt_scope:rt` (E82). The `rt_scope` above is the default spelled out; leave it off for the same three trial blocks, and pass `rt_max_schema_mb=<n>` to move the 400 MB budget. A second seed of a seeded generation is refused unless `reseed=true`.
4. **Verify** before flipping anything: `out/rt_seed.json` carries `calibration_digest`, `in_scope_listings` (4,887), `backfilled` and `scope`; in the database `select key from autodedup.settings where key like 'rt_scope%'` shows `rt_scope:rt`, and `autodedup.rt_calibration` holds one row for generation `rt`.
5. **Flip the repository variable:** `gh variable set AUTODEDUP_REALTIME_ENABLED --body true`. The `*/10` schedule then runs in **shadow only**. To stop it without touching the repository: `update autodedup.settings set value = 'false' where key = 'realtime_enabled'`.

### W9g — the first live pass was wrong, and why (E90, E90a, M150–M152)

The lane was seeded for the trial scope (obec 563510, obec 577626, cast_obce 490245; 4,887 listings; calibration `0df04dcdefc787fb`) and its first live pass ran: 77 claimed, 2,825 dirty, **36,940 pairs scored, 15,923 retained, 703 clusters, 876 s, $0**. It issued **1,024 K-B, 75 K-R and ZERO K-C** where the batch generation `g6` over the same cohort issued **1,771 K-C**, and 5,888 of the 7,878 pairs both generations hold moved in score. The schedule was switched off.

`--mode rt_parity` is the instrument that can see it (E90): read-only, writing nothing at all, it builds a sample of listings twice — once from the export artifact, once through `SqlFacts` — diffs them field by field, and re-scores the same pairs from both fact sets under ONE settings, model and calibration. What it found, on 200 listings, 2,847 images and 400 pairs:

- **The listing half of E77's fix holds.** Every listing-field difference is drift since the export, and the three that carry a price are all on listings whose snapshot stream says they changed.
- **`pop` is 0 on 99.5 % of images** (artifact ≥ 1), because `autodedup.phash_pop` — the table E70 freezes the corpus-wide pHash population into and the lane joins every image against — **holds 0 rows and has no writer anywhere in the repository**. That makes `pop_is_measured()` false for every gallery, `catalog_ratio` None for every listing, `catalog_ratio_max` ABSENT on every pair, and K-C structurally unreachable while K-B, which never reads it, goes on firing. In the sample: **3 of 3 K-C lost, 3 of 3 K-B kept.** E9's catalogue subtraction is disarmed with it (`n_images_min` moves by 2.22 images a pair) and so is `shared_image_population`, the hazard limb E64's rail reads.
- **The scores are a different defect.** With the facts alone moving, the score passes 0.01 on 5 of 400 pairs. The generation was seeded and run with empty `args`, so `Settings()` and the uncalibrated `hand_initialised()` prior scored it against g6's `w8` and `w6_gold` — five knobs apart including both thresholds, and `autodedup.pairs.model_version` is NULL on all 15,923 rt rows (E90a).
- **557 producer differences** over `phash`, `clip` and `tags`: the pHash and CLIP jobs re-ran after the export, so the corpus's derived signals move under a frozen calibration. That is not a lane defect, but it is the reason the instrument classes an image field by what it IS — a stored gallery fact, producer output, or the frozen statistic.
- **Re-clustering cost 682 s of the 876 s** over 700 components at ~10 statements each, because the store was empty and every merge edge was new. It is a first-pass cost; the shape that produces it — one serial round trip per component — is not, and returns on any pass that moves the merge graph at scale (M152).

### W9g — the fix: the lane reads the export's facts, and a gate says so every pass (E91)

**The frozen population has a writer, and it is the seed.** The number the live lane needs is the one the export already measured (`count(DISTINCT listing_id)` over `public.images`, which cannot answer less than 1 for a hash that exists), and every image record in the artifact carries it — so `rt_seed` copies it into `autodedup.phash_pop` instead of recounting it. Zero blocks of `public` at seed time, zero at pass time, and the lane joins against exactly the statistic the batch engine scored with. An artifact whose population probe timed out (`phash_pop_ok=false`) is refused rather than frozen into a generation where no K-C can ever fire. A hash the table does not carry is **unknown**, never 0: there is no index on `images.phash` (`images_phash_idx` is on `sreality_id`), D8 forbids adding one and no in-schema mirror exists, so a hash that arrived after the export stays unmeasured until the next export and re-seed — which costs that listing its `catalog_ratio` (conservative) rather than asserting its photo is unique, and every pass summary reports `population.images_unmeasured` so the drift is a number the operator can watch.

On the trial cohort that is **41,791 distinct hashes over 80,394 images — and 22,543 of them carry a population of exactly 1**, which is the half migration 528's prose ("materialized ONLY for hashes seen on >= 3 distinct listings") would have excluded and precisely the half that makes `pop_is_measured()` true. A table built to that comment could never have agreed with the export.

**Parity is a gate, not an instrument.** The seed cuts four digests per sampled listing — `stored`, `sighting`, `producer`, `population` (E91) — into `autodedup.settings` under `rt_parity_baseline:<generation>`, beside the calibration and at the same moment. `rt_seed` refuses to seed if the live facts already disagree; every scheduled pass re-checks a fresh 25-listing slice of the baseline BEFORE it opens its transaction and refuses on a single breach: non-zero, nothing written, no cursor moved. Producer movement (a re-run pHash or CLIP job) is reported, never refused; so is the SIGHTING clock — rule #4 bumps `last_seen_at` on every index sighting and rule #3 flips `is_active`, neither appending a snapshot, and live **78 of 200 sampled listings carried a newer `last_seen_at` with 74 of those showing no newer snapshot**, so a gate that read that clock as a stored fact would refuse every re-seed of a corpus still being scraped. A listing whose content changed since the export is drift, counted and skipped. The pass summary carries the numbers — `parity.checked`, `producer_moved`, `sighting_moved`, `drifted_skipped`, `breaches` — so a gate that is checking nothing is visible in every run.

**The generation carries its own scorer (E90a).** `rt_seed` now REQUIRES `settings=` and `model=` as NAMES resolved inside the repo (`autodedup/settings/w8.json`, `autodedup/models/w6_gold.json`) — the resolution the score lane always had and this lane never did, which is why `settings=w8` named no readable file and the shipped recipe passed nothing at all. `default`/`prior` are the two words for the uncalibrated ones, so choosing them is a choice a dispatch can be read to have made. A pass loads the settings and the model from `rt_calibration`, refuses a dispatch argument that names a different one (changing a generation's scorer is a re-seed), and `SqlStore` stamps `model_version` and `calibration_digest` on every pair row it writes.

**The re-cluster is spelled per PASS (E74).** `_components` walks the whole seed set together — one `merge_neighbours` per BFS level rather than one per level per seed, with a union-find that stops a component growing once it passes the cap — and `_recluster` batches the fact read, `pairs_within`, `clusters_touching` and the writes across every component of the pass. A stale cluster is now also dropped once by the whole pass rather than by whichever component ran last, which is a correctness fix hiding inside a cost one.

**What the fix measures live (runs `35524769272` and `35524925562`, $0).** `--mode rt_parity` grew one read-only argument for this: `population=artifact` makes the live side read the cohort's own counts — the exact rows `rt_seed` writes — so a seed's effect is measurable BEFORE anything is seeded. On the same 200 listings, 2,847 images and 400 pairs: **live certificates `{K-B 3, K-C 3}` against the artifact's `{K-B 3, K-C 3}`, `certificate_moves` empty where it was `K-C->-` 3, `score_differs_gt_0_01` 0 of 400 (was 6), no zone moves, `catalog_ratio_max` moved on 54 pairs instead of 387, and `image.pop` differs on 115 of 2,847 images instead of 2,832** — all 115 on images whose `phash` itself moved since the export, which is a hash the calibration never saw and is therefore unknown by design (M154).

**And the GATE itself, run against production as it stands (read-only, `rt_parity` reports what it would say):** against the live `autodedup.phash_pop` — still empty — it **REFUSES, 179 of 196 checked listings breaching on `population`** and every one of them "with an unmoved producer digest", which is the defect stated in the gate's own words. Against the population the seed will write: **0 breaches of 196 checked, `would_refuse: false`**, with 4 listings skipped as drift, 14 producer-moved and **76 sighting-moved** — the last number being why the scraper's clock is a digest of its own and not a stored fact (run `35525701436` frozen, `35525571949` artifact).

**Replay equivalence still holds exactly** — and it is equivalence it proves, not cost: the replay runs against `MemoryStore`, which has no round trip, so the statement A/B above is what prices the re-cluster and the replay is what says the batching changed no decision. On the same cohort and the same scorer (`w8` + `w6_gold`): 48,908 of 48,908 pair decisions identical, 15,811 of 15,811 stored rows identical, 939 of 939 clusters identical in key AND member set, 29 passes, 133.1 s, 0 budget refusals, $0 (`/home/hejtm/autodedup-artifacts/w9g/replay_after/replay.json`).

#### Re-seeding and re-enabling as W9g left it (SUPERSEDED by W9h's sequence below)

The generation `rt` in the store was seeded on the uncalibrated prior, holds a population table that was empty, and carries no parity baseline, so a pass on this build refuses it by design. It has to be re-cut, in this order:

1. **Re-seed** (works while the lane is dark; a re-seed re-cuts the calibration every stored decision was taken under, which is precisely what is wanted here):

   ```
   gh workflow run autodedup.yml --ref main -f mode=rt_seed \
     -f "args=export_run=35200225251,settings=w8,model=w6_gold,backfill=true,reseed=true,rt_scope=obec:563510 obec:577626 cast_obce:490245"
   ```

2. **Verify** `out/rt_seed.json`: `model_version` is `w6_gold`, `population.hashes_written` > 0, `parity.breaches` is 0 over `parity.checked` listings, `in_scope_listings` 4,887. In the database: `select count(*) from autodedup.phash_pop` is no longer 0, `autodedup.settings` carries `rt_parity_baseline:rt`, and `autodedup.rt_calibration` for `rt` carries `settings` and `model_version = 'w6_gold'`.
3. **Flip the variable:** `gh variable set AUTODEDUP_REALTIME_ENABLED --body true`.
4. **Read the first pass's summary**: `certificates` should carry K-C again, `parity.ok` is true, `population.images_unmeasured` is the count of photographs that arrived after the export, and `cluster_s` should be a handful of seconds rather than 682.

The old rows of generation `rt` are left where they are: they were decided by a scorer and on facts this build refuses, and the honest way to replace them is the re-seed above plus the passes that follow, not a rewrite of history.

### W9h — the lane decided ~80% of arrivals before their photographs were evidence (E92, E93, M115–M119)

W9g's gate says the live lane reads the export's facts. It says that about a listing the export **carried**. An arrival is a different question, and the answer was worse: **the lane decides it before its photographs exist as evidence, and then never looks again.**

The timeline is production's, measured: the `*/10` schedule plus a 300 s settle lag claims a new listing **5–15 minutes** after `first_seen_at`; its images are downloaded within about a minute (p50 0.9, p90 2.4); `compute_image_phash.yml` runs **hourly at :20** and `clip_tag.yml` **hourly at :40**, measured **p50 2.47 h and p90 4.51 h** from `first_seen_at` to a first tag. So **~80% of arrivals are claimed with every `phash` NULL**, only **22% ever carry a snapshot more than 2 h after arrival** — and **not one of the six feeds is keyed on a hash or a vector arriving.** A phash appends no snapshot (rule #2), flips no flag (rule #3) and moves no location, so nothing could claim the listing again. The probe that settled it: pass 1 claimed an arrival with NULL phash columns and wrote 9 pair rows and its index keys — `attr_area`, `attr_dispo`, `broker`, `text`, **no pHash posting**; the hash job then landed; pass 2 claimed 0, scored 0, and the stored keys still carried none. That listing is permanently unreachable by another listing's photo probe in that generation. The cost, measured on the artifact's own pairs by stripping the same evidence: **15 of 400 scores move past 0.01, 8 zone moves (4 merge→band, 4 reject→band) and every K-C certificate.**

**The fix is two halves, and one without the other is worse than neither.**

**(a) Re-decide when the evidence arrives (E92).** `rt_fp` now records what a decision rested on — `ev_images`, `ev_phash`, `ev_clip`, `ev_tags` and `first_decided_at` (migration **540**, additive, schema `autodedup` only, **not applied**) — and a **seventh feed** round-robins this generation's rows whose evidence is incomplete or whose first decision is inside `rt_evidence_horizon_hours` (**48 h**, data), probes `public.images` by listing id, and hands back every listing whose photographs no longer match. What it costs `public`, measured with `EXPLAIN (ANALYZE, BUFFERS)` on the shipped statement:

| | plan | rows | buffers | per listing | time |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| the shipped probe (200 listings) | `Index Scan using images_listing_id_idx` → `GroupAggregate` | 3,085 images | **2,484** (12 read warm, 1,911 cold) | **12.4** | 17 ms warm, 1.47 s cold |
| the same with the two CLIP joins instead | Bitmap Heap Scan + 2 index-only scans, 5,479 heap fetches | 3,085 | 34,419 | 172 | 173 ms |

The joins are avoided because **`images.clip_tagged_at` is the CLIP job's own stamp**, and on the trial scope's **73,208 images it agrees with `image_clip_embeddings` and `image_clip_tags` on every row** — 72,171 each, 0 disagreements in either direction (M116). At the trial scope the candidate set is **76 of 4,974 rows (1.5%)** incomplete in steady state plus ~73 inside the horizon at 36.6 arrivals a day, so a pass probes ~150 listings: **~1,860 buffers, 14.5 MB a pass, ~2.0 GB a day** at the `*/10` cadence — the same order as the entrant feed's 1,597 MB after W9e cut it 7.8×, and one sixth of what W9d's entrant sweep cost before it. The ceiling the slice sets is `rt_evidence_slice` (400) × 144 passes = **5.6 GB a day**, and `rt_evidence_slice=0` turns the feed off. The candidate arm and the release arm read `autodedup` only — **zero blocks of `public`**.

**(b) Do not act on evidence you do not have yet (E93).** Re-deciding alone would let a group form on nothing and be re-opened later, which is the wrong order. So while a side's gallery **exists and carries no hash at all**, inside the horizon, a merge that does not rest on **K-B** or **K-R** — the two certificates that read no photograph — is **HELD in the band**, reason `evidence_pending`, carrying `held_zone` / `held_reason` / `held_certificate` and certifying nothing. Rejects are never held. The release is either the evidence landing (the digest moves, the pair re-scores) or the horizon passing — and that second case moves no fact at all, which is why the sweep's second arm asks for held pairs **by reason** off a partial index rather than waiting for a digest.

**What this does to decision latency, stated plainly.** Every reject and every merge resting on K-B or K-R is decided **within the pass that claims the listing** — unchanged. A photo-dependent merge now waits for the hourly producers, so its arrival-to-merge time is their distribution: **p50 2.47 h, p90 4.51 h, hard-bounded at 48 h**, after which it is decided on what the lane has. That is a real cost and it is not this lane's to remove: the production change that would remove it is **hashing on download inside the image worker** (`scraper/`), which touches the production image and is the operator's decision under D26 — this program may name it and must not reach for it.

**Replay equivalence, still exact — and a new variant that is the point.** The hold is an argument rather than a setting, so a pass given none is W9's pass and the plain replay is untouched: same cohort, same `w8` + `w6_gold`, same calibration digest `0df04dcdefc787fb` — **4,887 listings, 44,724 pairs on both sides with 0 on either side alone and 0 differing, 849 clusters identical in key AND member set, 0 budget refusals, $0**, 25 passes in 108.4 s. The variant (`--withhold-photos`) strips `phash`, `pop`, `clip` and `tags` from every gallery at its first claim, delivers them afterwards, then expires the horizon: **19 merges were held on first decision** — 19 merges the live lane would have taken on photographs it had not processed — **all 19 released, 0 still held, and the final state equals the batch engine's at both grains: 44,724 of 44,724 pairs with 0 differing, 13,043 of 13,043 stored-grain rows with 0 differing, 849 clusters with keys and member sets identical** (75 passes, 524.8 s, $0; `/home/hejtm/autodedup-artifacts/w9h/replay_withheld/replay.json`, M118).

### W9h — the gate could pass on nothing, and the calibration had no expiry (E94, E95, E96)

**The gate (E94).** W9g's gate counted breaches, and a breach count is exactly what can be satisfied by comparing nothing. Two vacuities, both demonstrated against the Postgres fake: give every baseline listing a newer snapshot and every stored fact can then be broken with `checked` **0**, `breaches` **0**, `ok` true; delete every baseline listing and `absent` **120** passes the same way. Drift is monotone in export age — 4 of 200 at three days — so the gate **self-weakened as the export aged**. Now there are floors, all data, all refusing: `rt_parity_min_checked` (**15**) and `rt_parity_min_checked_share` (**0.6**) together, because one alone is defeated from either end (a baseline smaller than 15 could never meet the count; a 120-listing seed sample could meet it with 105 of its listings drifted or absent), with an empty baseline requiring at least one comparison. And `rt_parity_max_unknown_pop_share` (**0.15**) refuses when too much of what the pass read carries a hash the frozen population never measured. The **producer excuse is per IMAGE** now: the baseline carries `[image_id, phash, pop]` per image (~54 KB for 120 listings, still one settings row), a frame whose own hash moved is excused **and counted**, and every other frame of that listing is still checked — W9g excused the whole listing on one moved frame, so a population decaying image by image ran green.

**And the railed gate, run against production as it stands (read-only, `rt_parity` reports what it would say; run `35531784923`, $0).** Against the population the seed will write: **`would_refuse` false**, **196 of 200 checked** against a floor of `max(15, 0.6 x 200) = 120` (4 drifted, 0 absent), **0 breaches**, export age **3.4 days** against the 14-day rail, and an unknown-population share of **3.04%** against the 15% ceiling. The per-image excuse is what changed: **2,697 frames compared and 120 excused** as re-hashed, where W9g's gate skipped the whole population check for each of the **14** producer-moved listings. Every number a floor reads is now in the summary of every run.

**The staleness rail (E95).** Nothing refused on the frozen population's age or coverage, and the direction of that error is what makes it a defect: a frozen population can only **undercount**, and an undercount is anti-conservative — a frame that spread since the export is still read as rare, E9 does not subtract it, and the pair gets image evidence the batch engine would deny it. Every pass now reports the **export's** age, the calibration row's age and the population's **coverage** (the share of the photographs it actually scored that the table could measure), and refuses beyond `rt_calibration_max_age_days` (**14**). A baseline with no `exported_at` is refused rather than read as fresh.

**The refresh, and what it preserves.** One action: a **fresh export**, then

```
gh workflow run autodedup.yml --ref main -f mode=rt_seed \
  -f "args=export_run=<new run id>,settings=w8,model=w6_gold,backfill=true,reseed=true,rt_scope=obec:563510 obec:577626 cast_obce:490245"
```

which re-cuts the frozen calibration, re-materialises `autodedup.phash_pop` from the new artifact, re-cuts the parity baseline and re-backfills the fingerprints (now with their evidence counts). **Operator verdicts and must-not-links are generation-free and survive untouched.** `rt` pairs and clusters are rebuilt by the passes that follow; the old rows are left where they are rather than rewritten, because they were decided under facts the new generation does not share.

**The 115-image gap, and what a fresh export can and cannot do (E96, V4).** At three days of export age, **115 of 2,847 sampled images (4.0%)** read an unknown population and **54 of 400 pairs (13.5%)** lose `catalog_ratio_max`; every one of the 115 sits on an image whose `phash` itself moved after the export. A fresh export closes exactly that gap, because `COHORT_PHASH_POP_SQL` re-counts `count(DISTINCT listing_id)` over `public.images` for every hash the new cohort carries — the new hashes included — and the seed copies those counts in. It **cannot** be done without a new export: there is no index from a hash to its carriers (`images_phash_idx` is on `(sreality_id) where phash is not null`), D8 forbids adding one, and schema `autodedup` mirrors nothing, so the only measurement is the export's own sequential scan. A hash that arrives after the new export therefore reads **UNKNOWN** again — and that is confirmed to BAND rather than merge: `pop_is_measured()` false → `catalog_ratio` None → `catalog_ratio_max` absent → `certificate_c` (which requires it present) cannot fire → the pair falls to the model score. The gap reopens with time and closes with an export; it never asserts a photograph is unique.

#### Re-seeding and re-enabling, for the parent (the lane is OFF and stays off until this is done)

Unchanged from W9g in shape, with one step added and one argument stronger:
