1. **Apply migration `540_autodedup_evidence_arrival.sql`** (additive, `autodedup` only; 539 is already applied). Until it is applied a pass fails on the missing columns.
2. **Re-seed from a FRESH export** — not `35200225251`. At three days of age that artifact already costs 13.5% of sampled pairs their `catalog_ratio` and 4.0% of images an unknown population, and E95's age rail refuses a cohort more than 14 days old:

   ```
   gh workflow run autodedup.yml --ref main -f mode=export      # then take its run id
   gh workflow run autodedup.yml --ref main -f mode=rt_seed \
     -f "args=export_run=<new run id>,settings=w8,model=w6_gold,backfill=true,reseed=true,rt_scope=obec:563510 obec:577626 cast_obce:490245"
   ```

3. **Verify** `out/rt_seed.json`: `model_version` `w6_gold`, `population.hashes_written` > 0, `parity.breaches` 0 over `parity.checked` (which must now clear the floors — a seed that cannot verify itself is refused), `floors` as expected, `in_scope_listings` 4,887. In the database: `autodedup.phash_pop` is no longer empty, `autodedup.settings` carries `rt_parity_baseline:rt`, and `autodedup.rt_fp` rows carry `ev_*` and `first_decided_at`.
4. **Flip the variable:** `gh variable set AUTODEDUP_REALTIME_ENABLED --body true`.
5. **Read the first pass's summary**: `certificates` carries K-C; `parity.ok` true with `checked` above the floor; `calibration.export_age_days` well inside 14; `population.coverage` near 1.0; `evidence.candidates` / `listings_probed` / `images_probed` show the seventh feed's real cost, and `evidence.held_this_pass` is how many merges are waiting for the hourly producers.

### W9j — the generation was never BUILT, and the re-seed never reset it (E97, E98, E99, M160–M167, D37)

**What happened, in order.** The generation `rt` was re-seeded on 2026-09-20 from a fresh export (export run `35534165336`, seed run `35534490757`) with `settings=w8`, `model=w6_gold` and `backfill=true`: **4,976 fingerprints, 41,637 frozen photo-share counts, parity 120 checked / 0 breaches**. The first pass after it (run `35534642150`) ran clean in **6.9 s and 33 statements** — and **claimed nothing**: the evidence feed probed 400 listings and not one had moved. Two defects, both of DESIGN and neither of mechanism, and E70's replay-equivalence proof was structurally blind to both because it starts from an EMPTY store and feeds every listing as an arrival.

**Defect 1 — a backfilled seed writes fingerprints and no pairs (E97a).** `backfill=true` writes `rt_fp` and `fp_key` for every in-scope listing of the cohort and **not one row of `pairs`**. So the store held the whole scope and knew of no duplicate in it. A new arrival can then only ever merge with what arrives *after* the seed, or with one old listing at a time — and its group misses that old listing's own duplicates, because nothing ever scored them against each other. The fix is not a "backfill the pairs" step: a pair set written outside `run_pass` is a second engine, and the one proof this lane has says the incremental path equals the batch engine *when every listing arrives through it*. So the build goes through the proven path — `backfill=false`, and every in-scope listing is an arrival.

**Defect 2 — `reseed=true` resets nothing (E97b).** It re-cuts the calibration, re-materialises `autodedup.phash_pop` and re-cuts the parity baseline, and it **deletes no row**. The **15,923 pairs and 703 clusters** written by the *defective* first pass of 2026-09-20 (the wrong scorer, no photo-share counts, `model_version` NULL on all 15,923 — verified live) are therefore still in generation `rt`, and **nothing will ever re-decide them**: a pass re-scores a pair when a fingerprint digest moves, and those digests still agree. `fresh=true` is the other half of `reseed=true` and it is a DELETE of this generation's rows — twelve statements, one transaction, counted per table, `verdicts` / `must_not_link` / the judge's `judgements` / every other generation / every `public` table untouched, and a seed that refuses after it puts every row back.

#### The build, costed before it is run (E98, M160–M166)

The steady-state lane is built to stay off `public`: the entrant feed claims a **seventh** of a slice out of a membership snapshot, and **one** scope block is re-walked per pass on a **1 h / 6 h** cadence, because Vysočany's walk is 231 MB of cold heap reads (W9e/R3). Applied to a scope nothing has listed yet that is a day to *enumerate* Vysočany and eight hours of claiming after it. Hence the phase, and hence the clock.

**What one pass costs, measured rather than assumed.** The only live pass that has ever done work (run `35520830343`, 2026-09-20) claimed **77 listings** and took **876.7 s** — `cluster_s` **682.2 s** (78%), `score_s` 110.0 s over 36,940 pairs, `retrieve_s` 72.3 s, `refresh_s` 11.7 s over 2,825 dirty listings, 7,131 statements. That is **14.6 minutes of a 25-minute runner timeout on a claim a sixth of the shipped one**, and it is the pathological case this branch removes: the store held 4,887 fingerprints and zero pairs, so a claim of 77 widened to 2,825. The per-unit costs are still the right ones to project with: **0.97 s a component reclustered, 3.0 ms a pair scored, 2.0 ms a pair retrieved, 4.1 ms a listing refreshed**, and a fixed **6.9 s** a pass for the parity gate, the evidence sweep, a block walk and the storage readout (run `35534755407`).

**What the build totals.** The scoped replay (`w9h/reverify/replay.json`, the same three blocks) is the shape: **4,887 listings, 25 passes of 200, 44,724 pairs, 849 clusters, 1,410 component reclusters, 60,846 fact reads, 0 pair-budget refusals**. So

| what | count | at | total |
|---|---|---|---|
| component reclusters | 1,410 | 0.97 s | 1,368 s |
| pairs scored + retrieved | 44,724 | 5.0 ms | 224 s |
| listing fact fetches | 60,846 | 13 ms | 791 s |
| fingerprint refreshes | 4,976 | 4.1 ms | 20 s |
| fixed, per pass | 11 | 6.9 s | 76 s |

≈ **2,480 s of engine time for the whole build** (M162). With the first pass claiming ~90 under the conservative default rate and the rest claiming the full 500, that is **11 passes, ~225 s each, ~2 hours of wall clock at the `*/10` cadence** (M163) — five times inside the 25-minute job timeout, and the 900 s time budget binds long before it does.

**The pair budget is not the binding constraint, and that is measured, not hoped.** The whole scope holds **44,724 pairs**, under a third of one pass's `max_pairs = 150,000`, and the replay took 0 budget refusals at a claim of 200. A claim of 500 cannot want more than the scope holds. **Time** is the constraint, so the claim is bounded by it: `rt_pass_budget_s` (900 s) times the rate the last pass of this generation measured itself at (`rt_pass_rate_per_s:<generation>`, defaulting to the 0.088 listings/s of run `35520830343`). A build therefore ramps — 90, then 500 — rather than starting at a number nobody has measured.

**The evidence hold does not hold the build (M164).** E93 holds a photo-dependent merge while either side's gallery *exists and carries no hash at all*. Measured live across the three blocks: **42 of 4,976 listings (0.84%)** have images and zero `phash` — Jablonec 31, Vysočany 8, Turnov 3 — and 61 more have no images at all, which `Evidence.pending` reads as nothing to wait for. So the hold touches under one percent of the build, which is what it was designed to do: these are old listings whose producers ran months ago, not arrivals claimed five minutes after `first_seen_at`.

**What the build costs the production instance (M165, M166).** Measured today with `EXPLAIN (ANALYZE, BUFFERS)` on a 100-listing batch of obec 577626: the `listings` arm **3.1 buffers a listing**, the `images` arm **20.8** (1,806 images), the `image_clip_embeddings` arm **115.9** (1,790 vectors, 1.08 s — **70% of the whole fetch**), with location, price history and CLIP tags index-served on top: call it **~160 buffers = 1.25 MB of buffer traffic a listing**, of which **~29 blocks = 0.23 MB** came off disk on a warm instance. So

* **per pass** (a 500-listing claim, ~5,500 fact reads): ~885,000 buffers ≈ **6.9 GB of buffer traffic**, ~160,000 of them cold ≈ **1.25 GB read**, plus the evidence probe's 400 × 12.4 = 5,000 buffers (39 MB) and, outside the first pass, no block walk at all;
* **the block walks, once**: Vysočany **231 MB** + Jablonec **21 MB** + Turnov **6.6 MB** = **259 MB**, all of it in the bootstrap's first pass, after which W9e's ordinary cadence (1.60 GB a day) resumes;
* **the whole build**: ~60,846 fact reads ≈ **9.7M buffers, 76 GB of buffer traffic over ~2 hours** (≈ 10 MB/s of logical reads) — but the scope's **distinct** working set is only 4,976 listings' facts ≈ **6.2 GB of pages**, so after the first pass most of it is a shared-buffers hit and the cold share falls. Against W9d's *rejected* steady-state entrant sweep at 12.1 GB a day of cold reads, this is a one-off of the same order, and it ends.

#### The instrument the proof could not give (E99)

`--mode rt_equivalence` — read-only, no ledger row. Given `generation=rt` and `batch=<generation>` **scored on the same export with the same settings and model**, inside the live generation's own scope, it reads both stores and reports: the pairs both sides hold (zone, certificate, score within `tol`, default 1e-6), the pairs one side holds **by cause** — `scope`, `not_in_live_store`, `arrival_after_export`, `store_floor`, `retention` — with anything left over counted as **`unexplained`**, and the clusters by member set and by key, trimmed to the scope on both sides. The verdict is `ok` only when nothing shared differs, nothing one-sided is unexplained, the member sets agree and the build is finished. It is the check that would have caught E90 without `rt_parity` and E97(b) on the day it happened.

#### The build, for the parent (the lane is OFF and stays off until this is done)

1. **Score the batch generation on the same export.** `rt_equivalence` compares like with like or it compares nothing, so the batch side must be *this* export under *this* scorer:

   ```
   gh workflow run autodedup.yml --ref main -f mode=score \
     -f "args=export_run=35534165336,settings=w8,model=w6_gold,generation=g7_rtbase"
   ```

   **Name it `g7_rtbase`, not `g7`.** The validation UI defaults to the newest generation and `keep_generations=<n>` prunes oldest-first by `clusters.last_changed_at`, so a fresh generation is the *newest* and prunes nothing — but a short `keep_generations` on a later run would take **g4 / g5 / g6** before it took this one. Nothing is pruned by default; pass `keep_generations` on no run until this generation is retired by hand. Cost to the store: ~40 MB (91 MB of `pairs` over 39,832 rows across four generations, plus clusters, members and conflicts).
2. **Re-seed, clean and building:**

   ```
   gh workflow run autodedup.yml --ref main -f mode=rt_seed \
     -f "args=export_run=35534165336,settings=w8,model=w6_gold,backfill=false,reseed=true,fresh=true,rt_bootstrap=true,rt_scope=obec:563510 obec:577626 cast_obce:490245"
   ```

   Verify in `out/rt_seed.json`: `reset.pairs` **15923**, `reset.clusters` **703**, `backfilled` **0**, `bootstrap` **true**, `in_scope_listings` **4,976**, `parity.breaches` 0 over a `checked` that clears the floors.
3. **Flip the variable:** `gh variable set AUTODEDUP_REALTIME_ENABLED --body true`.
4. **Let it build: ~11 passes, ~2 hours.** Watch `bootstrap.backlog` fall and `claim_bound.bound_by` move from `time` to `count` after the first pass. The phase ends itself: `bootstrap.ended_this_pass` true once, then `bootstrap.active` false.
5. **Check it:**

   ```
   gh workflow run autodedup.yml --ref main -f mode=rt_equivalence \
     -f "args=generation=rt,batch=g7_rtbase"
   ```

   Pass criteria, all four: `pairs.differing` **0**, `pairs.causes.unexplained` **0**, `pairs.causes.not_in_live_store` **0** (anything else means the build is not finished — re-run after another pass), and `clusters.member_sets_identical` **true**. `verdict.ok` is exactly that conjunction. A fifth travels with them and holds even where pairs legitimately differ (E121): `pairs.score_self_consistency.defective` **0** over a `checked` that is **not** 0 — every row the instrument read a vector for, on both sides, carries the score its own vector implies. Read `exempt` beside it, because a `checked` of 0 under a large `exempt` is a rule asked of nothing, and `no_model_version` there is E97's shape returning.

### W9l — the NOT-ok verdict taken apart: one artifact, one drift, one store-path defect (E112, E113, E114, M171–M178, D41)

`rt_equivalence` run `35561654208` (2026-09-21 04:36:37–04:37:03 UTC) returned **NOT ok** on three reasons: **3,465 of 13,734** shared pairs differing in their DECISION, a score-only movement of **0.3924**, and cluster member sets differing inside the scope (live 898 / batch 903, shared 822, only_batch 81, only_live 76). Re-read live on **2026-09-21 04:44–05:01 UTC** — nothing was decided after 04:29:39 UTC, so the store did not move between the run and the reads — those three reasons are three different things and **only one of them is the engine**. Full attribution: `/home/hejtm/autodedup-artifacts/w9l/diagnosis.json`.

**The certificate difference is the INSTRUMENT (E112, M171).** Migration 539 added `pairs.certificate`; `incremental_sql.RT_PAIR_UPSERT_SQL` writes it and `score_sql.PAIR_UPSERT_SQL` never lists it — `score_lane.pair_params()` does not even build the key. Live: **3,462** of `rt`'s 13,738 rows carry a certificate; **0** of `rt_base_w13`'s 16,751 do, although **3,866** of their decision strings name one. So all 3,462 `by_field.certificate` differences are a column read against a column nobody wrote, and `decisions_moved` **3,465 = 3,462 + 3**: strip the artifact and **four** shared pairs differ in their decision. The right repair is **(b), the score lane writes the column**, not (a), the instrument parsing `decision`: over the 13,734 shared pairs both sides' decision strings name a certificate on exactly the same **3,400** pairs, and the live column carries **62 more** — E63 re-promotions under a `context_rule:` reason, which is precisely the loss the column exists to prevent, and precisely the edges E33 orders first. Parsing would make the two sides agree by throwing those 62 away and leave the store holding two meanings for one column. Recomputed under one definition: **both 13,734, zone 3, guard_veto 0, decision-string 1, decisions_moved 4, score 135, score_only 131, score_only_max 0.392432.**

**Every remaining PAIR difference is a fact that moved, and three features carry all of it (E113, M172, M173).** Diffing `pairs.features` vector by vector across all 13,734 shared pairs partitions them exactly: **9,820** byte-identical; **1,598** `overlap_days` only (57 score moves, max 0.0280); **1,525** `tfidf_cos` only (34, max 0.0026); **747** both (26, max 0.0073); **39** `both_active`+`overlap_days` (17 score moves and **all four decision moves**, max 0.3924); **5** all three (1, max 0.0212). No pair has an identical vector and a different score, so nothing is left for the arithmetic to explain.

* `overlap_days` and `both_active` are **fact drift** (M172). Of the 2,389 pairs whose `overlap_days` moved, **2,343 (98.1 %)** equal the value recomputed from *current* `public.listings` to within 0.02 d and **0** equal the batch's; mean live − batch **+0.248 d** (the export→pass lag), max **+3.145 d**. The **clock anchor is refuted as a cause**: both engines read `dataset.live_end_stamp` = `last_seen_at or inactive_at` under w13's `live_window_from_sighting`, and neither anchors on `now()`. The difference is *when* the facts were read, not *how* the window end is defined.
* The whole of the decision reason is **eleven listings** (M173). Fifty listings are endpoints of a `both_active` move; **11 of them — every one sreality — were `is_active=false` in the export with `inactive_at` 2026-09-17 23:5x** and are active with `inactive_at` NULL and `last_seen_at` 2026-09-21 01:19–01:24 now: a delist that **self-healed** (rule #3 / `touch_listings`) between the export and the passes. Those 11 produce all 44 `both_active`-moved pairs, all **4** decision moves and the **0.3924** score movement that trips the verdict's second reason.
* `tfidf_cos` is the **calibration cohort** (cause d), pre-registered: 2,277 pairs move, **61** by enough to move a score, max **0.0212**, **0** decision moves — reproducing the earlier verifier's 61 exactly.
* The evidence hold contributes **0**: no shared pair differs in `guard_veto`, `families` or `probes`.

**The clusters are a STORE-PATH defect, and it is the fourth of its class (E114, M174–M177, D41).** Inside the scope the merge-edge sets are all but identical — **live 4,915, batch 4,918, only_live 0, only_batch 3** — and those 3 are exactly the zone moves of the 11 healed listings. Three edges cannot move **305** listings into different member sets. Grouping the differences into connected components gives **47**: **2** explained by those 3 edges, and **45 (299 listings) in which both sides hold the SAME merge edges and cluster them differently** — 18 a pure re-partition of the same listing set, 27 where the clustered set itself differs, 6 touching the size-8 cap, and **0** clusters on either side carrying an out-of-scope member, so the scope trim hides nothing.

The minimal case names the mechanism. **8876 / 144514 / 162485**: three merge edges, all `certificate K-C`, all stored `score 1.0`, on *both* generations; floors 4 / 3 / NULL, so `floor_spread` permits {8876,162485} and {144514,162485} and refuses {8876,144514} and the triple. **The invariant set is not transitively closed, so which pair survives is decided by the edge ORDER** — and `cluster_conflicts` prints each side's order. The batch refused `(8876,144514)` at a stored `score` of 1.0 but a *conflict-row* score of **0.999999997735753**, then took `(144514,162485)`, then refused `(8876,162485)` at **0.9999999942645902**. The live lane refused `(8876,144514)`, **took `(8876,162485)`**, then refused `(144514,162485)`.

`autodedup.pairs.score` is **`real` — float4**. `cluster.edge_rank` is `(certificate first, −score, lo, hi)`. The batch lane ranks the in-memory float64 `Decision`s in the process that computed them; the real-time lane ranks `Decision`s rebuilt from stored rows (`incremental._recluster` → `store.pairs_within` → `PairRow.decision()`). Whenever two edges' float64 scores differ and their float4 images tie, the two engines sort them differently and the constrained union-find lands somewhere else. **2,882** of the batch's 4,918 in-scope merge edges are stored as exactly 1.0, and at least **1,152** of those had a float64 score that was not.

**Reproduced locally in 50 seconds for $0 (M175–M177).** Same export cohort, same scope, same w13 + `w6_gold`, the **same 46,706 candidate pairs and same 4,918 merge edges and same decisions**, clustered twice — once on float64, once with every score narrowed to float4 exactly as Postgres narrows it (`/home/hejtm/autodedup-artifacts/w9l/repro2.py`, out `repro_float4.json`):

| | float64 | float4 |
|---|---|---|
| clusters | **903** | **900** |
| member sets one-sided | — | **74 / 71** |
| `floor_spread` refusals | **842** | 965 |
| `size` refusals | **14** | **289** |

The float64 arm lands on **903 clusters and 842 `floor_spread` refusals — the batch generation's own in-scope cluster count, and D36 (viii)'s own number for g7.** The float4 arm loses three clusters and **74 / 71 member sets one-sided**, the same shape and the same order as the live store's 81 / 76, the residual being the fact drift above. Among 4,918 merge edges there are only **130 distinct float4 values**, and **4,800 of the edges** sit in one of the **12** buckets that hold more than one float64 score: **97.6 % of the merge edges lose their rank in the store.** The refusal census reads the same defect three ways — `size` refusals **14** (batch, in memory), **289** (local float4), **677** (live, cumulative over 12 passes).

**Why the replay proof missed it (E114).** `autodedup/replay.py` builds its incremental arm on `incremental_store.MemoryStore`, which keeps `PairRow` objects verbatim; both arms therefore rank the same float64 and **the float4 narrowing exists only in Postgres**. The proof is sound about the MECHANISM and blind to the STORE — the same shape as E90 and E97(b), for the fourth time. And the consequence is larger than the real-time lane: **generation `rt_base_w13` is not re-clusterable from its own store**, because its rows carry float4 scores *and* NULL certificates, so `edge_rank`'s first key collapses too.

**Two instrument repairs the report also owes (M178).** `causes` is summed across both sides, so `scope` 3,006 + `store_floor` 14 + `arrival_after_export` 1 = 3,021 reads as if it should equal `only_batch` 3,017 when it equals 3,017 + 4; split by side it is **3,006 one-sided by scope + 11 in-scope batch-only, all `reject` + 4 live-only, all `reject` at 0.1361**, and not one of the 15 is a merge edge, so none of them touches clustering. And `tol = 1e-6` is about **16 float4 ULPs**: **121** shared pairs already differ by more than 0 and at most the tolerance, which makes the score counter a knife edge (this recomputation reproduces the decision counters exactly and the score counters within 2 pairs).

### W9m — the repair: one certificate definition, a store that keeps the ranked score, attributed drift (E115–E121, M179–M182, D42)

W9l's three verdict reasons were three different things and only one was the engine. This is the repair, and each defect was reproduced in a test before it was fixed: `tests/autodedup/test_store_precision.py`, `test_pair_write_parity.py`, `test_replay_live_order.py`, and the W9m half of `test_rt_equivalence.py`.

**E115 — a field the clustering RANKS on may not carry precision the store cannot keep.** `autodedup.pairs.score` is `double precision` (migration 541), because `cluster.edge_rank` is `(certificate first, −score, lo, hi)` and the invariants it feeds — `floor_spread`, the size cap, must-not-link — are not transitively closed: of {a,c} and {b,c} exactly one survives, and the edge ORDER decides which. The batch lane ranks the float64 `Decision`s in the process that computed them; the real-time lane ranks `Decision`s rebuilt from stored rows, and while the column was `real` those were two different numbers on 97.6 % of the cohort's merge edges (M175). The minimal case is a test now: three merge edges, three distinct float64 scores, one float4 image, floors 4 / 3 / NULL — the true order takes (2,3) and the stored order takes (1,3), same decisions, same invariants, different partition. **Neither lane's parameter narrows the score on the way in any more either**: a `%(score)s::real` cast narrows the value before the column ever sees it, so widening the column alone would have repaired nothing.

**E116 — the replay proof covers the STORE and the LIVE claim order.** Two blind spots, both of them the reason four passing waves said nothing about a live store that disagreed. `incremental_store.MemoryStore` now narrows a score exactly as the column does, driven by the one constant `store_score.PAIR_SCORE_SQL_TYPE`, so `--score-column real` reproduces E114 inside the proof that missed it — and a migration that narrows the column again has to move that constant with it, or `test_store_precision` fails. And `replay.py` always runs a **third arm** in `RT_SCOPE_ENTRANTS_SQL`'s own order (ascending listing id, which is what the bootstrap phase claims in) at the production claim size of **500**, against the same batch reference; it is not a flag, because a proof whose hardest arm is opt-in is a proof nobody runs. **Both repairs are measured together (M180):** pointed at the pre-541 column the proof now lands on **903 batch clusters against 900, with 74 / 71 member sets one-sided** — W9l's local float4 arm exactly, carrying the live store's own examples — while the arrival-order and live-order arms give the SAME 900 / 74 / 71 and agree with each other on every member set. **The order was never the cause; the store's precision was**, and on the shipped column all three arms reach the batch pass's 903 clusters with identical keys (M179, M181).

**E117, E118 — every lane that writes a pair writes every column the clustering reads.** `score_lane.pair_params` emits `certificate` and `score_sql.PAIR_UPSERT_SQL` writes it, which is ruling D41 applied: the COLUMN is the definition, not the instrument parsing `decision` — that would have made the two sides agree by discarding the 62 E63 re-promotions E33 orders first. A census test (`CLUSTERING_COLUMNS` = `score`, `zone`, `certificate`, `guard_veto`, `families`) fails if either lane stops writing any of them, which is the standing rail against the next asymmetry of this shape. The second asymmetry goes with it: a refused **bridge** now names the invariant that refused it on both lanes (the score lane wrote NULL on all 1,976 of them, which is why M177 had to read one defect three ways by hand).

**E119 — every difference the instrument reports is ATTRIBUTED to a named cause.** At pair grain, by reading which FEATURES moved: `drifted_since_export`, `calibration_cohort`, `clock_anchor`, `store_write_asymmetry`, or `unexplained`. Drift is not waved through — it is **evidenced**, by recomputing the clock features from CURRENT `public.listings` through `features.clock_features`, the engine's own function, and asking whether the live value is the one closer to the facts as they stand now. (That extraction is itself the one-definition repair for the clock: `pair_features` and the instrument call the same function, and `ClockFacts` names the four listing facts it reads. The W9l finding stands — neither side anchors on `now()` — so this makes a refutation permanent rather than repeating it.) A pair whose vector is IDENTICAL and whose answer is not is `unexplained`, and so is one whose moved features are neither clock nor calibration: the two named causes are the only two things that legitimately move an input. At cluster grain the comparison is now by **connected component** — the unit W9l had to build by hand to find that 45 of 47 components held the same merge edges on both sides — and each one is `arrival` (a listing the batch cohort never had), `upstream_pair` (the two sides hold different merge edges, so the pair grain already accounted for it), or `unexplained`, which is exactly the shape E114 wore.

**E120 — an asymmetry in the store or the instrument is a DEFECT, not an engine disagreement.** Three of them are detected and each fails the verdict on its own: `store_write_asymmetry` (a side whose `decision` strings name a certificate on some row while the column holds one on none — evidenced, so a generation that merely certified nothing is not accused), `clock_anchor` (the two generations' settings disagree about `live_window_from_sighting`, read from `rt_calibration` and from the batch pass's own `autodedup.runs` row), and `store_score_precision` (the score column cannot hold what `edge_rank` ranks). Two smaller instrument repairs travel with them: the one-sided `causes` are reported **per side** as well as summed, and the score tolerance is never finer than the store's own resolution — `tol = 1e-6` was ~16 float4 ULPs and 121 shared pairs already sat inside it (M178), so the effective tolerance is raised to 32 units in the column's last place and the report says which was used.

**E121 — a cause may explain an INPUT; it may never explain an ANSWER, and the row's own arithmetic says which.** E119 classifies a difference by naming which inputs moved and never asks whether the moved inputs can ACCOUNT for it — so an adversarial read of it demonstrated, twice, that a **genuine, evidenced** clock drift (the live `both_active` IS what today's `public.listings` say) carried a zone flip merge → reject and a 0.9 score move with it, attributed `drifted_since_export`, `verdict.ok` TRUE over a real decision bug; and that the `calibration_cohort` branch, granted with no evidencing and no magnitude bound at all, buys the same flip for a 2e-6 nudge in `tfidf_cos` — a feature that moved on 2,277 of the live comparison's 13,734 shared pairs. So the tightest rule that closes it ships as **R1**: `decide._decide_layers` sets `score = model.predict_proba(feats)` in every branch but the two vetoes and `apply_context_rule` carries the score through untouched, so **a stored pair's score must follow from its own stored feature vector**. Every row the instrument reads a vector for is re-scored on **BOTH** sides under the model its own `model_version` names (`harness.model_of_version` — the lanes' definition, not a second one), and a row that does not reproduce its score is the defect **`score_not_from_vector`**, which fails the verdict *whatever* cause was attributed to that pair. It costs nothing to be sure of: the recomputation reads only what the comparison already reads, and on the export cohort the stored score IS `predict_proba(stored vector)` on **46,688 of 46,688** non-veto pairs, **max gap 0.0** (M182). Three details carry the rule's honesty. (i) The recomputation is **narrowed through the store's own column** (`store_score.narrow`) before it is compared, so a pre-541 `real` store is re-scored AS a `real` store rather than passed by a tolerance wide enough to hide what `real` did to it. (ii) The four rows a re-score cannot be ASKED of are counted and named rather than skipped — `guard_veto` (a veto writes 0.0 without consulting the model), `no_feature_vector` (the upsert coalesces, so a probe-only update keeps the vector the decision was taken on and a row can hold none), `no_model_version` (the shape E97 wore: the row does not name the scorer it was decided by, and inventing one would be the instrument deciding what the store failed to record) and `model_unavailable` — and the report prints the denominator: rows, checked, exempt by reason, defects per side, max gap, the tolerance and the column it was taken against. (iii) The verifier's **R2** — recompute the ZONE from the stored row — is **NOT shipped and is not cheap here**: it needs `certificate_of(feats, la, lb, …)` and E63's `apply_context_rule` (the 62 re-promotions of M171), both of which read the two LISTINGS, and short of them it would have to trust the very `certificate` column `store_write_asymmetry` reports as missing. R1 catches both demonstrated holes on its own, which is what the measurement says it is for.

**What the verdict now fails on**: `unexplained` at pair grain, at one-sided grain and at cluster-component grain; any defect, `score_not_from_vector` among them; a build that is not finished (`not_in_live_store`); a model version that is not the batch generation's; and an **unattributed** score-only movement above 0.05. It no longer fails on a difference it has named — which is the whole change: 3,465 "moved decisions" that were 3,462 instrument and 4 self-healed delistings would now read as one defect and four attributed drifts.

**D42 — and what this repair cannot do to a generation already written.** Widening a column is value-preserving and therefore repairs nothing retrospectively: `rt` and `rt_base_w13` were narrowed when they were written and the bits are gone, and `rt_base_w13` holds NULL certificates besides. **Both generations have to be re-made for the comparison to mean anything** — the batch baseline re-scored (so it writes the certificate and the full-precision score) and the live generation re-seeded and rebuilt through the arrival path (D37). Until then `rt_equivalence` will keep naming `store_write_asymmetry` and, until migration 541 is applied, `store_score_precision` — which is the instrument doing its job: it is saying the store cannot answer the question yet.

### The open risk this lane does not close

**GitHub cron is not a latency guarantee.** A `*/10` schedule on this fleet has measured 80–256 minute gaps under load, so the honest arrival-to-decision p95 is cron-bound, not engine-bound: the engine decides a listing in under a second and then waits for a runner. The lane is watermark-driven precisely so a late run claims everything since the cursor rather than a lost window — nothing is dropped — but a *real* p95 under a minute needs §11's always-on worker lane, which is the same `run_pass` behind `scraper/realtime_worker.py` and needs operator approval to touch the production image (D26). That is the one thing W9 designs and does not build.

**And one number this program has not measured: what a frozen calibration COSTS after it is frozen.** The replay isolates mechanism — both paths run the same calibration — so drift in block and corpus token frequencies, in the price deciles and in the certifying-code set over 30 days of arrivals is unmeasured, and the recalibration cadence is therefore an open number rather than a ruled one. The rail that would notice it exists (E64 re-opens a promotion a census has overtaken, and a recalibration is a new generation, which is the one moment E64's census can have grown), but the cadence itself is owed.

---

## 12. Q11 — Progress page and validation UI

**Reuse, don't duplicate — but reuse the right layer.** `BrowseExperience.tsx` is the whole Browse *cohort* surface (filters + map + cards), bound to `BrowseViewState` and to property-grain `CardRow` from `properties_public`. The autodedup surfaces are **iteration-, pair- and cluster-grain**. So: reuse `ImageCarousel`, `ImageLightbox`, `Tabs`, `Dialog`, `Spinner`, `Skeleton`, `ErrorBanner`, `SectionChrome` (`Chevron`, `useCollapsed`), `lib/format`, `lib/enums`, `lib/portals`, `lib/imageUrl` — and build exactly one new comparison component, `PairCard.tsx` (two-column listing comparison + evidence panel), used by both verdict views. The in-repo precedent for a verdict surface is `pages/NewDedupExamReview.tsx`; for a program drill-down, `pages/NewDedupCandidates.tsx`.

### The progress page (W1, before any validation view)

The operator asked for a **top-line menu entry** that shows how the program is actually going, iteration by iteration. It is the first UI this program ships — **W1, ahead of the validation views** — because it is what makes a long autonomous program legible while it runs.

- **Route `/autodedup/progress`** (nav entry `AUTODEDUP → Progress`), backed by `GET /autodedup/iterations`.
- **One row per iteration**, newest first, grouped by wave: wave and title, status (`running` / `done` / `failed` / `skipped`), **approach** (what was tried, in a sentence), **tools used** (the chips this document's per-wave "Tools used" paragraphs feed), **sample statistics** (cohort, blocks, n pairs, n labels), **metrics** (the numbers that iteration moved, with the gate it was measured against), **cost in USD read from `llm_calls`, never forecast** (E32), links to the GitHub run and to any artifact, and free-text notes.
- **Header strip**: total spend to date against the **$200 program cap** and the **$25/run cap** (D2), waves closed vs open, and the current mode — which reads **SHADOW** for the whole program (D4).
- The page is a read of `autodedup.iterations` (§13). Every lane writes its own row: `status='running'` at start, terminal status plus metrics and cost at the end. A lane that dies leaves a `running` row with a stale `started_at`, which is itself the signal.

### The validation views (W5)

- `/autodedup` — funnel, cost-to-date, precision/recall with CIs, per-stratum thresholds, latch state.
- `/autodedup/groups` — **proposed groups** (shadow mode: nothing has been applied). Infinite list of cluster cards: member listings side by side with portal badges, thumbnails, price, area, disposition, floor, first/last seen and active state; `min_edge_score`; `decided_by`; evidence chips (`6 interior photos matched`, `same street+no.`, `price path shares 3 events`, `re-listed after 214 days`, `judge: same_property 0.93`); a **merged price-history sparkline** — the operator's stated reason for wanting re-listings merged; and the **"what would move" panel** (E44/D5): the pipeline card, collections, tags, notes and notification rows that a merge *would* carry onto the survivor. Default sort **weakest edge first**, because that is where errors live. Actions (D39): **Stejné** / **Různé** (verdict + permanent must-not-link) / **Nevím**.
  Two things the first review sessions added to the card. (0) **The card shows EVERY advert of the group.** It used to render four and count the rest ("+1 further advert…"), and the operator read that as the group being smaller than its own header said — "I see only 4 adverts here while it says there should be 5" — which on a surface whose entire question is *are these the same flat?* is the one thing it may not do. All members render as full cards in the same grid (four to a row at lg, wrapping); past **12** the remainder folds behind one button that expands it **in place**, and any group being ruled unit by unit un-folds itself, since a split sends every member and a letter set over adverts nobody can see is a ruling by omission. (1) **The card pages the photos, it does not show a cover.** A cover is the weakest evidence a portal offers — two adverts for one flat often share only the floor plan, and two different flats in one development share the cover and nothing else — so the list statement ships the **first 12 frames per member** (`GROUP_MEMBERS_SQL`, capped in the statement, ordered exactly like the cover so `images[0]` *is* the cover) and the card runs the same carousel the dialog does; `n_images` still counts the whole album, which is how the card offers "+18 fotek v detailu". (2) **A group is not always one answer**: every member carries a unit letter and two letters raise the split row (E49, §9's split verdict). The assignment is page-level state shared by the card and the dialog, and a split always sends **every** member — including the ones a four-across card counted rather than showed. (3) **The dialog shows the advert's own text**, because the photos and the attribute row are exactly what a developer project makes identical: five units in one building share the render set, the price band, the disposition and the storey count, and differ only in what the ad *says* — "byt č. 14", "ve 4. NP", "orientace na jih", "68 m²". Each member therefore carries its **title** and its **whole description** under the attributes, in a 60ch measure with the advert's own line breaks, collapsed past six lines behind *zobrazit celý popis* (state per member, forgotten when the dialog closes), with those four token families marked in the text — a **finding aid, never a claim**: the marks say "compare these sentences", never "these two agree", and a town is deliberately not a compass point (`Jihlava` is not `jih`) because a highlight that cries wolf teaches the operator to ignore the highlights. Two things this does NOT do. It does not put the text on the **queue**: `GROUP_MEMBERS_SQL` runs for every member of every card on a 20-group page, and `listings.description` is TOASTed, so the text is a separate statement (`MEMBER_TEXT_SQL`) over the members of the ONE cluster being opened — the title is not even a column, each portal parser files it in `raw_json` (`title`, sreality's `advert_name`, else `name`). And it does not invent a second scrubber: **E28 is unchanged and now covers the title too** — every advert string on an operator surface goes through `judge.scrubbed_text`, which is the judge digest's own scrub with the *character cap* removed. That cap (1200) was always a **token budget, not a privacy rule**; the operator pays no tokens, and the sentence that separates two units is as often in the last paragraph as the first, so both operator surfaces — this dialog and `/autodedup/pair`, which renders the identical component — read the whole text while the paid judge lane keeps its capped digest exactly as it was.
- A group whose ruling was taken on a **different set of adverts** (E58) wears an ochre notice — *"Potvrzeno v g4 pro jinou sestavu inzerátů: přibyly #…, ubyly #…"* — with the adverts that arrived marked on their own cards and the ordinary verdict buttons underneath. The group counts as **unreviewed** until it is ruled again, the verdict filter offers **"změněno od verdiktu"** as its own queue, and the progress strip counts only rulings that apply. Nothing is hidden and nothing is inherited: the question changed, so it is asked again.
- `/autodedup/residual` — **residual duplicates**: pairs the engine did *not* accept but scored above a display floor (`p ≥ 0.20`), plus everything the recall probe flagged, ranked by score so the scroll is ordered by expected yield. Attribute diff table highlighting only the disagreeing fields, both covers as queue-grain thumbnails (the hero photos belong to the pair page — two 600px images push the diff, the reason and the three answers below the fold), the per-feature model contribution breakdown (a linear model is legible), and **"why it wasn't merged"** — which precondition failed. That last field is what turns a review session into design feedback. **Each thumbnail pages**, for the reason the group card does: the residual list statements ship the **first 12 frames per side** (the same cap, the same `sequence NULLS LAST, id` order, so `images[0]` *is* the cover) and `PairCard` runs the same carousel through `MemberGallery` — the 160px footprint is unchanged, the two sides page independently, and `n_images` still counts the whole album behind "+K fotek v detailu". Actions (D39): **Stejné** / **Různé** (two-step confirm — a negative PAIR verdict is permanent) / **Nevím**.
- `/autodedup/residual` serves that cohort in **two views**, switched by `?view=` and defaulting to **"po skupinách"** (**E56**). The operator's own words after the first sessions: *"Why is the UX of residual so different from groups, can we align it more, while you still get what you need from me? The UX of groups felt pretty good."* What the program needs from this surface is pair-grain labels on pairs the engine did NOT merge, taken blind — not one question per pair, which is what made it feel different and what made it slow. So the grouped view runs the **Groups card** over a candidate group: every advert with its paged gallery, a unit letter each, the split row, the dialog with the advert's own scrubbed text. **"po dvojicích" keeps the original pair queue unchanged**, one click away, and the two read the same cohort at the same display floor.
  Four things the grouped card does differently, each said on the card rather than left to be noticed. (1) **The letters start APART.** On the groups queue every member starts on `A` because the engine proposed they are one property; here it proposed nothing, so each unit opens on its own letter and the operator brings together what belongs together — opening on all-A would put the engine's answer in the operator's mouth, backwards. (2) **An already-merged group is one LOCKED unit**, visually bracketed ("už sloučeno · skupina #900") with ONE letter select for the whole group; a stored ruling that splits a lock (the operator partitioned that group on the Groups page) is collapsed onto the lock, because the card cannot honour both and does not own that group. (3) **Two one-click shortcuts** — "Vše je jedna jednotka" and "Nic k sobě nepatří" — set the letters and store **nothing**; the primary action still reads **"Uložit rozhodnutí"**, because every crossing pair becomes a permanent must-not-link and a one-click save over eight adverts is 28 permanent facts. (4) **REVIEWED IS DERIVED**, never stored: a candidate group is not a row anywhere, so a card counts as reviewed when **every residual pair inside it carries an operator pair verdict** — by ANY operator, this being a single-operator platform, which is the same reading the agreement statement makes of the same rows. Partly-ruled cards report `n_pairs_reviewed` so an abandoned card can be found again.
  Header chips are the **engine's own evidence** and nothing else — the score range of the card's edges, the zone mix, the evidence families, how many of its pairs are ruled. **No judge artefact reaches the queue payload at all**, which is how blind mode (default ON here, as on the pair view) is enforced rather than remembered; the drill-down carries `?blind=1`, and the card's dialog withholds the judge column until the card is ruled. The filter bar drops the controls this view does not send — the display floor (the packing has ONE floor, so a cohort a card came from cannot move under a control), the portal pair (a card spans several adverts), and "has a judge verdict" (a judge artefact) — and its verdict control is the card vocabulary, `nezkontrolované / zkontrolované`. Orders: weakest card edge first (the default, for the reason the groups queue has it), largest first, and the seeded random sample. The counter strip counts **cards** (`surface=candidates`), a third grain that is never added to the other two.
- The **200-pair gold-vs-operator session (D6)** runs through these views; the session's agreement number is metric 8 and gates the program. Three things exist for that session and nothing else (**E55**). A **`sort=random`** option on both queues ("náhodný vzorek") — a seeded, stable shuffle, the one order that is not a working order, because an error rate measured weakest-edge-first is an error rate about the weakest edges; the seed lives in the URL only when it is not the default `v1`. A **counter strip** on both queues (`GET /autodedup/validation-progress`), which answers the operator's actual question — "how many do I need to do?" — with the whole generation ("zkontrolováno 103 / 870 skupin", plus how many of those rulings were not `same`) and, in the seeded order, the sample: "Náhodný vzorek: 37 / 100 zkontrolováno". The sample is the first 100 of the seeded order over the whole generation and is deliberately **not** narrowed by the filter bar; the strip says so. And **blind mode** ("naslepo (skrýt verdikt soudce)") — default ON on the residual queue, offered and default OFF on the groups queue — which withholds every judge artefact for a row until the operator has ruled on it, then reveals all of it, and travels into the pair page as `?blind=1`.
- **"How many do I need to do?" is answered in the UI, not in this document**, because both numbers move: the queue is 870 groups, the sample is 100 of them per surface, and the gate is ~200 gold-labelled *pairs* — three different denominators that a page can hold together and a paragraph cannot.

**Filters on both verdict views:** town/quarter, source pair, `category_main`, `category_type`, cluster size, score range, evidence family, verdict state (unreviewed/confirmed/rejected), `decided_by`, `has_llm_verdict`, `shared_photo_warning`, `project_suspected`, date range.

Four properties of that bar, learned from the first production passes over it:

- **E54** **WHICH GENERATION a validation view reads is resolved against the store, never defaulted in code.** Every view — `/autodedup/groups`, `/autodedup/residual`, `/autodedup/blocks`, `/autodedup/pair`, and the SPA's own filter state — defaulted to `g1`, the first hand-prior pass: it over-merged developer units and was superseded by `g2` and `g3`, so the queue kept serving proposals the live engine no longer makes and the operator reviewed six certificate edges of a group `g3` never proposed. A verdict is not cheap feedback — it lands as a permanent must-not-link and as a calibration label — so reviewing a retired pass is worse than reviewing nothing. The generation parameter is now **optional everywhere**: omitted, the route resolves the newest pass off `autodedup.clusters` (`LATEST_GENERATION_SQL`, the first row of the rollup `GET /autodedup/generations` serves, so the picker and the default cannot disagree) and **echoes it back**, which is the only place the page learns which pass it is showing. A store with no clustering yet resolves to **no generation at all** — an empty queue — never to a fabricated name. An older pass stays readable (that is how a past review is re-examined) but the view says so in words and offers the newest in one click, and every per-row action — the evidence drill-down, the split — names the pass **its own row** came from rather than the queue's.
- **Every control offers a vocabulary, never a code.** "Town/quarter" is the block key — a RÚIAN bigint — and shipping it as a text field meant typing a town name produced NaN, which the query layer drops: the filter silently did nothing. It is now a select of the blocks *this generation clustered*, named off `listing_location` and carrying each block's group count (`GET /autodedup/blocks`, busiest first and capped). A block is a **code and a grain** — `o563510` is a town, `c490245` a quarter, and migration 529 exists because the two vocabularies share one number space — so the option value carries both and the query sends `block` + `block_grain`; a bare code (a link written before the grain existed) still filters grain-blind. The residual view's block predicate reads `listing_location` too, the same store `fingerprint.block_key_of` derives a block key from: it used to read `autodedup.listing_fp`, which no shipped lane writes, so every block on offer emptied the queue. The source pair is likewise two portal selects rather than a typed `a+b` string, composed in the order the server's `least()/greatest()` predicate compares.
- **The URL is the filter state** (`lib/useUrlFilters`), so a filtered queue is bookmarkable, shareable and survives a reload; a key present in the query string wins, an absent one means the default, and a value CLEARED against a non-empty default is written as an empty key because "no floor" and "never set" are different filters. The keyset cursor is deliberately not in the URL — a link means "this filter", never "this page of it".
- **Both lists report "20 of N"**, counted by the same `WHERE` fragment the page reads through, on the first page only (paging cannot change N). `null` means *not counted here*, and the page says what it loaded rather than inventing a total.

**API** — `api/routes/autodedup.py`, `APIRouter(prefix="/autodedup", dependencies=[Depends(deps.require_admin)])`, registered in `api/main.py` beside the other program routers, with the `store_ready(conn)` probe idiom of `api/routes/new_dedup_candidates.py` so a pre-migration DB renders an empty state rather than a 500:

- `GET /autodedup/iterations` — the progress page (W1)
- `GET /autodedup/stats`
- `GET /autodedup/generations` — every clustering pass the store holds, newest first, with `latest` named: the generation picker's vocabulary and the fact a view needs to notice it is showing a superseded queue (E54)
- `GET /autodedup/groups?after=&limit=&…` — keyset by `(min_edge_score asc, cluster_key desc)`; each item carries `member_verdicts`, the operator's own rulings on its members' pairs, so a card can show the split that is stored (E50)
- `GET /autodedup/groups/{cluster_key}` — members, per-edge evidence, judge rationale, the "what would move" report, and `member_verdicts` at MEMBER grain (the pairs the engine never scored included). Each member also carries `title` + the full scrubbed `description` (with `description_truncated: false` and `description_chars`) — the detail path ONLY; the queue route above deliberately ships neither
- `GET /autodedup/residual?after=&limit=&…`
- `GET /autodedup/candidates?generation=&sort=weakest|strongest|largest|random&seed=&verdict=unreviewed|reviewed&zone=&block=&after=&limit=` — the candidate queue (E56). Three cheap statements build the packing (the residual cohort's ids/score/zone/family/block, the generation's locks, and the fingerprint the in-process memo is keyed on); the page's members come from ONE statement over its listing ids, shaped exactly like a group card's member and carrying `unit_lock`, plus `member_verdicts` so the letters hydrate after a reload. Filters apply to the BUILT cards, never to the packing — a card whose membership moved with a select is not a card anybody can rule on
- `GET /autodedup/candidates/{candidate_key}` — one card, whole: members with `title` + the full scrubbed `description` (E28), every scored pair among them with its certificate, "why it wasn't merged" and whether it is one of THIS card's questions, and the judge's latest word per tier (the SPA hides it while blind)
- `POST /autodedup/verdict/candidate-split` — one candidate group ruled unit by unit (§9): the split route's fan-out, no cluster verdict, no write inside a lock, no reason chips
- `GET /autodedup/validation-progress?surface=groups|residual|candidates&generation=&seed=&min_score=` — the session counter (E55): the whole generation and the first 100 of the seeded order, at the surface's own grain (cluster on groups, pair on residual, **card on candidates** — counted off the cached packing rather than a statement, since a candidate group is not a row). Two aggregate statements, no paging
- `GET /autodedup/agreement?generation=` — operator-vs-judge (D6/E55): one statement, one row per comparable pair, and the arithmetic in `autodedup/agreement.py`
- `GET /autodedup/blocks?generation=` — the block filter's vocabulary: every block this generation clustered (`generation` optional here as on every view — omitted means the newest pass, E54), with its grain (`o` obec / `c` část obce, migration 529), its name from `listing_location` and its cluster/listing counts; busiest blocks first, capped, since at corpus scale this is thousands of obce
- `GET /autodedup/pair/{lo}/{hi}` — full evidence: every feature with value and presence flag, both digests, both image lists with per-image Hamming/cosine, the judge transcript, the score decomposition
- `GET /autodedup/verdict-reasons` — the reason registry (§9), served so the SPA hard-codes no vocabulary; no store read, so it answers against an un-migrated database
- `POST /autodedup/verdict` `{kind, cluster_key|lo+hi, verdict, note, reasons}` — a cluster `same` also retracts every operator veto inside the group (E52)
- `POST /autodedup/verdict/split` `{cluster_key, generation, units:[{listing_id, unit}], confirm_retract?, note, reasons}` — one group ruled unit by unit; the page names no relation (D39) and the server reads a missing one as `different`, keeping `relation`/`relations` as the older client's path (E51); the pairwise fan-out, the 409 confirmation and the must-not-link semantics are in §9

Filters are validated as **keys against a server-side registry**, never a predicate off the wire (the `AUDIT_BUCKETS` idiom). Reads go through the bearer-gated API rather than `*_public` views because the browser Supabase client is pinned to schema `public`, so `autodedup` is unreachable from the browser by construction — and because `listings` is RLS deny-all (raw broker PII inline). Photos render through the existing public `GET /images/{storage_path}` → 302 presigned R2 via `imageSrc({sreality_url, storage_path})`, so the UI needs no new media path.

**Nav:** an `AUTODEDUP` **section** of the single `Admin` dropdown in `Shell.tsx` inside the `showAdmin` block, with **Progress** as its first entry. It shipped as its own trigger beside `NEW DEDUP` and `Settings`, as this document specified; adding it was what finally overflowed the header row (1555px of nav inside 1440px, clipping `Settings` and the account button), and since the bar's 3.5rem height is load-bearing for three Browse panes the three triggers were collapsed into one menu with a section each — `NEW DEDUP`, `AUTODEDUP` and `Settings` keep their names as the section headings. Routes are declared in `frontend/src/lib/routes.ts` `def()` then wired in `frontend/src/routes.tsx` wrapped in `<AdminPage>`; hand-typed route literals are an eslint error. Copy in English; property vocabulary in Czech, pulled from the shared `lib/enums` / `lib/portals` modules.

## 13. Q12 — Postgres schema

Two migrations, both **applied via `gh workflow run apply_migration.yml --ref <branch> -f file=NNN_x.sql -f dry_run=false -f confirm=APPLY` before the PR merges** (a merged PR is not an applied migration):

- **`migrations/527_autodedup_called_for.sql`** — the three `called_for` values, the check constraint restated whole, nothing removed. Shipped and applied *before* any calling code, because `LLMClient._record_call` on the success path is not wrapped in try/except: a CHECK violation raises *after* the provider has already billed.
- **`migrations/528_autodedup_foundation.sql`** — schema `autodedup`, its tables, indexes, `enable row level security` on every table in the same migration that creates it, `revoke all … from anon, authenticated`, default privileges revoked, and **no FK into any production table** (the schema must be droppable in one statement without touching production). `set lock_timeout = '5s'` plain — never `SET LOCAL`; the apply path is statement-autocommit.

- **`migrations/538_autodedup_generation_scoped_store.sql`** — **the generation is part of the key, and a cluster verdict records the set it binds (E58)**. `clusters` becomes `(generation, cluster_key)`, `cluster_members` gains `generation` (backfilled from its cluster) and keys on `(generation, cluster_key, listing_id)`, `pairs` gains `generation` (backfilled from the `(model_version, feature_version)` map `autodedup.runs` records, `clusters` as the fallback, the literal `legacy` for a row neither can place) and keys on `(generation, listing_lo, listing_hi)`; every index the reads need is re-created generation-first under a NEW name with the old one dropped afterwards. `verdicts` gains `generation text` and `member_ids bigint[]`, plus a CHECK that a pair-grain row carries neither and a generation-aware cluster-grain unique index (`autodedup_verdicts_cluster_gen_uidx`, the old key-only one dropped after it exists) so one pass's ruling can never overwrite another's. The 224 cluster rulings taken on g4 groups are backfilled as literal UPDATEs — guarded on `member_ids is null` AND bounded to rulings decided before 2026-09-19, since the apply lands before the merge and the old api writes NULL/NULL rows the NULL guard alone cannot tell apart — from the last faithful g4 evidence — the implied labels of run `35468267042` (exported 14 minutes before the g5 promotion, each carrying its `cluster_key`) cross-checked byte-for-byte against the local g4 engine reproduction (870 clusters, `w5_gold`); every other cluster-grain row takes the generation that was newest at its `decided_at`, and only where that pass actually holds the ruled key — six rulings sit on g1 groups no later pass re-clustered, so they stay NULL and read as legacy. **The 34 surviving g4 cluster rows are left exactly as they are**: this migration does not resurrect the 836 keys g5 re-stamped (rewriting them back is the same defect pointed the other way); g4's clustering is restored by re-running the score lane at g4's settings, which after 538 writes only into `g4` and cannot disturb g5.

**Tables by concept:** control (`settings`, `runs`, **`iterations`**) · blocking substrate (`listing_fp`, `image_band`, `phash_pop`, `exploded_blocks`) · pair grain (`pairs`, `judgements`, `must_not_link`) · cluster grain (`clusters`, `cluster_members`, `cluster_conflicts`) · write path and learning (`merges`, `verdicts`, `labels`, `models`, `eval_samples`) · realtime (`resolve_queue`, `scan_cursor`, `judge_queue`).

**THE GENERATION IS PART OF THE KEY (E58).** `clusters` `(generation, cluster_key)` · `cluster_members` `(generation, cluster_key, listing_id)` · `pairs` `(generation, listing_lo, listing_hi)`. Every writer writes and deletes strictly inside its own generation; every read joins on it. The one statement pair that crosses a generation is the score lane's optional prune (`keep_generations=<n>`, off by default), which drops whole old passes and never the one it just wrote. **Storage cost, measured live 2026-09-20 on the 4,569-listing trial cohort: ≈40 MB a generation** — `pairs` 91 MB over 39,832 rows across four passes (~2.3 kB a pair, features JSONB), so g5's 15,856 stored pairs are ~36 MB, plus ~1.7 MB of clusters and members (836 + 4,078 rows) and ~4 MB of `cluster_conflicts` (10,850 rows).

**`autodedup.verdicts` — what a ruling is about.** `generation` (nullable: NULL is legacy, not a claim) and `member_ids bigint[]` — the sorted listing ids the SERVER resolved for that `(generation, cluster_key)` at write time. A cluster ruling applies to a group only when `member_ids` equals its current member set; otherwise the group reads as unreviewed with a stale-verdict hint. Pair-grain rows carry neither, by CHECK.

**`autodedup.iterations` — the progress ledger the SPA renders** (§12). One row per iteration of any wave, written by the lane itself:

| column | type | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `id` | `bigserial` primary key | |
| `wave` | `text` | `W0`, `W0b`, `W1`, … |
| `title` | `text` | what this iteration tried, in a few words |
| `status` | `text not null check (status in ('running','done','failed','skipped'))` | |
| `approach` | `text` | the sentence the progress page shows |
| `tools` | `text[]` | the per-wave "Tools used" chips |
| `sample_stats` | `jsonb` | cohort, blocks, n pairs, n labels |
| `metrics` | `jsonb` | the numbers this iteration moved, each with its gate |
| `cost_usd` | `numeric(10,4) not null default 0` | **read from `llm_calls`** (E32), never forecast |
| `artifacts` | `jsonb` | name → url |
| `run_id` | `bigint` | the GitHub Actions run |
| `notes` | `text` | |
| `started_at` / `finished_at` / `created_at` | `timestamptz` | `created_at default now()` |

RLS on, `anon`/`authenticated` revoked, and registered — with every other new relation — in `tests/test_migration_rls_grants.py::_ADMIN_ONLY_RELATIONS`.

**Python package `autodedup/`:** `guards.py` (E2–E5, E34 — imports only `toolkit.room_taxonomy`) · `normalize.py` · `fingerprint.py` + `fingerprint_sql.py` · `blocking.py` + `blocking_sql.py` · `fetch.py` + `fetch_sql.py` · `features.py` · `model.py` · `decide.py` · `cluster.py` · `judge.py` + `judge_batch.py` · `apply.py` · `store.py` + `store_sql.py` · `iterations.py` (the ledger writer) · `evaluate.py` · `settings.py` · `census.py` **[built]** · `lane.py` **[built]**.

**CI and packaging chores:** `"autodedup"` in `tests/sql_corpus.py` `RUNTIME_DIRS` **[done]** so every `*_SQL` constant joins the PREPARE gate for free; `autodedup*` in `pyproject.toml` `packages.find` **[done]**; the new relations registered in `tests/test_migration_rls_grants.py::_ADMIN_ONLY_RELATIONS`; `frontend/public/workflow-docs.json` regenerated with `python scripts/generate_workflow_docs.py` whenever `autodedup.yml`'s leading comment changes (a **hard** CI gate); a `COPY autodedup/` line in the `Dockerfile` only if and when the worker lane ships.

**In-function concatenated SQL is invisible to the PREPARE gate.** Every executed statement lives in a module-level `*_SQL` constant with `%(name)s` params. Two grain variants mean two constants, never a string-built column name — the pattern `census.py` already follows.

## 14. Q13 — Wave plan, gates, cost

`ad_*` settings ship dark and **shadow mode is the only mode in W0–W7** (E39/D4). The program's terminal deliverable is **W6: a measured, fully evaluated engine that has written nothing**. Any canary or rollout after that is a separate operator decision (Appendix A).

| W | deliverable | gate | LLM cost |
|---|---|---|---|
| **W0** Census *(closed)* | `autodedup.yml` + `autodedup/lane.py` + `autodedup/census.py` on `main` — **PR #1482, merged as `f236d2f7`**; first census run **35088558149** | census artifact returned and readable — **met** (`autodedup-census-35088558149`, 17,455 B). The *delta* probes are W0b's gate, not W0's | **$0** |
| **W0b** Program doc + foundation *(this PR)* | `docs/design/autodedup/PROGRAM.md` + `README.md`; migrations **527** (`called_for`) and **528** (schema `autodedup`, incl. `iterations`) written **and applied**; the census **delta** probes: per-portal signal coverage (B1), remax location re-measure (B2), re-post base-rate (B3), developer-project density (B4), ingest-rate `L` (B5), RLS/grant state (B7), plus the block-layer adds `with_desc200` / `with_source_url` / `no_signal_at_all`. **Probe B6 (merge-ledger split) is dropped by D7** — the engine reads nothing from `property_merge_events`, not even counts | both migrations applied before merge; delta census runs green; **the operator picks the four blocks** off the table; `L`, the re-post base rate and `ad_max_cluster_size` are measured, not guessed | **$0** |
| **W1** Export + local harness + **progress page** | `mode=export` dumps the cohort as one gzipped JSONL artifact: fingerprints, **scrubbed** descriptions, phash lists, CLIP vectors as float16, tags, last 12 snapshots, **hashed** broker keys, location fields, and precomputed cross-evidence for every candidate pair — no PII in the artifact. Plus `GET /autodedup/iterations`, the `/autodedup/progress` SPA page and the `AUTODEDUP` nav entry, and `autodedup/iterations.py` so every lane from here on writes its own row | features for 10k pairs compute locally in < 60 s (zero-cost iteration proven); the progress page renders W0/W0b/W1 rows with real costs from `llm_calls` | **$0** |
| **W2** Blocking + core features | K1–K6, explosion control, catalog subtraction, the **core ~25 features** (grows only by error analysis), hand-initialised model; `estimate`/`verify` modes with a pure-Python oracle diffed against the SQL | oracle and SQL agree on every block, zero disagreements; ≤ 25 candidates/listing p50, ≤ 60 p99; certificates retrieve 100% of rule-certain positives; **`b` measured on the real cohort** | **$0** |
| **W3** Ground truth | vision-in-batch smoke (20); Qwen smoke (20); 4,000 cheap bootstrap labels; 1,200 gold three-vote labels; **measured output-token anchor** | gold unanimity ≥ 0.92; cheap-vs-gold ≥ 0.93; `llm_calls` shows the spend and the measured $/pair; every lane asserted "done" not "drew" | **$32** |
| **W4** Model + thresholds | Stdlib LR fit, reliability diagram, **per-stratum** `T_hi`/`T_lo` (D10), diversity-gate tuning, error analysis, up to 3 prompt iterations, feature-set growth driven by the named error classes | ECE ≤ 0.05; auto-band precision Wilson-LB ≥ 0.99 **on ≥ 380 pairs**, point ≥ 0.995, no stratum < 0.97; `b ≤ ad_band_budget`; weak strata explicitly demoted to propose-only | **$10** |
| **W5** Validation UI | `/autodedup`, `/autodedup/groups`, `/autodedup/residual`, `PairCard.tsx`, verdict capture, the "what would move" panel (E44/D5) | operator can scroll both views and verdicts persist; **the 200-pair gold-vs-operator session runs (D6) and reaches ≥ 0.95, or the program stops** | **$0** |
| **W6** Full trial pass — **the program's terminal gate** | Score all four blocks; judge the band; the recall probe; the exhaustive O(n²) bound on the sparse block; the sealed holdout; the decision package, including the reversibility argument **stated as argued, not measured** (A5) | every §9 criterion: precision per D3, cluster recall ≤ 1.15, missed-duplicate ≤ 2%, projected monthly ≤ $150. Outcome = a decision package for the operator, **not a rollout** | **$60** |
| **W7** Realtime lane — **design-only unless approved** | By default: a written lane design (this §11) plus the queue/enqueue/latch/plausibility-monitor specification, reviewed and left unbuilt. **Only on explicit operator approval** does it become code: worker lane, `resolve_queue`, enqueue hooks, latches, budget rails, `verify_pipeline` arm, E43 monitor, run 7 days with **writes off** | design-only: the operator has read it. If built: 7 days shadow, queue p95 < 60 s, zero lane crashes, measured spend within ±30% of forecast | **$0** design-only / **$10** if built |

**Committed development spend ≈ $102 design-only, ≈ $112 if W7 is built — of a $200 program cap (D2).** GPU $0 planned, **$20 reserved**, contingency **$68–78** — roughly two extra full W6 passes.

### Tools used, per wave (the progress page mirrors these as chips)

- **W0 — Census.** `autodedup/census.py` + `autodedup/lane.py` dispatched through `.github/workflows/autodedup.yml` (`mode=census`), read-only SQL over `listings` ⋈ `listing_location` ⋈ `location_granularity_rank`, `out/census.json` uploaded as a 30-day Actions artifact. No LLM, no GPU, no writes.
- **W0b — Doc + foundation.** `apply_migration.yml` (the no-MCP apply path) for 527/528; `psql "$SUPABASE_DB_URL"` for verification SELECTs; `tests/sql_corpus.py`'s PREPARE gate and `tests/test_migration_rls_grants.py` as the CI rails; the census lane again for the delta probes.
- **W1 — Export + harness + progress page.** `mode=export` on the same Actions lane (gzipped JSONL artifact, scrubbed and hashed); a local Python 3.12 stdlib harness reading that artifact; FastAPI (`api/routes/autodedup.py`) + React/Vite/Tailwind (`/autodedup/progress`, `Shell.tsx` nav); `autodedup/iterations.py` writing `autodedup.iterations`.
- **W2 — Blocking + core features.** Pure stdlib Python locally (no DB, no network): `blocking.py`, `features.py`, `guards.py`, plus the pure-Python oracle diffed against the SQL constants. `hashlib`/`unicodedata` for SimHash and deaccenting; `math` only. Zero cost, unlimited iterations.
- **W3 — Ground truth.** `LLMClient.call` via the OpenAI Batch API — `gpt-5.6-luna` (T1 text), `gpt-5-mini` (T2 vision, 768 px q80), a three-vote gold panel; every call lands an `llm_calls` row under `autodedup_judge_text` / `autodedup_judge_vision` / `autodedup_judge_gold`; `--max-usd` pre-flight asserting "done"; R2 presigned reads for the vision images.
- **W4 — Model + thresholds.** Stdlib logistic regression (`autodedup/model.py`), a 10-bin reliability diagram, Wilson and Clopper–Pearson intervals computed in `statistics`/`math`, Horvitz–Thompson weighting, bootstrap resampling — all local, all free. Prompt iterations go back through the W3 tools.
- **W5 — Validation UI.** FastAPI admin routes behind `require_admin`, React + TanStack Query on the SPA, `PairCard.tsx`, the shared `imageSrc` → `/images/{storage_path}` → 302 R2 media path, `lib/routes.ts` `def()` for every route literal.
- **W6 — Full trial pass.** Everything above at once: the Actions lane for the scored pass and the band judging, the local harness for evaluation (Wilson/Clopper–Pearson, Horvitz–Thompson, bootstrap), and `psql` for read-only verification SELECTs. `scripts/autodedup_revert.py` is *written and unit-tested against fixtures* here; it is not run against the database in this program (A5).
- **W7 — Realtime lane.** Design-only by default: this document plus a reviewed lane spec. If approved: `scraper/realtime_worker.py` lane tuple, `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` queue drain, lease-row CAS (`location_data/resolver/lease.py` pattern), `worker_heartbeats` + the Health page, `verify_pipeline` arm.

### Risks and mitigations

1. **Circular ground truth** — the judge is both arbiter and label source. → Three separately reported agreement numbers, model-family/vote/image-count separation between gold and cheap, the 200 operator-anchored pairs, and **gold-vs-operator < 0.95 stops the program** (D6). Residual risk: a blind spot shared by both OpenAI models and Qwen would survive all three checks.
2. **Developer projects** — the precision killer. → Five rails (§2), the dense Praha block, and the **assembled negative-control cohort** measured as its own stratum (D1). If precision there cannot clear 0.97, `project_suspected` blocks ship propose-only (D10).
3. **The band-width assumption drives every cost number.** → It is a *measured* quantity at the end of W2 and a settings-capped dial thereafter (E25), with the sacrificed recall reported. This is a mechanism, not a forecast.
4. **bazos may be undecidable at 99%** — zero area on 138,997 rows, no broker row, obec-grain pins, 57% pin/town disagreement. → Its own stratum and its own threshold (E23/D10); propose-only if it cannot clear. The area parser gap belongs to the scraper track.
5. **Vision-in-batch and Qwen are both unexercised in this repo.** → Smoke first (E31); fallbacks are sync vision at 2× and `gpt-5-mini` self-consistency n=3 (a weaker independence check, reported as such). The fallback is taken **per pair on any failure of the third arm, and the arm itself is switched off for the rest of the run** when the failure is permanent — an exhausted DashScope quota or a dead key, told apart from a burst 429 by `judge_lane.classify_provider_error` (quota / rate_limit / fatal / transient). A rate limit is retried at 2/4/8s first. Only an auth or config failure on the **primary** model, which has no second family behind it, stops a pass; `summary.json` publishes `qwen_arm_disabled`, its reason and the pair index it happened at, plus `weaker_votes`. Judge run 35146813906 is why: a quota 429 on the third arm stopped a gold pass after 598 of 2,240 calls while `gpt-5-mini` was answering every one of them.
6. **Reasoning tokens.** → Budgeted at 1,200–1,500 output tokens and replaced by the measured `llm_calls` average before any threshold is fixed (E32).
7. **The generation stamp is a non-atomic seam.** → Intent recorded before the call plus a reconciler sweep (E40). Not exercised in this program (D4), so the seam is a design obligation carried into Appendix A, not a live risk here.
8. **`MergeError` on a queue race.** → Explicit policy (E41): terminal vs retry vs poison, never silent.
9. **Guards at the wrong grain.** → Evaluated at property grain too (E4), or the engine manufactures merges the last rail would refuse and counts them as errors.
10. **Reversibility is asserted, not measured** — it is the claim that would license the threshold, and under D4 this program cannot measure it without doing the very writes it forbids. → It stays an *open* risk: W6's decision package states the claim as argued (from `unmerge_group`'s deterministic replay and the single-child group shape, E38) and names **A5 — the revert rehearsal** as the operator decision that must close it *before* any write path is switched on. Residual risk: `recompute_one` / `sync_browse_list` run inline per merge, so a bulk revert is a bulk read-model rebuild of unmeasured duration.
11. **Silent changes to operator state** (CLAUDE.md rules 16/18/22). → E44/D5: every proposal shows exactly what would move, in the UI, before anything is ever applied.
12. **Storage growth, and the temptation to index a hot shared table.** → Measured sizing, the `ad_anchor_images` knob, no multi-GB pair table, and **D8: no DDL on `public.images` — no shared-table index at all in this program**. If the image lane proves slow on the existing indexes, that is a finding for the rollout conversation, not a migration.
13. **Cron throttle** (80–256 minute gaps on a `*/30` lane). → Nothing latency-sensitive runs on cron; a future band drain would run sync inside the worker.
14. **Collision with the parallel operator-guided program.** → Separate schema, package, workflow, concurrency group, settings prefix, `called_for` values; **`llm_calls` is the only shared table this program writes** (three new `called_for` values), and under D7 this engine reads nothing the other program produces. Two engines eventually writing through one chokepoint is a **rollout** problem, and it is explicitly deferred to Appendix A rather than mitigated by importing the operator's merge history — D7 forbids that import.
15. **Every corpus number here is doc-sourced, dated 2026-09-08 to 2026-09-15, and unverified live.** W0's census is the first real measurement and may move the region choice, `L`, `c`, `b` and the cluster cap materially.

Presentation delta: **86 % of pairs change**, **37.1 % of shown frames are new**, **89.2 % get ≥1 room pair** and 57.8 % get four (mean 3.02 of 4). Agreement: j1 4-way **0.864** / κ 0.775 / binary 0.969; j2 **0.850** / κ 0.749 / 0.961. Abstention 5.4 % → **2.9 %**; `developer_project_suspected` 55.1 % → **62.2 %**. **BY ZONE — and this is the reading a rollout needs, because a judge verdict only becomes a merge in the band**: band (152 pairs, 69 gold duplicates) j1 **6** false `same_property`, j2 **6**; j2's extra false positives land in the merge (2) and reject (2) zones, where the judge is a label, not a gate. Qualitatively the arms trade error classes: **j2 catches 4 of j1's 5 developer-marketing false merges**, quoting the E53 instruction back ('matched photographs are exterior/site/plan only'); its own 9 extra false calls weigh unit-level photo detail above a stated attribute conflict, and **3 of those rest on gold labels M22/M23/M24 and CLAUDE.md rule 15 already call unreliable** (a thousands-truncated plot, a cross-portal amenity `false`, the sanctioned dům↔komerční pair). Only 2 are genuine hazard misses. **The comparison is structurally biased toward j1**: every gold label is a majority of two `gpt-5-mini` votes taken under the j1 presentation, and the model's own self-agreement across a presentation change is 91.1 % (M34) — a 1.7-point gap is inside that noise. $0.004426/pair, 19.97 s mean | run `35185988862`; `judge_oss/cmp_final` | W5c |
